# Narottama-vilasa

*"The Pastimes of Narottama"*

*A poem by Narahari Chakravarti*

*Translated from the Bengali into English*

---

## Translator's Note

The *Narottama-vilasa* is a Bengali poem by Narahari Chakravarti (Ghanashyama Dasa), the author of the
*Bhakti-ratnakara* (18th century), about Srila Narottama Dasa Thakura Mahashaya. Its twelve vilasas tell of his
guru Lokanatha Gosvami; of Narottama's birth at Kheturi, his childhood and departure for Vrindavana;
of his initiation and study under Jiva Gosvami; of the books of the Gosvamis stolen at Vishnupura; of
Narottama's pilgrimages through Gauda and to Nilachala; of the installation of the six Deities at Kheturi and
the great festival attended by Jahnava-devi, Srinivasa Acharya and the associates of Mahaprabhu; of the
deliverance of the fallen — reviling brahmanas, kings and robbers; of Virachandra's visit, the departure of
Srinivasa and Ramachandra Kaviraja and, finally, of the hidden withdrawal of Narottama himself in the waters of
the Ganga at Gambhila. The twelfth vilasa lists the "branches" — Narottama's disciples and their disciples.

**Source.** The translation is based on a public-domain edition: ed. Rakhaldas Kaviratna, 2nd ed. (Calcutta,
1924). For ease of reading a Unicode typesetting of the same text (ed. Raghunatha Dasa Shastri, 2021) was used;
every portion was checked against the 1924 edition, and where the readings differ the 1924 reading is preferred
(significant differences are noted). The few passages found only in the 2021 edition (a Sanskrit passage from
the *Sangita-madhava* and a verse of Vishvanatha Chakravarti in vilasa 12; conversely, the author's signature in
vilasa 7 is found only in the 1924 edition) are translated in their place with a note. **Only the text of the
poem** has been translated: the editors' explanations and notes have been neither translated nor paraphrased.

**Principles.** The payara couplets are translated line by line, without rhyme, and numbered consecutively
within each vilasa (the numbering is the translator's own: the 1924 edition has almost none, and that of the
2021 edition contains many errors); each tripadi, song (pada) and Sanskrit shloka is given a single number. The
Sanskrit shlokas are translated; where the author names the source, it is given. The vilasa titles are
descriptive and supplied by the translator (except those the author gives in his colophons). Names and terms
follow `GLOSSARY.md`.

**Notes** at the end of each vilasa explain realia and persons, record divergences from the
*Bhakti-ratnakara*, *Prema-vilasa*, *Chaitanya-charitamrita* and *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, and note the readings of
the editions. The interpretation follows Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati; where the author says otherwise, the
text is rendered as it stands and the difference is noted. Thus the *Prema-vilasa* tells some episodes
differently: the date of Narottama's birth, the debate with the pandita Rupanarayana, and the vision at the
first kirtana in Kheturi (in Narahari, Gauranga Himself appears in the kirtana with His associates).

In all, 3,319 couplets and songs have been translated.

---

## Contents

- Vilasa 1. Lokanatha Gosvami
- Vilasa 2. The Birth and Childhood of Narottama; His Departure for Vrindavana
- Vilasa 3. The Books in Vishnupura; Narottama's Journey through Gauda
- Vilasa 4. Narottama's Journey to Nilachala
- Vilasa 5. Narottama's Journey through Gauda after Nilachala
- Vilasa 6. Gauranga in Kheturi and the Gathering of the Vaishnavas for the Festival
- Vilasa 7. The Installation of the Six Deities and the Great Festival in Kheturi
- Vilasa 8. The Farewell of the Vaishnavas from Kheturi
- Vilasa 9. Jahnava in Vrindavana; Her Return through Kheturi; the Brahmana Healed of Leprosy
- Vilasa 10. The Deliverance of the Fallen
- Vilasa 11. Virachandra in Kheturi; the Departure of Srinivasa, Ramachandra and Narottama
- Vilasa 12. The Branches and Sub-branches of Narottama's Tree

---

# Vilasa 1. Lokanatha Gosvami

*Opening verses and glorifications; the life of Lokanatha: Talkhari, his going to the Lord in Navadvipa, his search for Mahaprabhu, the dream-vision and the prophecy of Narottama; Lokanatha in Vrindavana with the Gosvamis; in Ramakeli the Lord calls Narottama by name*

**1.** *O you who are dear to those surrendered to the Lord of Sri, O king of dancers,*
*uniquely skilled in bestowing the treasure of your prema,*
*O friend of the life of Sri Gaura-Vishvambhara —*
*O Lokanatha, O Prabhu, be gracious to me!*[^01-1]

**2.** *I bow to Sri Lokanatha, the associate of Sri Chaitanya,*
*whose one life is Sri Radha-vinoda, who is the life of the people.*

**3.** *I bow to Sri Narottama, dear to Sri Gaura,*
*a bee at the lotus feet of Lokanatha, maddened by the rasa of Radha and Krishna.*

**4.** *I worship the branches of Sri Narottama Prabhu,*
*endowed with every good quality, removers of every anartha.*

**5.** *For the delight of the Vaishnavas and to fulfil my own cherished desire,*
*I briefly relate the book called "Narottama-vilasa."*[^01-2]

**6.** Glory, glory to Sri Gaura-Govinda, the Lord of all,
whose body, full of prema, enchants the worlds!

**7.** Glory to the son of Shachi and Jagannatha Mishra,
glory, glory to the life of Nityananda and Advaita!

**8.** Glory to the Lord of Gadadhara Pandita's life,
glory to the Lord of Srivasa, renowned in the world!

**9.** Glory to Him who is subdued by the prema of Haridasa and Vakreshvara,
glory to Him who is expert in increasing Murari's joy!

**10.** Glory to the friend of Gauridasa and Gadadhara,
glory to the Beloved of Narahari, full of majesty!

**11.** Glory to the treasury of virtues, dear to Svarupa,
glory to the life of Sanatana, Rupa and Gopala!

**12.** Glory, glory to the Lord together with the host of His devotees!
Reveal to me a little of the pastimes of Your devotees that I long for.

**13.** O listeners, hear the words of a fool such as I;
seeing my destitution, be kind to me, all of you.

**14.** I know neither good nor bad, I have no knowledge at all;
whatever I say, it is only the will of the sadhus that is strong in it.

**15.** This book, the "Narottama-vilasa," is delightful:
correct it and relish it continually.

**16.** In the order in which I made the mangalacharana in the preceding verses,
in that order I shall now speak — listen attentively.[^01-3]

**17.** Glory, glory to Lokanatha, dear to Sri Chaitanya,
the lamp of a brahmana family, renowned in every respect!

**18.** Here I shall tell a little of his life —
listen: it is known throughout the world.

**19.** In the land of Jessore there is a village named Talkhari:[^01-4]
there he appeared, incomparable in every way.

**20.** His mother was Sita, his father Padmanabha Chakravarti;
what can I say of how the two of them shone?[^01-5]

**21.** Padmanabha Chakravarti is known in the world:
Prabhu Advaita had great kindness for him.

**22.** A great Vaishnava, extraordinary in all his deeds,
full of every virtue, the foremost of the brahmanas of Rarh.

**23.** Day and night he was immeasurably intoxicated with sankirtana;
who could keep composure seeing the streams from his eyes?

**24.** By the grace of Sri Advaita, with great joy in his heart,
he would constantly come to Nadia to see Gauranga.

**25.** When he returned home, nothing pleased Padmanabha:
together with his wife he always sang of Gaurachandra's virtues.

**26.** As was Padmanabha, so was his wife Sita —
a great Vaishnavi, devoted to her husband.

**27.** Having obtained such a son as Lokanatha, the virtuous mother
raised him — how, I have no power to tell.

**28.** Having dedicated her son to the feet of Gaurachandra,
she watched her son's doings with great joy.

**29.** Sri Lokanatha had great eagerness on the path of bhakti;
beautiful in every limb, he was like compassion embodied.

**30.** From a young age he was learned in all the shastras
and most diligent in serving his father and mother.

**31.** He worshipped the feet of Krishna constantly
and by the power of bhakti drew all hearts to himself.

**32.** When after some time his father and mother passed from sight,
he told his friends what was in his mind.

**33.** Leaving worldly life and its pleasures like filth,
he set out for Nadia to see Prabhu.

**34.** In Navadvipa he gave himself to Prabhu's feet,
and Prabhu graciously kept him near Himself.

**35.** Prabhu, anxious at heart about the sannyasa He was to take,
quickly sent Lokanatha to Vraja.

**36.** Who can understand Prabhu's doings? They are utterly deep:
having sent Lokanatha away, He Himself was not at ease.

**37.** Lokanatha understood what was in Prabhu's heart:
in two or four days He would leave His home.

**38.** Prabhu is the independent Lord, all is by His will:
what Lokanatha's journey was like, I cannot describe.

**39.** Tears flowed incessantly from his eyes;
a day's journey took him four or five days.

**40.** Some way along he heard that Prabhu had taken sannyasa
and, consoling His dear devotees, had gone to Nilachala.

**41.** Remembering that the hair was no longer to be seen on Prabhu's head,
he wept aloud.

**42.** Half dead, by Prabhu's order,
after some days he entered Vrindavana.

**43.** He stayed some days, gazing at the beauty of Vrindavana,
and there he heard that Prabhu had gone to the south.

**44.** Anxious at heart, Lokanatha
set off for the south, where Sri Gaurasundara was.

**45.** Some way along he learned all the news:
Prabhu had returned from the south to Nilachala.

**46.** Having set out for Vrindavana by the road through Gauda,
He had gone back to the Kshetra at the wish of the devotees.[^01-6]

**47.** Again Lokanatha heard that Prabhu had come to Vrindavana,
and at once he set out for Vraja.

**48.** Coming to Vrindavana, he learned all the news:
in these very days Prabhu had left for Prayaga.

**49.** Grieved, Lokanatha resolved firmly:
"At dawn I shall go to Prayaga to see Prabhu."

**50.** Remembering Prabhu's virtues, he wept;
he rolled on the ground, and none could hold him.

**51.** At the end of the night, by Prabhu's will, he fell asleep
and in a dream saw Gaurachandra in Nadia:

**52.** His body anointed with sandal, like pure gold,
flowers arranged in His lovely curling hair,

**53.** a divine tilaka on His forehead, the sacred thread on His neck —
who would not be enchanted by the play of His eyes and brows?

**54.** How sweetly He smiles and smiles with His gentle mouth,
destroying the pride of the moon and showering nectar!

**55.** What arms reaching to the knees, what a broad chest!
He wore a lovely cloth tucked in three places.

**56.** Every limb was adorned with jewelled ornaments;
His age was youthful, and in it waves of rasa.

**57.** With sweet words He said to Lokanatha:
"I always abide here together with all of you.

**58.** Here in Navadvipa My pastimes are endless;
not even Brahma and the others can reach their limit."

**59.** Having said this and more, He began to embrace Lokanatha —
and he awoke, unable to bear his grief.

**60.** By Prabhu's will sleep drew him in again,
and again Prabhu appeared before Lokanatha —

**61.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, the crown of sannyasis —
and spoke to Lokanatha in the sweetest words:

**62.** "You have resolved to go to Prayaga —
why should you go? See, here I am before you.

**63.** O Lokanatha! I had a great longing at heart
to live together with you in Vrindavana.

**64.** That is why I quickly sent you to Vrindavana
and Myself took sannyasa from Bharati.

**65.** I was eager to see the forest of Vrinda,
but that did not come to pass; I went to Advaita's house.

**66.** All were in great sorrow at My sannyasa;
staying at Advaita's home, I consoled them all.

**67.** Knowing everyone's wish, I went to Nilachala;
having stayed there some days, I travelled through the south.

**68.** For My sake you too set out for the south;
I came to Vraja, and hearing of it you came to Vraja.

**69.** As fate would have it, we did not meet;
there is no reckoning the sorrow you suffered.

**70.** Hearing from people that I had gone to Prayaga,
you resolved to go there at dawn.

**71.** I am always near you —
do not go anywhere away from Vrindavana.

**72.** From Prayaga I shall go to Nilachala,
and you will hear all that befalls Me.

**73.** Sanatana, Rupa and others dear to Me
you will very soon see here.

**74.** Through them I shall reveal the wish of My heart,
and an ocean of happiness will surge up in Vrindavana.

**75.** On the waves of that happiness you will float always,
and all that is in your heart will be fulfilled.

**76.** After some time a king's son
will become your disciple; his name will be Narottama.

**77.** He will always float in the rasa of prema-bhakti
and will easily destroy the impurity of living beings.

**78.** He will reveal a lofty and most sweet singing,
on hearing which even wood and stone will melt."

**79.** Having said this, He embraced Lokanatha,
and Lokanatha, falling to the ground, bowed at His feet.

**80.** At that moment sleep broke and Prabhu vanished;
in his distress Lokanatha could hardly keep his life.

**81.** Remembering and remembering the virtues of Gaurangachand,
he sighed and wept with stifled sobs.

**82.** After a while he consoled himself and grew calm,
yet still streams of prema flowed from his eyes.

**83.** Seeing that morning had come, he performed his morning duties
and, sitting in solitude, chanted the holy name.

**84.** At the request of the brahmanas of Vraja, at the proper time
he ate fruits and the like, and lived beneath a tree.

**85.** He never stayed long in one place,
but wandered about the region of Vrindavana.

**86.** Seeing a wondrous beauty of the forest somewhere,
he would remain there some days in secret.

**87.** Suddenly he heard from someone
that Sri Subuddhi Mishra had come to Vrindavana.[^01-7]

**88.** Then Sri Rupa Gosvami came
and went back again to where Sri Gaurasundara was.

**89.** Sanatana came and went on to Nilachala.
Hearing all this, Lokanatha shed tears of prema.

**90.** Crying "Sanatana! Rupa!" he would sigh:
"When shall we live together?"

**91.** As he spoke thus, in utmost agitation,
a voice came from the sky: "They will come soon."

**92.** How Rupa and Sanatana came after some days —
all that is described at length in other books.

**93.** Sri Gopala Bhatta and others came to Vrindavana,
and Lokanatha Gosvami met with all of them.

**94.** What joy there was at their meeting —
I, a fool, cannot describe even a trace of it.

**95.** Sri Rupa Gosvami always pleased
Lokanatha Gosvami with respect in every way.

**96.** What Sanatana Gosvami's attitude to him was,
he himself made known in his book.

**97.** Thus in the *Vaishnava-toshani*:
*I bow to those dear to Vrindavana, who have taken shelter at the feet of Sri Govinda —*
*to Sri Kashishvara, Lokanatha and Sri Krishnadasa.*[^01-8]

**98.** With Sri Gopala Bhatta, Raghunatha Bhatta and others,
Lokanatha was ever overwhelmed with prema.

**99.** Together with them Lokanatha, absorbed in prema,
enjoyed pastimes in Vrindavana with gladness of heart.

**100.** I cannot describe his wondrous life;
well known is his friendship with Bhugarbha Gosvami:

**101.** their body and mind were one, in nothing different —
most wonderful was the love between the two.

**102.** I cannot expand here on their love;
Lokanatha was in every way after his heart.

**103.** What shall I say? Hearing of the Gosvami's renunciation,
even a heart hard as stone splits apart.

**104.** Ever independent of all, in full accord with the scriptures of bhakti,
he was devoted to the service of the Deity Sri Radha-vinoda.

**105.** How he obtained Sri Radha-vinoda
is made known in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.[^01-9]

**106.** As he gazed on the sweet beauty of Sri Radha-vinoda,
he suddenly beheld the sweet beauty of Gaura.

**107.** Then he remembered Prabhu's command in the dream,
and, overwhelmed with prema, could not stop his tears.

**108.** Speaking of the lovely deeds of Gaurangachand,
he would swoon and roll on the ground.

**109.** He constantly reproached himself
for not having seen the wonderful pastimes of Gauranga.

**110.** When Krishnadasa Kaviraja asked the Gosvami
for permission to write his book,

**111.** the Gosvami gladly gave him permission,
but forbade him to describe himself in it.

**112.** When he went for permission to Sri Gopala Bhatta Gosvami,
he too, with great regret, forbade him in the same way.[^01-10]

**113.** These accounts I heard from the lips of the elders;
I have described a little — fortunate are those who relish it.

**114.** Lokanatha Gosvami is supremely merciful,
a vessel of Sri Chaitanya's mercy, full of the jewel of prema.

**115.** He lived ever in Vrindavana; who can fathom his intent?
With a pleased heart he bestowed his mercy on Narottama.

**116.** Thus in these verses:[^01-11]
*He whose only wealth is the mercy of Krishna-Chaitanya, whose heart is rich with the golden ornaments of prema for Him —*
*falling to the ground we bow to him constantly; we take shelter of him, Lokanatha Prabhu.*

**117.** *He who obtained eternal residence in Vrindavana, in whom Krishna's pastimes and rasa shine forth,*
*who follows his own good conduct ceaselessly and without rest — we take shelter of him, Lokanatha Prabhu.*

**118.** *Whose power of mercy the great sage named Narottama came to know,*
*whose detachment from worldly things is vast — we take shelter of him, Lokanatha Prabhu.*


**119.** Glory to Sri Thakura Mahashaya Narottama,
the dearest disciple of Lokanatha Gosvami!

**120.** Krishnananda Datta, the elder brother of Sri Purushottama —
his son is Narottama, known everywhere.

**121.** How Narottama was born in his house
I cannot describe here at length,

**122.** yet I shall tell something of it — listen carefully:
it is the greatest joy to hear.

**123.** In Gauda there is the village of Ramakeli, a wonderful settlement;[^01-12]
there lived Rupa and Sanatana Gosvami —

**124.** ministers of the great king, expert in all the shastras,
always engaged in discussing the shastras with scholars.

**125.** Maharashtra, Karnataka, Dravida, Tailanga,
Utkala, Mithila, Gauda, Gujarat, Vanga,

**126.** Kashi, Kashmir and other lands — great scholars from there
were honoured in their assembly.

**127.** Their wondrous fame spread through the world —
I have told something of it at length in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**128.** Sanatana and Rupa were very dear to the king of Gauda;
their opulence was boundless and all their ways were wonderful.

**129.** Hearing from people that in Navadvipa
Sri Gaurasundara was performing His pastimes, they rejoiced at heart

**130.** and again and again sent Prabhu humble letters —
this is set forth in the book *Chaitanya-charitamrita*.

**131.** Having surrendered themselves at Prabhu's feet, intent,
they always longed to see Prabhu.

**132.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya, the Lord of all, subject to His devotees,
was anxious at heart for Sanatana and Rupa.

**133.** Having taken sannyasa and gone to Nilachala,
Prabhu, consoling His dear devotees, set out for Vrindavana.

**134.** Prabhu's way lay through the land of Gauda;
His dear devotees would not leave His company.

**135.** Hundreds of thousands of people ran to see Prabhu;
thus Prabhu Gaura Raya came to Ramakeli.

**136.** Here Sanatana and Rupa, at Prabhu's coming,
floated with their circle in an ocean of great happiness.

**137.** Keshava Chhatri and all the others dear to them[^01-13]
were filled with great jubilation.

**138.** The royal ministers Sanatana and Rupa, in secret,
first met Prabhu together with His dear associates.

**139.** Prabhu Nityananda showed great mercy:
it was he who brought the two of them to Sri Krishna-Chaitanya-chandra.

**140.** Meeting them, Sri Gaurasundara with a joyful heart
sprinkled them with nectar in many sweet words.

**141.** Prabhu Nityananda, Haridasa, Vakreshvara,
Mukunda and all the rest were immensely happy.

**142.** In what joy Sanatana and Rupa were immersed
by Prabhu's mercy — who can describe it?

**143.** Mahaprabhu stayed there only a few days,
and there was no end to the crowds of people.

**144.** Seeing Prabhu, people could not be still;
they swam continually in the ocean of the bliss of prema.

**145.** Who can understand Prabhu's wondrous pastimes?
What to speak of others — even yavanas floated in prema!

**146.** One day Prabhu, with His dear associates,
danced in sankirtana, intoxicated with great prema.

**147.** Gazing in the direction of the village of Sri Kheturi,
He shed wondrous streams of bliss from His eyes

**148.** and called out again and again, "Narottama!" —
out of love for His devotee He could not be still.

**149.** The ocean of mercy, Prabhu Nityananda Raya,
roared aloud with great joy in his heart.

**150.** Haridasa, Vakreshvara and the others, full of prema —
great gladness rose in their hearts.

**151.** Seeing Prabhu's wondrous state, everyone
whispered to one another:

**152.** "Prabhu says the name 'Narottama' again and again —
from this we have understood something of the reason:

**153.** there is a vessel of Prabhu's prema named Narottama,
and he will appear in some village of this land.

**154.** We do not know which fortunate man
will obtain such a son, full of love for Prabhu.

**155.** She who bears such a Narottama in her womb —
there is none in the world as fortunate as she.

**156.** Through Narottama much will be accomplished."
Something of this became evident in Prabhu's ecstatic absorption.

**157.** In the same way, in Nilachala, Prabhu, the enchanter of the worlds,
wept, repeating the name "Srinivasa."

**158.** In whose house Srinivasa would appear,
that Mahaprabhu revealed to the world.

**159.** His father Sri Chaitanya Dasa and his mother Lakshmipriya
both went to Nilachala and saw Prabhu,

**160.** and then by Prabhu's order the two returned to Gauda;
and I with great joy saw them both there.[^01-14]

**161.** The Lord's devotees said to one another:
"Through Srinivasa much will be accomplished.

**162.** Gaurahari will manifest an embodiment of prema —
shall we see such a Srinivasa to our eyes' content?"

**163.** Thus they spoke of many things — I heard it with my own ears.
Who can understand Prabhu's pastimes?

**164.** In Nilachala Prabhu made Srinivasa known,
and coming to Ramakeli He drew Narottama to Himself.

**165.** "Srinivasa and Narottama are Prabhu's servants:
will the two of them come before our eyes?"

**166.** Saying many such things with great joy at heart,
they gazed on Gaurangasundara amid the assembly of devotees.

**167.** Thus Prabhu, overwhelmed in ecstatic absorption,
danced and wept, calling the names of devotees to come.

**168.** O brothers, how wonderful are the deeds of Chaitanya!
He made the village of Ramakeli supremely holy.

**169.** Bound by the love of Sanatana and Rupa,
He saw Kanai-natashala and went back to Nilachala.[^01-15]

**170.** These accounts spread everywhere,
and all longed for Narottama to appear.

**171.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the first vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^01-1]: The verse admits a double reading: the name "Lokanatha" means "lord of the worlds," and the whole address can be taken as referring to Gaura Himself (the Consort of Sri, king of dancers in sankirtana, bestower of His own prema). In its direct sense it addresses Lokanatha Gosvami, Narottama's guru.
[^01-2]: The "branches" (shakha) are Narottama's disciples and followers; Vilasa 12 is devoted to them.
[^01-3]: The author follows the order of the opening verses: first Lokanatha (Vilasa 1), then Narottama, then his "branches."
[^01-4]: The text has Talgari (তালগড়ি); usually Talkhari, north of Jessore (now Bangladesh).
[^01-5]: "Shone" follows the 1924 edition (কান্তি); the 2021 edition has "fame" (কীর্তি).
[^01-6]: Following the 1924 edition ("গৌড়পথে," "by the road through Gauda"); the 2021 edition has "Lokanatha" here. This refers to Mahaprabhu's first attempt to go to Vrindavana through Gauda, when He turned back to Puri from Ramakeli (Kanai-natashala) (CC Madhya 1; below, vv. 133–169).
[^01-7]: Subuddhi Raya (Mishra), a former ruler of Gauda who became a Vaishnava and settled in Mathura (CC Madhya 25).
[^01-8]: A verse from the opening of the *Vaishnava-toshani*, Sanatana Gosvami's commentary on the tenth canto of the *Bhagavata*.
[^01-9]: BR 2: Radha-vinoda Himself came to Lokanatha (according to tradition, from the forest near the village of Umrao) and gave Himself to him.
[^01-10]: Cf. the CC: Krishnadasa Kaviraja writes almost nothing about Lokanatha and Gopala Bhatta — at their own request.
[^01-11]: The 1924 edition names no source; the 2021 edition ascribes these verses to Vishvanatha Chakravarti's *Stavamrita-lahari* (his octet on Lokanatha).
[^01-12]: Ramakeli is a village near Gauda (close to present-day Malda), where Rupa and Sanatana, ministers of Sultan Husain Shah, lived (CC Madhya 1).
[^01-13]: Keshava Chhatri, a dignitary at the court of Gauda (CC Madhya 1).
[^01-14]: "I saw" — so in both editions (মুই … দেখিলু); since the author lived in the eighteenth century, these are presumably the words of an eyewitness whose account he passes on (cf. v. 163: "I heard it with my own ears").
[^01-15]: Kanai-natashala, a place near Rajmahal, from where the Lord turned back to Puri (CC Madhya 1).

---

# Vilasa 2. The Birth and Childhood of Narottama; His Departure for Vrindavana

*Narottama's birth in Kheturi; his first feeding and naming; studies and detachment; the brahmana Krishnadasa's accounts of the Lord and of Srinivasa; in a dream the Lord bids Narottama go to Vrindavana; his flight from home*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of mercy!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** Here, after some time, by Prabhu's will,
Narottama was born to manifest bhakti.

**4.** On the full-moon day of the month of Magha, at the sixth danda,[^02-1]
every auspicious sign came together at the moment of his appearance.

**5.** The mother's beauty increased most wonderfully;
seeing her son, she shed tears of joy.

**6.** The lying-in chamber shone divinely,
and all who were there could not keep still for joy.

**7.** In the village of Sri Kheturi the highest good came about:
evil-mindedness vanished, people were overwhelmed with joy.

**8.** No sound but "Hari! Hari!" was on their lips,
their bodies thrilled, tears streamed from their eyes.

**9.** Bhakti-devi entered the hearts of all,
and all came running, jostling, to Krishnananda's house.

**10.** Everyone brought various gifts,
and the most discerning Datta received all with honour.

**11.** Looking at his son's face, he could not turn his eyes away —
what wondrous happiness was in Krishnananda's heart!

**12.** Sri Krishnananda's father, a most generous man,
gave away much wealth for his grandson's welfare.

**13.** How he satisfied the singers, musicians,
bards, panegyrists and heralds — who could describe?

**14.** What marvels occurred at the moment of his appearance
I cannot describe here for fear of length.

**15.** Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates
danced — Narayani saw it before her eyes.

**16.** No woman is as fortunate as Narayani,
from whose womb Thakura Narottama was born.

**17.** Day by day Narottama grew like the moon;
seeing her son's face, the mother was always beside herself.

**18.** Fortunate Krishnananda, having gained a jewel of a son,
fed the brahmanas with care every day.

**19.** Looking at his son's face soothed his eyes and heart;
on an auspicious day he performed the rite of first feeding.

**20.** The festivity there was at the first feeding —
is it possible to tell it with one mouth?

**21.** A most fortunate astrologer was there;
on seeing the child his knowledge became clear.

**22.** At the raja's bidding he examined all the auspicious marks
and said, "The name worthy of him is Narottama."

**23.** Hearing this, the brahmanas said, "So it is, so it is:
he is surely the best among men."[^02-2]

**24.** What the other men and women said at the naming
I cannot relate in detail.

**25.** What happened at the first feeding
I shall tell, and people will be amazed.

**26.** They carefully put rice to the child's mouth,
but he would not eat, turning his face away.

**27.** They tried in many ways, but he would not take it,
and all became greatly worried.

**28.** The astrologer said: "Do not worry about this:
he will never eat anything but food offered to Vishnu."

**29.** At once they brought rice that was Vishnu's prasada,
put it to the son's mouth, and he ate it with delight.

**30.** From that day the raja told everyone:
"Give him nothing but Krishna's prasada."

**31.** From that very day Krishnananda Datta
himself held rice-prasada of Vishnu to be supreme.

**32.** From of old they had the service of a Deity of Sri Krishna;
now his eagerness for that service grew greatly.

**33.** Thus the first feeding took place;
whoever hears of it has his desires fulfilled.

**34.** Some days later the rite of first tonsure was performed,
and they had him taught grammar and other subjects.

**35.** Whoever taught Narottama any branch of learning
had his own doubts dispelled by Narottama's grace.

**36.** Seeing Sri Narottama's conduct, the learned
spoke quietly to one another of his virtues.

**37.** Some said: "He has descended as a portion of a god —
otherwise could a human being have such power?

**38.** At such a tender age he is well trained in every task
and pleases everyone in every way."

**39.** Some said: "Looking at him for just a moment
we forget all our sorrows, and our eyes are soothed."

**40.** Others said: "The prince is so delicate!
We have never seen another so beautiful in every limb."

**41.** Saying many such things, they praised Krishnananda,
and Krishnananda was immersed in the joy of raising his son.

**42.** Seeing that his son was fit in every way,
he always pondered with great joy at heart:

**43.** "I will have my son married and give him the kingdom,
and, like my father, I shall be free of cares."

**44.** Having resolved this, he told the wise kayasthas
to search for a bride for the marriage.

**45.** Meanwhile Narottama, absorbed in prema, in secret
worshipped Krishna, tears flowing from his eyes.

**46.** A deep mood of detachment was ever in his heart;
he could not bear talk of royal pleasures and the like.

**47.** Seeing his son's detachment at every moment,
Krishnananda Raya was greatly worried.

**48.** Nothing but Narottama pleased him;
so too the mother Narayani — her life was in her son.

**49.** They kept guards by their son at all times,
and still fear dwelt constantly in their hearts.

**50.** Narottama, like a prisoner, thought within himself:
"I see no way — how am I to leave home?"

**51.** Thinking thus, he did not reveal his mind;
"What will happen? Gauranga!" he would cry and sigh.

**52.** Crying "Nitai! Advaita!" he would run in all directions,
rolling on the ground, his body grey with dust.

**53.** Raising his arms he called again and again:
"Prabhu, with Your associates deliver me!"

**54.** Thus every day, finding a very secluded place,
he would weep aloud in great distress.

**55.** The deeds of Gaurachandra had spread throughout the world;
he could not get to hear of them, yet he listened attentively.[^02-3]

**56.** In the village of Sri Kheturi there was an old brahmana
named Krishnadasa, devoted to Krishna.

**57.** He had fully conquered his senses; everyone stood in awe of him,
and no one dared disobey him.

**58.** He too could not do without Narottama;
having finished his service to Krishna, he would secretly go to see him.

**59.** Narottama, with great respect,
falling to the ground in obeisance, would seat him.

**60.** He would ask about Prabhu and His devotees,
and the brahmana told of each one separately.

**61.** The nectar of Chaitanya's early, middle and final pastimes
he narrated to him in order, attentively, in part.[^02-4]

**62.** The pastimes of Nityananda and Advaitachandra
he told in prema — hearing them, wood and stone melt.

**63.** Pandita Sri Gadadhara, Pandita Srivasa,
Vakreshvara, Svarupa, Murari, Haridasa,

**64.** Narahari Dasa, Gauridasa, Gadadhara,
Vasu Ghosha, Mukunda, Sanjaya, Damodara,

**65.** Kashishvara, Sri Paramananda Bhattacharya,
Krishnadasa Brahmachari, the excellent Lokanatha,

**66.** Sanatana, Rupa, Sri Gopala, Raghunatha,
Raghunatha Bhatta, Jiva, renowned in the world,

**67.** Subuddhi Mishra, Raghava, Krishna Pandita and others —
the accounts of all of them he told as was fitting.

**68.** In the course of it he spoke of Srinivasa Acharya —
how he was born and where.

**69.** As he spoke, tears streamed from both his eyes;
taking Narottama by the hand, the brahmana said:

**70.** "O Narottama, his life is wonderful:
in a short time he became learned in all the shastras.

**71.** An embodiment of prema-bhakti, in great eagerness
he set out for Nilachala to see Sri Chaitanya.

**72.** Some way along he heard of Sri Prabhu's disappearance;
he fell senseless, and only by His will did he remain alive."

**73.** Thus in the *Guna-lesha-suchaka* of Sri Karnapura Kaviraja:[^02-5]
*He appeared in the house of a foremost brahmana of the Ghanteshvara line of the Rarhi brahmanas;*
*with a pure intellect expert in many shastras he conquered his opponents while still a boy;*
*hearing that the feet of Shachi's son were manifest at Niladri, he gave up everything —*
*may he, Sri Srinivasa Prabhu, my ocean of mercy, be victorious!*

**74.** *Going to Sri Purushottama, he heard on the way of Chaitanya's disappearance;*
*falling senseless, tearing his hair, striking his head and reproaching himself,*
*he placed His feet in his heart and went on to Nilachala by himself —*
*may he, Sri Srinivasa Prabhu, my ocean of mercy, be victorious!*

**75.** "Prabhu consoled him in a dream and brought him to Nilachala;
seeing Srinivasa, all were awash in tears of prema.

**76.** Gadadhara, Vakreshvara, the Pandita and all the others
showed Srinivasa mercy, each as was fitting,

**77.** and all bade him go to Vrindavana.
Having seen Jagannatha, he set out for Gauda.

**78.** Having come to Shrikhanda and setting out again for Nilachala,
on the way he heard of the disappearance of Pandita Gosani.[^02-6]

**79.** Half dead, he came back to the land of Gauda;
in a dream Sri Pandita consoled him endlessly.

**80.** In the morning, distraught, he went along the road to Gauda,
and there he met people from Gauda.

**81.** Hearing from them of the disappearance of Prabhu Nityananda and Advaita,
he fell unconscious.

**82.** Coming to his senses, he kindled a fire to burn himself,
but the two Prabhus consoled him in a dream

**83.** and bade him go to Vrindavana by way of Gauda;
when the night ended he set out for Gauda.

**84.** Going to Khanda, he bowed to Narahari and Sri Raghunandana,
received their permission and set out at once."

**85.** Thus in the same *Guna-lesha-suchaka*:
*Going on his way, he came to the town called Khanda, bowed to the beloved of Chaitanya-chandra,*
*the best of Thakuras, Sri Sarakara, and received his permission;*
*then, bowing at the feet of Raghunandana, he went on, remembering him —*
*may he, Sri Srinivasa Prabhu, my ocean of mercy, be victorious!*

**86.** "Coming to Navadvipa, he saw a marvel:
the manifest pastimes of Gauranga with His associates.

**87.** Astonished, he looked again — and saw
an ocean of sorrow surging in Navadvipa.

**88.** Eagerly Srinivasa went to Prabhu's house,
and there Vishnupriya Devi showed him great mercy.

**89.** Dasa Gadadhara, Srivasa and others
favoured Srinivasa, and all were awash in tears of prema.

**90.** Then, going to Shantipura, he saw Mother Sita —
her motherly affection cannot be described.

**91.** From there, absorbed in prema, he went to Khardaha,
and there Sri Jahnava showed him great mercy.

**92.** He went to Khanakul, to Sri Abhirama,
who together with Malini showed Srinivasa mercy.

**93.** He came again to Shrikhanda, and Sri Narahari
with great affection sent him off to Vraja.

**94.** Sri Raghunandana, overwhelmed with tenderness,
told him all about the way.

**95.** Srinivasa, having consoled his mother at Yajigrama,
a few days ago went alone to Vraja."

**96.** When Narottama heard this account of Srinivasa Acharya,
prema spontaneously arose in his heart.

**97.** Eagerly Narottama thought within himself:
"Who knows after how many days I shall gain his company?"

**98.** As he pondered thus, like the current of a river
the sweetest streams of prema flowed from his eyes.

**99.** Who can understand this way of Narottama's?
Again and again he listened to the lives of Prabhu's devotees.


**100.** He constantly reproached himself
for not having seen such an incarnation of Prabhu.

**101.** He found no peace, his heart was always overflowing;
food did not please him, he passed the nights awake.

**102.** One day, when by Prabhu's will he fell asleep,
Gaura Raya appeared to him face to face in a dream:

**103.** a form enchanting the worlds, an ocean of rasa —
lightning, kunkuma, gold: what can be compared to Him?

**104.** The knot of His curling hair rolled down His back;
even chaste wives lost their modesty on seeing Him.[^02-7]

**105.** Earrings in His ears, His cheeks gleaming,
tilaka on His forehead — who could keep his life?

**106.** Brows like bows, lotus eyes, the snares of Kama,
a face steeped in smiles that surpasses the full moon.

**107.** Arms reaching to the knees, a broad chest,
lovely necklaces of many gems on a conch-like neck.

**108.** A deep and shapely navel set in three folds,
a slender waist surpassing a lion's.

**109.** Thighs like inverted plantain stems, enchanting even sages,
lovely soles surpassing the lotus.

**110.** He wore an incomparable cloth tucked in three places.
Seeing such wondrous beauty, Narottama

**111.** could not blink; tears streamed from his eyes
like strings of pearls on a lotus.

**112.** His most tender body was filled with thrills,
their lustre surpassing the beauty of kadamba filaments.

**113.** In rapture he fell to the ground and clasped Prabhu's feet,
and Prabhu placed His foot on Narottama's head.

**114.** Spreading out both arms, He embraced him
and, full of affection, spoke sweet words:

**115.** "O Narottama, see, here I am before you!
My heart cannot bear your weeping.

**116.** Do not worry: you will soon go to Vrindavana
and become the disciple of My dear Lokanatha.

**117.** With great joy he will give you the mantra of initiation,
and through you much will be accomplished."

**118.** As He was saying much in this way, the sleep was broken;
without the sight of Prabhu, waves of sorrow surged.

**119.** Distraught, he fell to the ground and rolled about —
and by Prabhu's will sleep drew him in again.

**120.** In the dream he saw: in Navadvipa, on the bank of the Ganga,
Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita joyfully sporting.

**121.** Gadadhara, Srivasa, Svarupa, Narahari,
Haridasa, Vakreshvara, Mukunda, Murari,

**122.** Govinda, Madhava, Vasu Ghosha, Shuklambara,
Gauridasa, Shriman, Sanjaya, Damodara,

**123.** Mahesha, Shankara, Yadu, Acharya-nandana —
the host of devotees surrounded Prabhu and performed sankirtana.

**124.** The people of Navadvipa came running from all sides —
who would not long to see such beauty?

**125.** Brahma, Shiva and Shesha, immeasurably intoxicated with joy,
gazed on that form with unblinking eyes.

**126.** Purandara, with all the gods in heaven,
showered flowers incessantly to see that beauty.

**127.** Gandharvas and kinnaras, mingling with men,
sang of the virtues of Prabhu and His associates, danced and ran about.

**128.** That ocean of prema surged and flooded the worlds —
neither the fallen, nor the low, nor the insentient escaped it.

**129.** Hundreds of thousands of beasts and birds forgot themselves at the sight of that beauty;
those blind from birth gained sight and came running.

**130.** Narottama saw this wondrous scene,
and his eyes poured like the current of a river.

**131.** Prabhu Gaurachandra, beholding Narottama,
took him on His lap — and could not restrain His heart.

**132.** He drenched Narottama with the tears of His eyes,
and Narottama fell at Prabhu's feet.

**133.** Lifting him from the ground, Gaurahari, full of affection,
took his hand and entrusted him to Nityananda and Advaita.

**134.** Having had His dear devotees bestow their mercy on him,
He urgently bade him go to Vrindavana.

**135.** Again He said, "Be merciful to him, My dear ones,"
and saying this, sent him off to Vrindavana.

**136.** Narottama could not be still even for an instant
once he glanced at the beauty of Prabhu Nityananda.

**137.** Falling to the ground, he bowed at Prabhu's feet,
and Prabhu placed his holy feet on his head.

**138.** Spreading out his arms, he embraced him
and gave him the priceless treasure of Gauranga's prema.

**139.** He gave him permission to go to Vrindavana
and, seeing him distraught, consoled him greatly.

**140.** Seeing the great beauty of Prabhu Advaita,
Narottama fell at his feet, rolling on the ground.

**141.** Prabhu Sri Advaita could not restrain himself;
lifting him by the hand, he embraced him again and again.

**142.** Entrusting him to the lotus feet of Gauranga,
he commanded, "Go to Vrindavana."

**143.** Gadadhara, Srivasa and the others dear to Prabhu —
seeing their beauty, his eyes blossomed.

**144.** Falling to the ground he bowed at the feet of all,
and all, in prema, embraced Narottama.

**145.** Narottama bathed in the tears from everyone's eyes
and offered his mind and life at the feet of all.

**146.** Prabhu's associates consoled Narottama,
entrusted him to Prabhu's feet and sent him off.

**147.** Just as Narottama was setting out for Vrindavana,
the sleep broke, and his heart was filled with great sorrow.

**148.** Waking, he saw that the night was turning to dawn;
he performed his morning duties and consoled his heart.

**149.** At that time various auspicious omens appeared,
and Narottama in his joy was bathed in tears.

**150.** Meanwhile Narottama's father suddenly
went to Gauda on royal business with many people.

**151.** Narottama knew that this was the auspicious moment,
and on some pretext took leave of his mother.[^02-8]

**152.** Most prudent, he considered everything carefully
and, eluding the guards, set out in secret.

**153.** Not visiting Navadvipa and other places,
for fear of people he went to Vrindavana by forest paths.

**154.** The Mahashaya disguised himself
so that no one would recognize him if he met anyone.

**155.** Having passed a fifteen days' journey,
his anxiety eased somewhat, and he went on calmly.

**156.** How the mother and father fared here without Narottama —
who could describe it with one mouth?

**157.** Everywhere in Gauda people said to one another:
"The prince Narottama has gone to Vraja.

**158.** The one whom Prabhu drew to Himself in the village of Ramakeli —
this is that Narottama, we now know for certain.

**159.** Otherwise could another have such power,
that whoever saw him lost the fear of worldly existence?"

**160.** People said many such things and wept;
everyone spoke eagerly of Narottama.

**161.** All those dear to Nityananda, Advaita and Chaitanya
prayed constantly for Narottama's welfare.

**162.** Narottama went on unhindered along the highway;
what his displays of prema were like — who could tell?

**163.** He sang the Lord's virtues incessantly,
his eyes pouring like the current of a river.

**164.** Whoever glanced but once at Narottama
was freed from the miseries of worldly life.

**165.** In whatever village Narottama spent the night,
gladness grew in the hearts of the villagers.

**166.** Men and women stayed near Narottama
and spoke softly to one another of many things.

**167.** Some said: "Gold and champaka — let them keep their distance!
Look what a wondrous form is shining!"

**168.** Some said: "What a face, what long eyes,
what a nose, cheeks, brows, forehead, ears!"

**169.** Some said: "What arms, what a broad chest,
a navel in three folds, what a slender waist!"

**170.** Some said: "What knees, what beauty in the feet!
Of what and by whom was he fashioned — with what care!"

**171.** Some said: "This is no ordinary man —
either a god or a king's son."

**172.** Some said: "Alas, at such a tender age,
in such renunciation he wanders from land to land!"

**173.** Some said: "What more can one say —
how will his mother and father keep alive without him?"

**174.** Some said: "Perish the Creator, heartless as he is,
who drove such a boy out of his home!"

**175.** Saying many such things to one another,
they could not leave Narottama and go home.

**176.** They brought various foods and fed him a little with care,
and brought him a fine bed to sleep on.

**177.** But neither food nor sleep pleased Narottama;
in sankirtana of the name he stayed awake all night.

**178.** His body grey with dust, tears in his eyes —
seeing that state, everyone's heart wept.

**179.** At dawn he set out, taking leave of all,
and people ran after him in distress.

**180.** Whoever saw this state of his on the road —
Narottama stole the hearts of all.

**181.** Having seen all the holy places in a short time,
Narottama entered Vrindavana with gladness of heart.

**182.** First he went to Sri Mathura, to the Vishrama-ghata,
bathed in the Sri Yamuna and stayed there.

**183.** A watch of the night had passed and all was deserted,
and in prema he performed sankirtana of the holy name.

**184.** At that time a brahmana of Mathura,
a great Vaishnava, most pure in conduct,

**185.** having offered wonderful foods to Krishna,
fed Narottama, overcome with affection.

**186.** Whatever the brahmana, overwhelmed by fatherly love, asked,
Narottama, won by affection, told him.

**187.** Narottama asked for news of Vraja,
and the brahmana with an aching heart told him everything.

**188.** Hearing that Raghunatha, Kashishvara, Rupa and Sanatana
had disappeared, he wept.

**189.** Uttering the names of Sri Rupa and Sanatana,
he rolled on the ground, his body grey with dust.

**190.** "Kashishvara Pandita! Sri Bhatta Raghunatha!" —
uttering these names, he beat his head with his hands.

**191.** "Alas, alas! What has happened!" he cried again and again,
"I have not seen the holy feet of any of them!"

**192.** Saying many such things, Narottama fainted;
streams flowed from both his eyes like rivers.

**193.** Seeing that he had become as if dead, the best of brahmanas
took Narottama on his lap and wept bitterly.

**194.** After a while the very old and most steady brahmana
composed himself and calmed Narottama.

**195.** In much conversation the night had almost passed,
and by Prabhu's will sleep came over them both.

**196.** In a dream Rupa and Sanatana appeared,
with Raghunatha Bhatta and Kashishvara — all four.

**197.** Narottama, seeing their beauty, awash in tears,
fell at the feet of all, rolling on the ground.

**198.** Then they embraced Narottama with great affection
and drenched Narottama's body with tears of prema.

**199.** They spoke nectarean words of consolation,
and the fortunate brahmana heard some of them.

**200.** All of them, greatly pleased with Narottama,
showed him their mercy and vanished.

**201.** In that separation Narottama's heart lost all composure;
waking, he lamented and looked in every direction.

**202.** "Where have you gone?" he cried, tears streaming from his eyes;
seeing Narottama's state, the brahmana was astonished.

**203.** Eagerly the brahmana took Narottama on his lap
and, crying "I am purified!", was bathed in tears.

**204.** Speaking many sweet words to Narottama,
after a while the old brahmana grew calm.

**205.** Seeing that the night had turned to morning, the best of brahmanas
wanted to take Narottama to his own house.

**206.** Narottama, bowing to the brahmana,
anxiously begged leave again and again:

**207.** "Be merciful to me — I shall go
and see the feet of all the Gosvamis.

**208.** Grant that my longing be fulfilled."
The brahmana affectionately embraced him and blessed him.

**209.** He went some distance with Narottama,
but his feet would not go on — he was very tired.

**210.** Showing Narottama the way to Vrindavana,
he, full of affection, gave him a man as companion.

**211.** Narottama went on, having bowed at the brahmana's feet,
and the brahmana, pained by separation, gazed after him along the road.

**212.** As he walked, Narottama thought to himself:
"They have brought one as unworthy as I to Vrindavana!

**213.** The merciful Prabhu, Sri Gosvami Lokanatha —
will he accept one as fallen as I?

**214.** Sri Gopala Bhatta, Sri Bhugarbha Mahashaya,
Sri Jiva Gosvami and the others, abodes of prema —

**215.** shall I be able to place their lotus feet on my head?
Will they all be merciful to a helpless one like me?

**216.** Srinivasa Acharya, the embodiment of prema —
will he show love to a wretch like me?"

**217.** As soon as he said this, tears of prema flowed from his eyes;
he could not walk, his body tottered.

**218.** Meanwhile, the previous night, Srinivasa suddenly
lost his composure — his heart was pervaded by gladness.

**219.** Seeing great auspicious omens, he thought within himself:
"Surely I shall meet some friend of my life."

**220.** As prema rose of itself his eyes poured,
and for much of the night he joyfully sang the holy name.

**221.** Toward the end of the night he suddenly slept a little,
and in a dream Sri Rupa said to Srinivasa:

**222.** "O Srinivasa! When this night turns to morning,
you will meet Narottama."


**223.** Having said this, the Gosvami vanished;
Srinivasa awoke and saw that the night was over.

**224.** Going quickly to Sri Jiva Gosvami,
he bowed and told him what had happened in the night.

**225.** Sri Jiva Gosani said to Srinivasa:
"In the same way Prabhu has made known to me that he is coming.[^02-9]

**226.** The one I told you about before —
that very Narottama is coming here.

**227.** I was eager to tell you my dream,
and hearing it from your lips I have gained great happiness."

**228.** Saying this, he hurried to have darshan of Govinda,
and Srinivasa in great joy returned to his own place.

**229.** Suddenly someone came with the news:
"A king's son has come from Gauda.

**230.** He is young, his form is most lovely,
his body is always drenched with tears from his eyes.

**231.** The transformation that came over him at the sight of Sri Govinda —
who can describe it? It is astonishing.

**232.** Sri Jiva Gosvami took him on his lap
and sprinkled his body with the tears of his own eyes.

**233.** Having consoled him with the sweetest words,
he has sent me to fetch you."

**234.** Hearing this, Srinivasa could not keep still,
and with gladness of heart he went to Govinda's gate.

**235.** There he met Narottama —
as a pauper finds a priceless jewel.

**236.** What Srinivasa said as he embraced him
is so sweet that I cannot expand on it here.

**237.** What Narottama became at the sight of the Acharya —
who could describe it with one mouth?

**238.** People said to one another in amazement:
"We have seen a wonder — this natural love."

**239.** Seeing Srinivasa and Narottama together,
they discussed many things among themselves.

**240.** Whatever desire was in Narottama's heart,
Govindadeva fulfilled it.

**241.** Sri Krishna Pandita, the servant of Govinda,
carefully gave him a garland-prasada.

**242.** In passing I shall tell of Krishna Pandita:
he is an associate of Chaitanya, greatly learned.

**243.** When Kashishvara Gosvami disappeared,
Sri Krishna Pandita served the feet of Govinda.

**244.** It is known everywhere that Sri Krishna Pandita Gosvami
had great love for Narottama.

**245.** How Narottama, bowing to Sri Krishna Pandita,
showed humility — hearing of it, the heart weeps.

**246.** Sri Jiva Gosvami quickly took Narottama
to the ashrama of Lokanatha Gosvami.

**247.** He was sitting alone in deep seclusion,
his heart burning in separation from Sanatana and Rupa.

**248.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, bowing, very softly
told the Gosvami about Narottama.

**249.** Hearing and seeing Narottama, he was bathed in tears,
and Narottama fell at the Gosvami's feet.

**250.** Remembering what had gone before, he could not contain his tenderness
and placed his holy feet on Narottama's head.[^02-10]

**251.** Bathing Narottama in nectarean words,
he let him know that initiation would take place in a few days.

**252.** To Sri Jiva Gosvami he said again and again:
"See to it that he gains eligibility for the books of bhakti."

**253.** And to Srinivasa he said with great tenderness:
"Always keep him attentive on the path of bhakti."

**254.** Having said this, he took the names of Rupa and Sanatana
and, in great distress, heaved long sighs.

**255.** Seeing the Gosvami's state, Sri Jiva Gosani
became such as cannot be described.

**256.** Unable to check his ceaseless tears,
he found a moment and took his leave.

**257.** What happened at the darshan of the lotus feet of Sri Radha-vinoda —
who could describe it?

**258.** What Srinivasa said to Narottama there —
those accounts of prema are described at length elsewhere.

**259.** Having calmed Narottama, Sri Jiva Gosani
quickly took him to Bhatta Gosvami.

**260.** He was sitting alone in deep seclusion,
ever distressed without Rupa and Sanatana.

**261.** What his attitude toward Sanatana was —
what can I say? It is known everywhere.

**262.** Thus in a verse:[^02-11]
*Whose heart is flooded with love for Sanatana,*
*in whom everything is marked by friendship with Sri Rupa,*
*who grants the desires of those who worship him, whose only life is Radha-ramana —*
*to him, Gopala Bhatta, I bow.*

**263.** Seeing the Gosvami's state, Sri Jiva Gosani
became such as cannot be described.

**264.** Bowing humbly, he said:
"This is that Narottama," and hearing it, he rejoiced.

**265.** Narottama fell at the Gosvami's feet,
and he embraced him and drenched him with the tears of his eyes.

**266.** Asking after his welfare in the sweetest words,
he showed him such tenderness as I cannot describe.

**267.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, bowing to the Gosvami,
went on, taking Srinivasa and Narottama with him.

**268.** The joy that came as they gazed their fill on the beauty of Sri Radha-ramana —
I cannot tell it.

**269.** Sri Govinda, Gopinatha, Madanamohana —
the face, the chest and the holy feet of these three in turn

**270.** are seen when the darshan of all three is joined together —
so Sri Jiva Gosvami explained to Narottama.[^02-12]

**271.** Thus, speaking of many things in prema,
they entered the temple of Sri Gopinatha.

**272.** Sri Madhu Pandita informed the Gosvami:[^02-13]
"Narottama has come from Gauda today."

**273.** Narottama fell at the Gosvami's feet,
and he, greatly delighted, took him on his lap.

**274.** Drenching Narottama with the streams from his eyes,
he spoke to him with such tenderness as I cannot tell.

**275.** He gave him darshan of Radha-Gopinatha
and, bringing a garland-prasada, gave it to Narottama.

**276.** What Narottama became on seeing Gopinatha —
who could describe it?

**277.** Sri Jiva Gosvami took the two of them from there
quickly to the dwelling of Bhugarbha Gosvami.

**278.** He, full of prema, a great and profound scholar,
was one body with Lokanatha Gosvami.

**279.** He sat in seclusion meditating on Prabhu's pastimes;
Sri Jiva Gosvami went and met him there.

**280.** He introduced to him the dear Narottama,
and the Gosvami was filled with supreme joy.

**281.** Narottama fell at Sri Bhugarbha's feet,
and he showed great affection, embracing him.

**282.** Taking Narottama on his lap, he could not let him go;
all that he said I cannot relate in detail.

**283.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, bowing to Sri Bhugarbha,
went to his own place with Srinivasa and Narottama.

**284.** He gave them darshan of Sri Radha-Damodara,
and Narottama, in prema, lost his composure.

**285.** The state he fell into at the sight of Rupa Gosvami's samadhi —
who could describe it?

**286.** Narottama fell to the ground and rolled about;
streams flowed from his eyes like the current of a river.

**287.** His body became motionless, his breath stopped;
Srinivasa hurriedly lifted him onto his lap.

**288.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, after a while bringing him round,
took Narottama to his own hut.

**289.** At that time someone told the Gosvami:
"Come quickly to Govinda's temple."

**290.** As soon as he heard, he went quickly with the two
and saw the arati of Govinda's raja-bhoga.

**291.** There they partook of maha-prasada
and again came back to his dwelling, the two with him.[^02-14]

**292.** After spending a while in talks about Krishna,
they went to have darshan of Sri Madanamohana.

**293.** Going there, they saw the arati of awakening,
and he told everyone about Narottama.

**294.** Krishnadasa Brahmachari Gosvami, with affection,
showed him such mercy as I cannot describe at length.

**295.** Narottama, seeing Sri Madanamohana,
could not hold himself up; streams flowed from his eyes.

**296.** The pujari Gosani gave him a garland-prasada —
what happiness there was cannot be told.

**297.** Where the samadhi of Sanatana Gosvami is,
Sri Jiva himself showed Narottama.

**298.** What Narottama became at the sight of the samadhi —
who could describe it with one mouth?

**299.** Who can describe Sri Jiva Gosvami's tenderness!
He calmed Narottama in many ways.

**300.** With them all Sri Jiva Gosvami went to his dwelling
and entrusted Narottama to his dear Srinivasa.

**301.** In great happiness Srinivasa took Narottama,
bowed to the Gosvami and went to his own place.

**302.** The two passed the night in the relish of talks about Krishna,
and at dawn bathed in the Yamuna, absorbed in prema.

**303.** Having bowed at the feet of their own worshipful Deities,
they joyfully went to Sri Jiva Gosvami.

**304.** He quickly sent them to Radha-kunda,
and they went and saw the sweetness of the two kundas.

**305.** Srinivasa went to Dasa Gosvami
and carefully told him about Narottama.

**306.** Though the Gosvami's heart was in great distress,
still, hearing this, he felt joy arise.

**307.** "Where is Narottama?" he said and opened his eyes,
and Narottama went and bowed at his lotus feet.

**308.** Overwhelmed with tenderness, Sri Dasa Gosani
showed him such mercy as cannot be described.

**309.** With all the very wise ones who were there
Narottama met.

**310.** Sri Raghava Pandita Gosani at Govardhana
attained supreme bliss on seeing Narottama.

**311.** Srinivasa and Narottama, having gone everywhere,
came and reported it all to Sri Jiva Gosvami.

**312.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, hearing it all, was pleased
and quickly had Narottama begin his studies.

**313.** Narottama studied the books of bhakti,
stealing the hearts of all by his skill in explaining their meaning.

**314.** Who can understand Narottama's heart?
He was intent on serving Lokanatha Gosvami.

**315.** How he served cannot be put into words;
the Gosani was pleased with Narottama's service.

**316.** One day, seeing Narottama distraught,
he fulfilled his longing by giving him the mantra of initiation.

**317.** What that wondrous mantra and rite of initiation were
I cannot expand on: the shastras of bhakti are the authority for it.

**318.** In Vrindavana everyone rejoiced
to see Narottama's wonderful eligibility.

**319.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, understanding everyone's wish,
gave him the title "Sri Thakura Mahashaya."

**320.** Hearing the lovely title "Sri Thakura Mahashaya,"
all the great souls rejoiced at heart.

**321.** "As is Narottama, so is his title" —
this saying spread everywhere.

**322.** Who does not weep at the virtues of Sri Thakura Mahashaya?
He was the object of everyone's deep affection in Vraja.

**323.** What his way of service in the mind was in Vrindavana
is all made known in the book *Bhakti-ratnakara*.[^02-15]

**324.** For fear of length I cannot describe it here;
now I shall tell how he came back again to Gauda.

**325.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the second vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^02-1]: A danda is twenty-four minutes; the sixth danda is about two and a half hours after sunrise. Both editions read "মাঘ পূর্ণিমা." Tradition usually gives Magha Purnima as Narottama's appearance day; the *Prema-vilasa* differs (ev-narottama-birth).
[^02-2]: A play on words: *nara-uttama*, "the best of men."
[^02-3]: That is, in the guarded house he had no way of hearing about Gaura, yet he caught every word of it.
[^02-4]: "Early, middle and final" follows the 1924 edition; the 2021 edition has "early and middle."
[^02-5]: Karnapura Kaviraja was a disciple of Srinivasa Acharya; his praise of Srinivasa is also quoted in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*. Not to be confused with Kavi Karnapura, the son of Shivananda Sena.
[^02-6]: Pandita Gosani is Gadadhara Pandita. Srinivasa's journey is told at length in BR 3–4; cf. *Prema-vilasa* 4–6.
[^02-7]: A hyperbole customary in describing the Lord's (as Krishna's) beauty: none can withstand His form; attraction to Him is not worldly passion but the awakening of prema.
[^02-8]: "On some pretext" (প্রকারে) — the text does not specify.
[^02-9]: That is, Jiva too had been told in a dream of Narottama's coming.
[^02-10]: "What had gone before": the Lord's prophecy in the dream about his future disciple Narottama (Vilasa 1).
[^02-11]: Neither edition names the source of the verse.
[^02-12]: According to tradition, the form of Krishna manifested by Vajranabha is divided among the three Deities of Vrindavana: the face is Govinda's, the chest Gopinatha's, the feet Madanamohana's.
[^02-13]: Madhu Pandita, the servant of the Deity Gopinatha in Vrindavana.
[^02-14]: That is, Jiva returned to his dwelling together with Srinivasa and Narottama.
[^02-15]: "Service in the mind" (manasi-seva): inner service of Radha and Krishna in meditation; Narottama's service in Vrindavana is told at greater length in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

---

# Vilasa 3. The Books in Vishnupura; Narottama's Journey through Gauda

*Jiva sends Srinivasa, Narottama and Shyamananda with the books to Gauda; the theft of the books by King Vira Hambira; Narottama and Shyamananda in Kheturi; Shyamananda with Hridaya Chaitanya in Ambika and in Orissa; Narottama in Navadvipa, Shantipura, Ambika and Khardaha*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of mercy!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** Sri Jiva Gosvami together with all the mahantas
fixed an auspicious day to send the books to Gauda.

**4.** He entrusted the books to Srinivasa Acharya,
through whom Prabhu wished to distribute them.

**5.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, in a verse of his own,
described this — it is known to all.

**6.** Thus:[^03-1]
*Through one power — Sri Rupa and the others — Prabhu revealed these books,*
*and through a supreme power named Sri Srinivasa He spreads them;*
*He who out of mercy manifested these two powers on earth —*
*when will Sri Chaitanya, the ocean of compassion, come before my eyes?*

**7.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, deep as millions of oceans,
was distraught at heart with separation, yet outwardly most steady.

**8.** Having had Srinivasa take leave of everyone,
he sent him off to Gauda at an auspicious moment.

**9.** Lokanatha Gosvami, full of affection,
entrusted Narottama to Srinivasa.

**10.** Again and again he told Narottama
to perform the service of the Deity, sankirtana and good conduct.

**11.** Hearing much of this kind, Narottama rejoiced;
who could describe the happiness Srinivasa felt?

**12.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, entrusting to Srinivasa
Narottama and Shyamananda, was overwhelmed with great prema.

**13.** He said to Srinivasa: "These two are yours;
in every way their care rests with you."

**14.** To Shyamananda he gave the order: "Having gone to Gauda,
you will go on to Utkala by way of Sri Ambikapuri."

**15.** I cannot describe all this here;
you will learn it in detail from the book *Bhakti-ratnakara*.[^03-2]

**16.** Having bowed at the feet of all the mahantas,
the three set out with the books of bhakti.

**17.** Sri Jiva Gosvami and the others, distraught at heart,
quickly went with them as far as Mathura.

**18.** They sent ahead a cart filled with books,
with eleven armed men of Vraja to guard it.

**19.** Arriving in Mathura, they all spent the night there,
and the people of Mathura were overjoyed.

**20.** What happened at the parting in the morning
not even millions upon millions of mouths could describe.

**21.** Srinivasa, Narottama and Shyamananda, the three,
in some days reached the land of Gauda.

**22.** By a forest road, near Vana-Vishnupura,
they came to a village in the midst of the forest.

**23.** There they passed much of the night on guard,
but by Prabhu's will they all fell asleep.

**24.** Someone told King Vira Hambira:
"A rich man has come with a cart full of jewels."

**25.** Hearing this, the king gleefully sent robbers at once
and without difficulty had the jewel-like books brought to him.

**26.** Without taking the books out of their chests,
the king, merely on seeing the chests, became agitated.

**27.** Again and again he bowed, falling to the ground;
the king could not understand what was happening in his heart.

**28.** The king said: "What has happened in my heart?
I do not know what jewel lies within these chests."

**29.** Saying many such things, the king shed tears;
Bhakti-devi showed him many auspicious signs.

**30.** After much deliberation within himself,
the king quickly opened the book-chests in private.

**31.** Seeing the jewel-like books in the chests,
the king spoke in great remorse, weeping:

**32.** "Alas, alas, what misfortune is mine!
What great soul have I, a wretch, caused to suffer!

**33.** If I am fortunate enough to see him,
I shall give back the jewel-like books and take shelter of him."

**34.** Thus the king spoke much, sitting in private.
Meanwhile, the books having been stolen, all awoke.

**35.** The state all fell into when the books could not be seen —
how could I, a wretch, describe it with one mouth?

**36.** They threw themselves on the ground and wept aloud;
no one could be calmed in any way.

**37.** Acharya Thakura, summoning some composure,
addressed everyone with sweet words:

**38.** "Carefully we came by a difficult road without mishap,
and here suddenly we all fell asleep.

**39.** We did not even know who stole the books or when;
surely there is some hidden purpose in this."

**40.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya said quietly:
"It seems that by this pretext mercy will come to this land."

**41.** At that moment a divine voice spoke from the sky:
"Do not worry; the books will be recovered without difficulty."

**42.** Meanwhile someone said softly to the Acharya:
"This is the king's doing — go to Vana-Vishnupura."

**43.** Hearing this, Srinivasa Acharya consoled everyone
and sent a man to Vrindavana with a letter.

**44.** With great care he urged Sri Thakura Mahashaya
again and again to go quickly to Kheturi.

**45.** To Shyamananda he said, full of prema:
"Go to Kheturi, and then quickly on to Utkala.

**46.** I shall search for the books in Vana-Vishnupura
and soon send news that they have been found.

**47.** Now do not worry about anything at all."
Saying this, he sent the two of them off.

**48.** The two did not transgress the Acharya's word
and, distraught with separation, set out.

**49.** Arriving in Sri Kheturi, Sri Thakura Mahashaya
could not let Shyamananda leave him even for a moment.

**50.** Meanwhile in Vana-Vishnupura Srinivasa Acharya
bestowed his grace on Sri Vira Hambira.

**51.** Giving back the jewel-like books, the king took shelter
and with his household became greatly devoted to bhakti.

**52.** I have told this here in brief;
I have described it at length in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**53.** This news from Vana-Vishnupura
became known everywhere, and all were amazed to hear it.

**54.** Sri Acharya Thakura, with great joy at heart,
sent to Vrindavana a letter that the books had been recovered.

**55.** And to where Sri Thakura Mahashaya and Shyamananda were
he quickly sent a letter with this news.

**56.** As soon as he read the letter, Sri Thakura Mahashaya
was immersed in a joy that cannot be told.

**57.** Shyamananda, overcome with joy, for a long time
sang and danced with arms upraised.

**58.** Greatly delighted was the son of Purushottama Datta,
named Sri Santosha Datta, an abode of virtues.

**59.** He was Sri Narottama's cousin,
to whom Krishnananda Datta had given the kingdom.

**60.** So Raja Sri Santosha, in celebration,
gave many gifts to brahmanas and good people.

**61.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya was pleased with him
and quickly sent a letter to Vana-Vishnupura.

**62.** After that Shyamananda took his leave;
the sorrow of that separation — who could describe it?

**63.** The words they spoke to each other at parting —
hearing them, beasts and birds wept.

**64.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, greatly anxious,
gave him a man to accompany him to Utkala.

**65.** Shyamananda set out with an aching heart
and by way of Navadvipa came to Ambika-nagara.

**66.** At the sight of the temple of Sri Chaitanya and Nityananda
he was overcome with prema; streams flowed from his eyes.

**67.** Seeing his state, some worthy man
went and told Sri Hridaya Chaitanya:

**68.** "Your Duhkhini Krishnadasa has come;[^03-3]
I have seen a wondrous manifestation of prema-bhakti.

**69.** Seeing the holy temple from afar, he falls to the ground
and bows again and again in great humility.

**70.** How he floats in the tears from both his eyes!
He is coming from a distance; I have hurried ahead."

**71.** Hearing this, Thakura, with great joy at heart,
said again and again, "Bring him quickly.

**72.** My heart is always anxious for him;
his zeal for bhakti cannot be put into words.

**73.** Having received the mantra of initiation, he stayed here some time
and won over Nitai and Chaitanya-chand with his prema.

**74.** With great care I sent him to Vrindavana;
there he studied the shastras of bhakti.

**75.** He wrote to me of his inner longings,
and seeing his yearning I gave him a corresponding command.

**76.** He devoted himself ceaselessly to service in the kunja,
found happiness, and received the name 'Shyamananda.'[^03-4]

**77.** In Vrindavana all showed him great mercy.
He wrote in advance that he would come here.

**78.** Nitai and Chaitanya, in their mercy, will accomplish through him
a work that will pervade the world.

**79.** I tell you: he is my dear disciple;
after many days you will see him."

**80.** Even as he said this, Shyamananda arrived
and fell reverently at his feet.

**81.** Sri Hridaya Chaitanya Thakura, with affection,
placed his holy feet on Shyamananda's head.

**82.** As he was about to embrace him, Shyamananda went and stood at a distance,
awash in tears, with great joy in his heart.

**83.** Still Thakura embraced him at once
and in prema led him into the courtyard of Prabhu's temple.

**84.** He entrusted him to the feet of Nityananda and Chaitanya,
and seeing Prabhu, Shyamananda lost his composure.

**85.** The ecstatic transformations he showed I cannot describe;
Thakura brought him along to his own quarters.

**86.** He gladly gave Shyamananda the remnants of his own meal,
and Shyamananda partook of them in supreme bliss.

**87.** Then Sri Thakura asked for news,
and Shyamananda told everything from beginning to end.

**88.** The joy he felt at the words of his very dear disciple Shyamananda
cannot be told.

**89.** For some days Shyamananda stayed with his guru
and served his guru with great gladness of heart.

**90.** One day the merciful Hridaya Chaitanya
spoke to Shyamananda in the sweetest words:

**91.** "Now do not delay in going to Utkala:
much will be accomplished through you."

**92.** Saying this, he took him before Nitai and Chaitanya,
brought a garland-prasada and gave it to Shyamananda.

**93.** Infusing great power into him, he sent him off,
and Shyamananda, distraught, wept aloud.

**94.** How Shyamananda went to Utkala
cannot be described here at length.

**95.** The pashandis and evil-doers who were in Utkala —
Shyamananda delivered them all.

**96.** He made many disciples, Sri Rasikananda and others,
and by a trace of their mercy the land was blessed.

**97.** Here I have told all this briefly;
I have described it at length in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**98.** Now I shall tell how Shyamananda, with gladness of heart,
came from Sri Kheturi to the land of Sri Utkala.

**99.** The man who had come with him from Sri Kheturi
he sent back with a letter of news.

**100.** Meanwhile in Kheturi Sri Thakura Mahashaya
was greatly anxious without Shyamananda.

**101.** Receiving from him a letter with news of great good fortune,
he quickly sent it on to Vana-Vishnupura.

**102.** On reading the letter, Thakura, with great joy at heart,
sent his own letter to Shyamananda.

**103.** He sent a letter to Sri Thakura Mahashaya as well,
and reading it, the Mahashaya was greatly delighted.

**104.** The Mahashaya quickly sent a letter in return
and set out from Kheturi for Navadvipa.

**105.** In prema he went along the road like a maddened elephant,
his face and chest washed by the streams of both his eyes.

**106.** Whoever saw Sri Thakura Mahashaya but once
floated in an ocean of pure prema-bhakti.

**107.** Gazing on his beauty, people could not leave his company:
when he came to a village, everyone came running.

**108.** Saying many things, all gazed at him,
and when he left the village everyone grieved.

**109.** Thus after some days, coming near Navadvipa,
he lamented bitterly in great distress:

**110.** "O merciful Prabhu, to make me suffer
You had me born on earth at such a time!

**111.** I did not get to see these pastimes in Nadia!" —
as soon as he said this, tears flowed from his eyes.

**112.** Slowly, weeping in sorrow, he went on,
and entering Navadvipa he saw a marvel:

**113.** in every house — what joy and festivity!
An incessant clamour of "Hari! Hari!";

**114.** men and women, with great gladness of heart,
going from all sides to Prabhu's dwelling;

**115.** Gaura Raya sporting with His associates,
Nadia an ocean of the happiness of sankirtana.

**116.** Having watched this for a while, he then saw
the town of Nadia floating in an ocean of sorrow.[^03-5]

**117.** "What did I see! What did I see!" he said again and again,
unable to walk, tears flowing from his eyes.

**118.** After a while the Mahashaya, reflecting,
went on a little and asked for Prabhu's house.

**119.** Some, weeping, with bowed heads, said:
"Look, there is Prabhu's house; go this way."

**120.** Seeing Prabhu's house, Narottama wept;
streams flowed from both eyes like the current of a river.

**121.** Along that road came the brahmachari Shuklambara;
seeing Narottama, he was stirred at heart.

**122.** Narottama bowed, falling to the ground;
"Tell me who you are," he said, and embraced him.

**123.** When Narottama told who he was,
he spoke with the greatest affection, weeping:

**124.** "When Gaurachandra went to the village of Ramakeli,
He, mad with great prema, drew you to Himself.

**125.** Who can understand Prabhu's doings?
Already then He made your name known.

**126.** O my child Narottama, to see you
was the great longing in the hearts of all the mahantas.

**127.** In separation from Prabhu no one's mind is at peace;
some have passed from sight within a short time."

**128.** Saying this, he made himself known
and brought Narottama to meet the devotees of Prabhu.

**129.** Narottama bowed at the feet of all,
and all embraced Narottama with love.

**130.** Though distraught with the great pain of separation,
still, seeing Narottama, they were happy.

**131.** With much affection they asked for news,
and Narottama told everything from beginning to end.

**132.** Damodara Pandita and others dear to Prabhu
could not let Narottama go even for a moment.

**133.** For some days Narottama stayed in the town of Nadia
in the houses of Prabhu's dear associates.

**134.** How the Mahashaya lamented incessantly
cannot be described with one mouth.

**135.** The devotees he had not seen and wept for —
they all appeared to him in dreams.

**136.** How much mercy they showed Narottama
I have no power to tell in detail.

**137.** The mahantas who were manifest in Navadvipa
showed him great mercy, keeping him by them.

**138.** After some days, in great distress,
they bade him farewell, speaking sweet words:

**139.** "So that we might meet you, Prabhu
has kept life in our bodies through such pain.

**140.** But with Srinivasa we did not meet again."
Saying this, their throats choked and tears streamed from their eyes.

**141.** With great tenderness, gazing at Narottama's face,
all took leave of him — their hearts were breaking.

**142.** Placing the feet of all on his head, Narottama
went — the state he was in cannot be described.

**143.** Going to Prabhu's house with a troubled heart,
he saw the servants and maidservants — they were as if dead.

**144.** Seeing Narottama, all, distraught at heart,
said: "Much will be accomplished through you."

**145.** Saying this, their throats choked, streams flowing from their eyes,
they sent Narottama off with a gesture of the hand.

**146.** Narottama, distraught, wept aloud,
fell in Prabhu's courtyard and rolled in the dust.

**147.** After a while, checking his tears,
he set out along the road to Shantipura.

**148.** The marvel he saw on entering the village
I have no power to describe.

**149.** Going to the house of Prabhu Advaita,
he bowed at the feet of Sri Achyutananda.

**150.** He embraced Narottama, showed him great mercy,
asked for his news and introduced him to his dear ones.

**151.** He commanded: "Go to Nilachala and come back soon,
and spread the lovely kirtana, a treasury of rasa."

**152.** Saying this, he shed ceaseless streams from his eyes;
his withered body swayed in the wind.

**153.** Narottama, bowing his head at the feet of all,
took his leave and went on slowly.

**154.** Coming to the village of Harinadi and crossing the Ganga,
he reached Ambika and asked for the Pandita's house.[^03-6]

**155.** Someone said, "You have arrived — it is very close,"
and seeing Narottama, people's happiness grew greatly.

**156.** A worthy man, going ahead very quickly,
bowed and said to Sri Hridaya Chaitanya:

**157.** "I have seen a marvel — a beautiful man
whose body is filled with prema for Gaura and Nityananda.

**158.** He is coming here, asking the way;
so many streams flow from his eyes that he cannot walk."

**159.** Sri Hridaya Chaitanya, hearing this,
knew that Narottama had come here.

**160.** In prema he hurried out to the outer gate,
saw Narottama coming — and his heart was soothed.

**161.** Narottama, seeing Sri Hridaya Chaitanya,
could not hold himself up and fell at his feet.

**162.** Sri Hridaya Chaitanya, taking him by the shoulders,
embraced Narottama and sprinkled him with tears from his eyes.

**163.** He quickly led him into Prabhu's temple
and gave him darshan of Nityananda and Chaitanya.

**164.** Narottama, seeing the two Prabhus,
fell to the ground in obeisance and wept.

**165.** Hridaya Chaitanya, calming him with care,
brought a garland-prasada and gave it to him in private.

**166.** The conversation that passed between them
I have no power to relate in detail.

**167.** Sri Hridaya Chaitanya Thakura mercifully
kept Narottama with him for two or four days.
