**168.** Entrusting him to the feet of Nityananda and Chaitanya,
he urgently bade him go to Nilachala.

**169.** What Sri Thakura Mahashaya became at the parting
cannot be described.

**170.** All the great devotees who were there,
seeing Narottama's state, were pained at heart.

**171.** At the thought of the virtues of Prabhu's devotees his heart overflowed;
as he walked, his helpless body would collapse in a swoon.

**172.** How wondrous was his gait in prema!
Whoever saw him but once found no peace of mind.

**173.** Others could not understand Narottama's ways:
he was most eager to go to Khardaha.

**174.** Along the way to Khardaha, where there were devotees' homes,
the Mahashaya stayed, met them and went on.

**175.** Entering Khardaha and seeing a marvel,
the most steady Narottama lost his composure.

**176.** At that moment Mahesha Pandita and others, from afar,
seeing Narottama, said softly:

**177.** "In separation from Prabhu we have become as if dead,
yet at the sight of him happiness arises in the heart.

**178.** Without Prabhu's power this would not be possible."
Saying this, they asked and learned who he was.

**179.** All said to Narottama again and again:
"Your name has long been known in the world.

**180.** How you left home and went to Vrindavana —
we heard all of it from people.

**181.** When you came by the forest road from Vrindavana,
we heard that the books were stolen and recovered.

**182.** We also heard that you had come to Navadvipa;
that is why we are still alive — to see you with our own eyes."

**183.** Saying this, all made themselves known
and, filled with prema, showed great affection.

**184.** Narottama floated in the tears of both his eyes,
falling at the feet of the devotees, rolling on the ground.

**185.** Prabhu's dear ones, embracing Narottama,
sprinkled him with tears, wholly losing their composure.

**186.** After a while, growing calm, they took Narottama
and quickly entered Prabhu's house.

**187.** Sri Vasudha and Jahnava, hearing about Narottama,
at once called him into the inner apartments.

**188.** Narottama, deeming himself blessed,
went and bowed at the feet of the two Ishvaris.

**189.** He bowed at the lotus feet of Sri Virabhadra
and on seeing him was overwhelmed with prema.

**190.** Sri Vasudha and Jahnava Devi, seeing Narottama,
lost their composure — their hearts overflowed with prema.

**191.** "The name 'Mahashaya' befits him" —
thus they praised him much and fondly to each other.

**192.** Showing Narottama the greatest mercy,
they kept him four days, unable to let him go.

**193.** They asked for all the news, item by item,
and Narottama told it in detail.

**194.** What went on in their hearts as they heard it —
who has the power to tell it with one mouth?

**195.** Sri Vasudha and Jahnava together with Virachandra
could not let Narottama go even for a moment.

**196.** The devotees who lived in the region of Khardaha
came to Khardaha and met Narottama.

**197.** Though they were sorrowful, still joy arose;
the affection they showed cannot be described.

**198.** The all-knowing Sri Jahnava Gosvamini
told Narottama something in private — I do not know what.

**199.** She quickly gave him permission to go to Nilachala
and again brought him together with all the devotees.

**200.** Mahesha Pandita and others dear to Prabhu
embraced Narottama again and again.

**201.** All bade him go to Nilachala,
and Narottama bowed at the feet of all.

**202.** Taking leave, he went off weeping and weeping,
and all the devotees wept, overwhelmed with affection.

**203.** Having gone some distance, all stopped,
calmed Narottama, and returned to their own places.

**204.** This is Sri Narottama's journey through Sri Gauda:
whoever hears of it has his desires fulfilled.

**205.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the third vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^03-1]: A verse by Narottama; the editions give no source (it is also quoted in the BR).
[^03-2]: The sending of the books and their theft in Vishnupura: BR 7; cf. *Prema-vilasa* 12–13.
[^03-3]: "Duhkhini (Duhkhi) Krishnadasa" was Shyamananda's former name.
[^03-4]: The tradition of how Radha gave Duhkhi Krishnadasa the name "Shyamananda" is told in the BR.
[^03-5]: Narottama first sees Navadvipa as it was in the Lord's time (the eternal lila), then in its present sorrow of separation; the same happened to Srinivasa (Vilasa 2).
[^03-6]: The Pandita is Gauridasa Pandita, who established the service of Gaura-Nitai in Ambika (Kalna); after him his disciple Hridaya Chaitanya.

---

# Vilasa 4. Narottama's Journey to Nilachala

*Narottama goes to Puri along the Lord's road; the old Vaishnava brahmana; meeting with Gopinatha Acharya and the associates in Puri; darshan of Jagannatha; Tota-Gopinatha and Mamu Gosani; Haridasa's samadhi; the dream of the Lord's kirtana before the chariot and His blessing; the return through Nrisimhapura and the meeting with Shyamananda*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of mercy!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya went to Nilachala,
his heart stirred as he thought of Chaitanya's pastimes.

**4.** By the road on which Chaitanya-chandra went to Nilachala
he went too, praising the good fortune of that road.

**5.** Where Prabhu had rested with His devotees,
there he stayed the night, talking of it.

**6.** The people along the way who had seen Sri Chaitanya-chand —
at the sight of them his heart could hold no composure.

**7.** Praising their good fortune again and again,
he went on, bowing to them all.

**8.** Seeing Narottama, all became attached to him;
all said, "This is a devotee of Chaitanya.

**9.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya Prabhu purifies the worlds;
who but His devotee could be like this?

**10.** Ah, what beauty, what a sweet gait!
The eyes are soothed to see him — wondrous are his ways of prema."

**11.** Saying this, people ran after him,
and Narottama sent them off with kind words.

**12.** Wherever Prabhu had manifested some pastime,
he heard of it from the people and stayed there.

**13.** In the morning he went on, and people went with him;
they could not be held back — there were crowds on the road.

**14.** Where Prabhu Nityananda broke the holy staff,
he went and was greatly overwhelmed with prema.[^04-1]

**15.** How Prabhu's staff came to be broken —
he heard the whole account from people's lips.

**16.** Generously rewarding all those people,
he went on with a wondrous gait, tears in his eyes.

**17.** Along that road came an old brahmana,
a great Vaishnava, expert in all the shastras.

**18.** Seeing Narottama's wondrous ways of prema,
he suddenly felt great love for him.

**19.** Coming slowly up to Narottama,
he spoke gently, looking at Narottama's face:

**20.** "What is your name, my child, where have you come from?"
Hearing this, he bowed respectfully and told him.

**21.** At Narottama's words the brahmana was greatly moved;
he embraced him and drenched him with the tears of his eyes.

**22.** Taking Narottama in his arms, he could not let him go,
and again spoke softly in the sweetest words:

**23.** "For many days I have been hearing about you,
and I had a great longing to see you, my child.

**24.** Today the Creator has been very gracious to me:
coming from the Kshetra, I have seen you on the road.

**25.** The devotees of Prabhu who are manifest in Nilachala
are all very kind to me.

**26.** There all of you are talked of constantly,
and filling my ears with it I soothe my life.

**27.** I heard how you came from Vrindavana,
and how on the way the books were stolen and recovered.

**28.** At the same time I heard that you would come to the Kshetra,
and I saw everyone eagerly awaiting you.

**29.** Gopinatha Acharya and the others at Kashi Mishra's house
have been speaking of you for many days.

**30.** In the village of Ramakeli Prabhu drew you to Himself,
and joy increased in Prabhu Nityananda's heart.

**31.** The Lord's devotees were amazed,
and since then all have longed to see you.

**32.** They all keep watching the road for you;
I set out from there only today.

**33.** Do not delay — go quickly;
I shall meet you there later."

**34.** As he was saying this, his son came there,
and he introduced him to Sri Thakura Mahashaya.

**35.** The affectionate brahmana told his son everything
and with great joy gave him to Narottama as a companion.

**36.** Taking leave, the brahmana went slowly on,
and Narottama put the dust of the brahmana's feet on his head.

**37.** Arriving in the Kshetra with the brahmana's son,
Narottama stood and gazed at the beauty of Narendra lake.

**38.** Remembering Prabhu's water-pastimes,[^04-2]
he lost his composure; tears streamed from his eyes continually.

**39.** Quickly bathing in the Narendra,
he went eagerly to the Lion Gate.

**40.** Meanwhile Gopinatha Acharya and Prabhu's other associates
at that very moment came out onto the road to the Lion Gate.

**41.** Sri Shikhi Mahiti says to Mangaraja:
"Why has joy suddenly risen in my heart?"

**42.** Kanai Khutiya says: "I do not understand the cause,
but such auspicious signs portend a great treasure."

**43.** Gopinatha Acharya says to Vaninatha:
"Narottama will surely come here today."

**44.** At that moment the most worthy brahmana's son,
coming on ahead, brought the news of Narottama.

**45.** What each became on hearing the news of Narottama
cannot be described.

**46.** The brahmana's son went back to Narottama
and from a distance told him who they all were.

**47.** Narottama, seeing them all,
could not hold himself up; tears poured from his eyes.

**48.** He bowed again and again, falling to the ground;
seeing that state, everyone's heart wept.

**49.** Gopinatha Acharya and the others, losing their composure,
took Narottama in their arms and were bathed in tears.

**50.** What happened at the meeting with Narottama
could not be described with hundreds of thousands of mouths.

**51.** Calming Narottama in many ways,
they took him to see Jagannatha.

**52.** As he entered the Lion Gate, Narottama
saw Patita-pavana and fell prostrate on the ground.[^04-3]

**53.** Seeing Sri Nrisimhadeva, he shed tears;
what arose in his heart cannot be told.

**54.** At the darshan of Jagannatha he lost his composure:
streams flowed from his eyes, wondrous bhavas arose.

**55.** Jagannatha and Balarama together with Subhadra
sported on the throne, the abode of bliss.

**56.** Sri Padmalochana, the great ocean of mercy,
showed Narottama the utmost mercy.

**57.** A servant of Jagannatha, knowing the Lord's intent,
brought a garland-prasada and gave it to Narottama.

**58.** All the servants of Sri Jagannathadeva,
seeing Narottama's state, were bathed in tears.

**59.** Moment by moment Narottama lost more of his composure,
unable to check the streams from his eyes, like a river.

**60.** Consoling Narottama, Gopinatha Acharya
took him from the holy temple to his own house.

**61.** At once giving him an experienced man as companion,
he sent him to have darshan of Gopinatha and the samadhi.

**62.** He let everyone know of Narottama's coming
and had various kinds of maha-prasada brought.

**63.** Meanwhile Narottama set off quickly,
and on the way he saw several people coming.

**64.** With great pain in their hearts
they said to one another: "Alas, what has happened!

**65.** What a height of happiness we saw here!
Now the Creator has turned everything upside down in Nilachala.

**66.** The devotees of Sri Gaurachandra, purifiers of the worlds,
are passing from sight one after another.

**67.** Gopinatha Acharya and the other great Vaishnavas —
we have seen how all of them have grown frail."

**68.** Someone said: "I have come from Gopinatha;
what I saw there I cannot tell.

**69.** Unable to bear his sorrow, Sri Mamu Gosani[^04-4]
lies in one spot as if dead.

**70.** That beautiful body has withered;
it seems he will soon pass from sight."

**71.** Hearing this talk, Narottama, distraught,
lamented so much that I cannot describe it.

**72.** Losing his composure, his body barely holding up,
he went to Tota and saw Gopinatha.

**73.** How sweetly, how gracefully He sits!
Who could keep composure, glancing at Him even once?

**74.** His glossy dark body surpasses a fresh cloud,
the sweetness of His face enchants millions of Kandarpas.

**75.** The beauty entered Narottama's heart;
he lost his composure and was bathed in tears.

**76.** He bowed many times, falling to the ground,
and the pujari brought and gave him a garland-prasada.

**77.** To the place where Sri Pandita Gosvami's seat is
his companion took him.

**78.** Rolling on the ground near the seat,
he bowed many times in distress.

**79.** Unable to check the tears that flowed from his eyes,
he raised his arms and cried again and again:

**80.** "Ah, Prabhu, Pandita Gosani Gadadhara!
You did not come before the eyes of a sinner like me!"

**81.** Saying many such things, he wept aloud —
at that weeping wood and stone split apart.

**82.** Sri Mamu Gosani, who had lain unconscious,
heaved a long sigh and rose up, weeping.

**83.** He asked everyone, "Tell me, who is weeping?"
All said, "Narottama has come from Gauda."

**84.** Hearing the name of Narottama, weeping and weeping,
he took Narottama in his arms and could not be calm.

**85.** He threw himself down on the ground;
a tumult of weeping rose in Gopinatha's house.

**86.** By Prabhu's will, growing calm after a while,
he asked after Narottama's welfare, gazing at his face.

**87.** Though his life hung in doubt from cruel sorrow,
still, seeing Narottama, he rejoiced.

**88.** Hearing Narottama's words, he was filled with prema
and entrusted Narottama to Gopinatha's feet.

**89.** He bade him: "Go quickly to see the samadhi:
the Acharya is there, watching the road."

**90.** Hearing this, Narottama bowed sorrowfully to the ground
and went with his companion to the seashore.

**91.** Seeing the samadhi of Haridasa Thakura,
he fell to the ground and wept bitterly.

**92.** In great grief he said again and again:
"I was deprived of that happiness — such is my evil fate!"

**93.** Saying many such things, he shed tears incessantly;
whose heart would not melt to see that state?

**94.** The Vaishnavas who were there in service of the samadhi
with great effort calmed Narottama

**95.** and sent him to Gopinatha Acharya's house;
Narottama, overwhelmed, bowed and went.

**96.** The people of the Kshetra, seeing Narottama on the road,
left all their work and went along with him.

**97.** Narottama, honouring them all,
went quickly to Gopinatha Acharya's house.

**98.** Gopinatha Acharya, full of affection,
seated him beside himself and spoke sweet words:

**99.** "Everyone longs at heart to see you;
rest a moment and then go to their houses."

**100.** Meanwhile, hearing of Narottama's arrival, all
were most eager to see him.

**101.** What can I say of the state they were in, in Nilachala!
Without Prabhu they had no desire for food or water.

**102.** With great difficulty they kept their bodies alive,
always rolling on the ground, their eyes streaming.

**103.** They sighed long and often, very weak,
unable to walk, their bodies tottering.

**104.** Yet to see Narottama at Gopinatha's house
they came — love filled their bodies with strength.

**105.** At that time Narottama with his companion,
going there, saw them all coming along the road.

**106.** Narottama asked his companion
who each one was, and he told him all.

**107.** Narottama bowed at the feet of them all,
and all of them embraced Narottama.

**108.** Holding him in their arms, they went into the house
and drenched Narottama's body with the tears of their eyes.

**109.** Narottama, seeing and touching them,
could not hold himself up; streams flowed from both his eyes.

**110.** Gopinatha Acharya with the greatest care
calmed them all and seated them well.

**111.** All asked Narottama about his welfare,
and Narottama told everything from beginning to end.

**112.** What their state became on hearing it —
what can I say? Only the fortunate saw it.

**113.** Gopinatha Acharya said eagerly to all:
"Partake of Sri maha-prasada together with Narottama."

**114.** Hearing this, all, with great affection at heart,
sat down with Narottama to honour maha-prasada.

**115.** By Prabhu's will they ate a little prasada
and with most loving words fed Narottama.

**116.** After rinsing their mouths, all went to their dwellings,
and they told Narottama to rest.

**117.** Having rested, Sri Thakura Mahashaya
bathed and so on, knowing it was time for darshan.

**118.** Kanai Khutiya took
Sri Thakura Mahashaya to Jagannatha's temple.

**119.** The evening arati and everything up to the putting to rest
Narottama watched, standing apart.

**120.** With Kanai Khutiya and many others
he came to Gopinatha Acharya's house.

**121.** No one could leave Narottama and go,
but at the Acharya's word they went to their own homes.

**122.** The Acharya said to Narottama: "This place is secluded;
lie down here and sleep now."

**123.** The Acharya's fatherly love cannot be told;
when Narottama had lain down, he went to his own house.

**124.** But sleep would not come to Narottama:
he could not check his great anxiety and grief.

**125.** When by Prabhu's will he dozed a little,
in a dream he saw his Beloved before the chariot:

**126.** Krishna Chaitanya, enchanter of the worlds, Nitai,
Sri Advaita, Pandita Gadadhara Gosani,

**127.** Srivasa Pandita, Murari Gupta, Govinda,
Haridasa, Kashi Mishra, Ramananda Raya,

**128.** Vasudeva Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya,
Kashishvara, the generous Jagadisha Pandita,

**129.** Vasu Ghosha, Mukunda, Madhava, Vakreshvara,
Gauridasa, Mahesha Pandita, Damodara,

**130.** Svarupa Gosani, Shuklambara Brahmachari,
Dasa Gadadhara, Yadu, Shridhara, Kamsari,

**131.** Suryadasa, Ramai, Sundara, Dhananjaya,
Ramananda Vasu, Ghosha, Shankara, Sanjaya,

**132.** Lokanatha, Bhugarbha, Sri Rupa, Sanatana,
Sri Jiva, Gopala Bhatta, Acharya-nandana,

**133.** Krishnadasa Brahmachari, Pandita Raghava,
Paramananda Bhattacharya, Acharya Madhava,

**134.** Raghunatha Bhatta, Sri Tapana, Raghunatha,
King Sri Prataparudra, Acharya Gopinatha,

**135.** Sri Shikhi Mahiti and others renowned in the world —
all the devotees of Gauda, Vraja, Utkala, the south and other places

**136.** were sporting with Prabhu.
What can I say? The supreme bliss cannot be described.

**137.** How wonderful is the dancing before Jagannatha's chariot!
In the middle Gaurachandra, all around His dear ones.

**138.** How wondrous the beauty of Gaura with His associates!
There is nothing in the three worlds to compare with it.

**139.** At a mere sign from Prabhu His dear associates
began the lovely singing of the name.

**140.** Mridangas and other instruments played, sweet as nectar,
and on all sides "Jaya! Jaya!" resounded incessantly.

**141.** All the gandharvas and kinnaras in human guise
danced, sang and joyfully played various instruments.

**142.** The ocean of the happiness of sankirtana surged up
and flooded everything — heaven, earth and the netherworld.

**143.** Sri Krishna Chaitanya dances in sankirtana —
who in the three worlds does not long to see it?

**144.** Countless men and women run from all sides,
the gods with their consorts shower flowers.

**145.** The lame leap and strut about proudly,
those blind from birth see the sweetness of Gauranga.

**146.** He from whose mouth no word would come
calls "Gaurachandra!" again and again.

**147.** He whose eyes would shed no tear even if he were cut,
he too, hearing Gaura's virtues, is bathed in tears.

**148.** Hearing the lovely kirtana that purifies the worlds,
not even beasts and birds could keep still.

**149.** Narottama, standing to one side, watched,
overwhelmed with bliss, streams flowing from his eyes.

**150.** Seeing Narottama's state, Prabhu, in prema,
took both his hands and spoke softly:

**151.** "You will manifest unearthly song and music,
on hearing which all will rejoice.

**152.** You will see, when you perform kirtana:
just so I shall dance with everyone.

**153.** My inner longing will be revealed in song and music,
and the supreme rasika sadhus will always relish it.

**154.** Never worry about anything:
your desire will be fulfilled — I have said it.

**155.** Do not delay — go quickly to the land of Gauda
and manifest bhakti in every way.

**156.** Whoever comes and takes shelter of you
will soon gain the priceless treasure of prema.

**157.** With Ramachandra, the son of Chiranjiva Sena,
you will have a wondrous friendship.

**158.** What more shall I say to you, Narottama?
All your good and ill are Mine."[^04-5]

**159.** Seeing the utmost mercy shown to Narottama,
the ocean of everyone's bliss surged up.

**160.** Nityananda, Advaita, Gadadhara, Haridasa,
Sarvabhauma, Ramananda Raya, Srivasa,

**161.** Vakreshvara and all the others dear to Prabhu
embraced Narottama firmly.

**162.** Narottama floated in the tears of both his eyes
and, falling at their feet, deemed himself blessed.

**163.** Prabhu's associates, calming Narottama,
took his hand and spoke many words to him with affection.

**164.** All were agreed to send him to Gauda —
and then the sleep broke, and he was distraught with separation.

**165.** After a while Narottama, growing calm,
quickly finished his morning duties.

**166.** Gopinatha Acharya, together with Shikhi Mahiti,
quickly sent him to have darshan of Jagannatha.

**167.** Seeing the mangala-arati,
he could not hold himself up — his heart overflowed.

**168.** As soon as he thought, "How am I to go to Gauda?"
Jagannatha at once gave him a garland as His permission.

**169.** Receiving the garland-prasada, he reflected:
"Without doubt Prabhu has given me leave."

**170.** Staying a while and bowing to Jagannatha,
he went to Gopinatha Acharya's house.

**171.** Wherever Prabhu's associates were staying,
he bowed at the feet of each and came back to his lodging.

**172.** What Prabhu had said to Gopinatha in a dream,
he was eager to tell Narottama.

**173.** Composing himself, he said slowly to Narottama:
"Prabhu has directed you to go quickly to Gauda."

**174.** Having said much in this way, he fixed a day,
and the mahantas of the Kshetra gathered together.

**175.** Sending Narottama off to Gauda,
all said so much that it cannot be told.

**176.** At the parting, taking Narottama by the hand,
they spoke sweet words with great affection:

**177.** "The longing of our hearts is fulfilled — we have seen you;
but Srinivasa we shall not see again with our eyes.

**178.** We have heard that Duhkhini Krishnadasa is most worthy
and that he has recently received the name Shyamananda.

**179.** We had a great longing to see him" —
saying this, all were drenched with tears.

**180.** Narottama, seeing their state,
fell to the ground and bowed, weeping and weeping.

**181.** Calming themselves and calming Narottama,
all sent him on his way, remembering Krishna Chaitanya.

**182.** His companions, with great love,
went ahead carrying Sri maha-prasada.

**183.** What each one became after sending Narottama off
cannot be described.

**184.** Narottama went on as if dead,
and coming to the Narendra he wept bitterly.

**185.** The brahmana he had seen when coming to the Kshetra
he saw on that same road with his son.

**186.** As soon as he put the dust of the brahmana's feet on his head,
the brahmana embraced him and spoke softly:

**187.** "O Narottama, you are dearer to me than life!
I heard that you are leaving for Gauda today.

**188.** Having finished an important task, I hurried here;
by Jagannatha's will we have met on the road.

**189.** Otherwise I would have died, burning with grief at heart" —
saying this, he would not let him out of his arms.

**190.** After a while the old brahmana, distraught at heart,
gave him many blessings and let him go.

**191.** The brahmana went some distance with Narottama,
unable to part; his sorrow grew greatly.

**192.** Narottama with great effort turned him back
and went on quickly, much distressed.

**193.** Resting two days at Jajpur,
after some days he came to the village of Nrisimhapura.[^04-6]

**194.** Someone, coming from afar, told Shyamananda:
"Sri Thakura Mahashaya has come from the Kshetra."

**195.** As soon as he heard it, Shyamananda was overjoyed
and quickly went forward with his people to meet him.

**196.** Seeing each other, both lost their composure
and, bowing to each other, were bathed in tears.

**197.** Narottama took Shyamananda in his arms
and could not let him go — his heart overflowed with joy.

**198.** All the people, seeing this wondrous meeting,
could not check the ceaseless tears from their eyes.

**199.** Some said: "O brother, how wondrous!
Never since I was born have I seen such love."

**200.** Some said: "What I heard, that I have seen;
all the wishes of my heart — to whom shall I tell them?"

**201.** Some said: "O brother, ever since I heard of him
I had a great longing to see him once."

**202.** Some said: "Great is our good fortune —
that is why Sri Thakura Mahashaya has come here."

**203.** Some said: "Shall we have such good fortune —
shall we see Acharya Thakura even once?"

**204.** Some said: "Oh, the wish will be fulfilled:
Sri Acharya Srinivasa will give us his darshan."

**205.** Saying many such things, no one's mind was at rest;
the villagers came running in crowds.

**206.** Shyamananda gladly gave Thakura Mahashaya
a secluded lodging for fear of the crowds.

**207.** Still, to see Narottama
many people came — they could not be held back.

**208.** The merit of these people cannot be told:
they gained such a jewel by Shyamananda's mercy.

**209.** Seeing that by Shyamananda's mercy this land was blessed,
Sri Thakura Mahashaya was very happy.

**210.** Having bathed and so on and grown calm,
Narottama sat down with Shyamananda.

**211.** Finding the moment, Shyamananda carefully
told Sri Thakura Mahashaya, point by point:

**212.** "Acharya Thakura has gone from Vana-Vishnupura
to Yajigrama in these last few days.

**213.** Yesterday, in the first watch of the day,
came his gracious letter — see it, Mahashaya."

**214.** At the sight of the letter he overflowed with joy,
and as he read it his eyes were bathed in tears.

**215.** The Mahashaya read the letter with great care,
and Shyamananda, in prema, went on to tell him:

**216.** "From Sri Ambika, Prabhu has graciously[^04-7]
sent Sri maha-prasada together with a letter."

**217.** Reading the letter, Narottama was bathed in tears
and in prema praised Shyamananda's good fortune.

**218.** Bowing again and again to the Sri maha-prasada,
he ate it with boundless joy.

**219.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya told his companions:
"Bring the prasada here."

**220.** Taking the maha-prasada of Sri Jagannatha,
he put it into Shyamananda's mouth with great joy.

**221.** Having honoured the Sri maha-prasada with great care,
Narottama said softly to Shyamananda:

**222.** "The associates of Prabhu who live in Nilachala
are burned incessantly by the fire of separation.

**223.** Their state cannot be described:
only by Prabhu's will does life remain in them.

**224.** All of them long to see you:
do not delay — go quickly to Nilachala.

**225.** Having seen their feet there,
distribute the priceless treasure of prema in Utkala.

**226.** In some days I shall send a letter;
you will come to the village of Kheturi with your people."

**227.** Saying many such things, he stayed two days,
and all these words became known everywhere.

**228.** What the two were like at the parting
cannot be described with one mouth.

**229.** Sri Shyamananda's disciple Rasika Murari
stood to one side and wept, tears flowing from his eyes.

**230.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, with great affection,
embraced him and showed him great mercy.

**231.** All who had taken shelter at Sri Shyamananda's feet —
how he loved them cannot be described.

**232.** Gazing and gazing at Sri Thakura Mahashaya,
all in distress fell to the ground, rolling about.

**233.** Taking the dust of his two feet on their heads,
all, with hands on their heads, sobbed and wept.

**234.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya set out for Gauda,
unable to be calm, streams flowing from both his eyes.

**235.** Here Shyamananda wept, fallen on the ground,
and however he tried, could not be calm.

**236.** How wondrous were his ways — they cannot be understood!
With a distraught heart he set out for Nilachala.

**237.** Shyamananda went in prema to Nilachala,
and Sri Thakura Mahashaya came to Gauda.

**238.** How Shyamananda went to Nilachala —
see it in detail in the book *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**239.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the fourth vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^04-1]: The place where Nityananda broke the Lord's sannyasa staff (CC Madhya 5), on the Bhargavi river near Puri (Dandabhanga).
[^04-2]: The Lord's water-pastimes with His devotees in Narendra lake (CC Madhya 14; Antya 10).
[^04-3]: Patita-pavana ("Purifier of the fallen"), the form of Jagannatha at the Lion Gate of the temple.
[^04-4]: Following the 1924 edition, "শ্রীমামু গোসাঞি": Mamu Thakura, the servant of Tota-Gopinatha after Gadadhara Pandita (by tradition his relative and disciple); the 2021 edition has "শ্রীমান্ গোসাঞি."
[^04-5]: That is, "I take responsibility for all that befalls you" — a sign of complete protection.
[^04-6]: Nrisimhapura, a village in Utkala (Orissa) where Shyamananda was then living.
[^04-7]: "Prabhu" here is Shyamananda's guru, Hridaya Chaitanya of Ambika.

---

# Vilasa 5. Narottama's Journey through Gauda after Nilachala

*Shrikhanda: Narahari Sarakara and Raghunandana; Yajigrama: meeting with Srinivasa, news of the disappearance of the associates in Puri; Katwa: Dasa Gadadhara and Yadunandana, the place of the Lord's sannyasa; Ekachakra: Nityananda as an old brahmana shows the places of His pastimes and appears as Balarama*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of mercy!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** In Gauda there is a famous village named Shrikhanda;
there came Narottama, the abode of virtues.

**4.** Someone who saw Narottama on the road
was going to the house of Sri Sarakara Thakura.

**5.** Coming to the Thakura, he said softly:
"A man of wondrous beauty is coming.

**6.** What a gait of prema — now he walks, now he cannot!
Gazing toward Shrikhanda, he is bathed in tears.

**7.** It seems he has come from Nilachala;
four men are with him."

**8.** Hearing this, the Thakura said, "What more is there to say?
Narottama has come from Nilachala."

**9.** Sri Raghunandana, hearing this, went out to meet him,
and seeing Narottama from afar, he rejoiced.

**10.** Narottama, learning from people who he was,
felt a joy that cannot be told.

**11.** As he fell to the ground and bowed to Sri Raghunandana,
he ran and embraced him and could not let him go.

**12.** His throat choked, streams flowed from both his eyes;
he could not say anything of all that rose in his heart.

**13.** After a while Sri Raghunandana, growing calm,
quickly went off with Narottama.

**14.** Coming to Sri Sarakara Thakura,
Narottama bowed, rolling on the ground.

**15.** Though the Thakura was burning in the fire of separation,
still, seeing Narottama, he rejoiced at heart.

**16.** "Come, come!" he said, spreading out both arms,
and taking Narottama in his embrace, he was bathed in tears.

**17.** With what wondrous affection he seated him beside himself,
and, looking at Narottama's face, spoke gently:

**18.** "I had a great longing to see you;
it is well that you came soon — I have seen you with my eyes.

**19.** Through you Prabhu will distribute the treasure of bhakti;
many people will take shelter of you.

**20.** Prabhu in His ecstatic mood will manifest Himself in lofty song —
who will not be maddened by your kirtana?

**21.** Prabhu will fulfil all your desires;
never worry about anything.

**22.** Go quickly to Kheturi by way of Yajigrama:
Srinivasa Acharya is watching the road for you.

**23.** He came from Vishnupura a few days ago
and is always anxious about you.

**24.** When he sees you his heart will be at peace;
yesterday he came here and went back home."

**25.** Saying this, he asked for news of Sri Kshetra,
and Narottama told him the state everyone was in.

**26.** What happened to Sri Sarakara Thakura on hearing it
I could not tell with a thousand mouths.

**27.** Growing calm, he bade Sri Raghunandana:
"Take Narottama to Gauranga's courtyard."

**28.** Sri Raghunandana, taking Narottama by the hand,
led him to Gauranga's courtyard, calming him.

**29.** Narottama, seeing the Deity of Gaura-Krishna,
could not restrain his heart; streams flowed from both his eyes.

**30.** Falling to the ground he bowed again and again —
who could keep composure seeing those transformations of prema?

**31.** After a while, growing calm, he gazed his fill,
and the pujari brought and gave him a garland-prasada.

**32.** Hearing that Narottama had come, the people of Shrikhanda
all came and gathered in Gauranga's courtyard.

**33.** What happened at their meeting
could not be described even with hundreds of mouths.

**34.** All spoke to Narottama in sweet words,
calmed him and took him to a lodging.

**35.** Narottama, seated in the lodging, at once
gave Sri maha-prasada to Sri Raghunandana.

**36.** Taking the maha-prasada of Sri Jagannatha,
he quickly went and gave it to Sri Sarakara Thakura.

**37.** The Thakura ate the Sri maha-prasada with care;
remembering the past, great grief arose in him:

**38.** streams flowed from both his eyes, he could not restrain his heart;
he heaved long sighs, saying "Gaurachandra!"

**39.** After a while, growing calm, he said to Sri Raghunandana:
"Give the Sri prasada to everyone."

**40.** Sri Raghunandana gave the Sri prasada to all,
and as they ate the prasada no one's mind could be still:

**41.** remembering Prabhu's wondrous pastimes in Nilachala,
all shed tears ceaselessly.

**42.** With great effort all grew calm,
but they could not let Narottama go even for a moment.

**43.** Having passed day and night in the relish of talks about Krishna,
Narottama in the morning performed his morning duties.

**44.** Having bathed and so on and seen Gauranga,
he quickly went to the Thakura.

**45.** Sarakara Thakura, looking at Narottama's face,
said with great affection, "My eyes are soothed.

**46.** I shall not see you again," he said,
and became distraught in a way that cannot be described.

**47.** Narottama, falling to the ground again and again,
took the dust of his feet, tears flowing from his eyes.

**48.** The Thakura embraced Narottama
and, remembering Gauranga, sent him off.

**49.** Narottama set out, having taken leave
and bowed to the associates who lived in Khanda.

**50.** Sri Raghunandana went some distance with him;
unable to part, his sorrow grew greatly.

**51.** He gave him a man to go with him to Yajigrama
and consoled Narottama in many ways.

**52.** As he sent him off, his heart was breaking;
again and again he gazed at Narottama's face.

**53.** Embracing him, he stood still and composed himself,
and Narottama, bowing, was bathed in tears.

**54.** Distraught, he went along the road to Yajigrama;
whoever saw that state of his floated in tears of prema.

**55.** The wise man who had come from Khanda
pointed out the Acharya's house from afar.

**56.** Meanwhile Srinivasa Acharya, in his own house,
was teaching the shastras to his disciples.

**57.** At that moment someone came hurriedly and said:
"Sri Thakura Mahashaya has come from the Kshetra."

**58.** Someone else said: "What a marvel I have seen with my eyes:
gazing toward Yajigrama, he loses his composure."

**59.** Hearing this, Srinivasa Acharya was going out to meet him,
when Narottama came and entered the house.

**60.** Seeing each other, both were bathed in tears;
in the hearts of both the ocean of prema surged.

**61.** As Srinivasa spread his arms to embrace him,
Narottama fell to the ground in obeisance.

**62.** Who can understand the wondrous ways of these two?
It is known everywhere that only their bodies are different.

**63.** After a while both grew calm and sat down,
and told each other all that had happened.

**64.** What he had heard of the state of the devotees in the Kshetra,
he asked Narottama about again and again.

**65.** At that moment a brahmana came from the Kshetra,
a great Vaishnava, learned in all the shastras.

**66.** In the hope of studying the books of the Gosvamis
he offered himself to the Acharya.

**67.** Acharya Thakura received him courteously
and asked for news of Sri Nilachala.

**68.** Heaving a long sigh and awash in tears, the brahmana
said: "Nilachala has been emptied of its jewels.

**69.** From the day after
Sri Thakura Narottama came there, disaster struck:

**70.** one after another almost all have disappeared;
Shyamananda came — and fell into an ocean of sorrow.

**71.** The state he fell into cannot be described;
only by Prabhu's will did life remain in him.

**72.** Those who still remained consoled Shyamananda
and sent him off to his own land; I saw this and came away.

**73.** I could not stay in the Kshetra — what more can I say?
Day and night I travelled to come to Gauda."

**74.** As he spoke, the brahmana lost his composure
and wept, taking the names of the devotees of the Kshetra.

**75.** Acharya Thakura took that brahmana in his arms
and, weeping, overwhelmed, was bathed in tears.

**76.** Narottama too wept, his heart in great distress;
how much he lamented cannot be told.

**77.** Vyasa Chakravarti, Krishnavallabha and all the others[^05-1] —
what can I say of the state of them all?

**78.** After a while Acharya Thakura, growing calm,
consoled the brahmana and gave him a lodging.

**79.** Acharya Thakura, won over by his love,
began his studies with him that very day.

**80.** He took Sri Thakura Mahashaya aside
and told him many things — who can understand them?

**81.** They passed the night in talks about Prabhu,
and in the morning Narottama took his leave.

**82.** The state of the two at the parting —
seeing it, no one could keep his composure.

**83.** The Acharya, gazing at the road Narottama had taken,
became like one inert, streams flowing from both his eyes.

**84.** Vyasa Chakravarti and others went some distance with him,
and Narottama with effort sent them back.

**85.** Narottama went on, bathing in his tears,
and came to Kantaka-nagara, to Bharati's place.[^05-2]

**86.** What befell him at the sight of Dasa Gadadhara's Gauranga —
who could describe it?

**87.** Sri Gadadhara's disciple Sri Yadunandana,
known as Chakravarti, expert in all the shastras,

**88.** seeing Narottama's state, greatly moved,
came out of Prabhu's temple.

**89.** Giving Narottama the garland from Prabhu's neck,
he embraced Narottama and was bathed in tears.

**90.** His throat choked, and he said slowly:
"It is well that you came quickly to Kantaka-nagara.

**91.** For you my Prabhu Gadadhara
was so anxious — who can understand his heart?

**92.** At times, forgetting himself, he said again and again:
'Look, look, how far off is Narottama?'

**93.** O brother, what more can I say of what has happened?
Day by day the boundless ocean of sorrow grows.

**94.** When Vishnupriya Ishvari passed from sight,
he came from Navadvipa and lives here in seclusion.[^05-3]

**95.** Neither food nor drink pleases him, he grieves incessantly;
his golden body has grown dim and thin."

**96.** Saying many such things to Narottama,
he took him to where Dasa Gadadhara was.

**97.** He was sitting grey with dust,
eyes closed, streams flowing down his chest.

**98.** Remembering the lovely deeds of Sri Gaurachandra,
he heaved long sighs and said, "Hari, Hari."

**99.** Finding the moment, Yadunandana said:
"Narottama has come here from the Kshetra."

**100.** Hearing Narottama's name, he opened his eyes
and saw Narottama weeping, having lost his composure.

**101.** Spreading his arms, he took Narottama in his embrace
and washed Narottama's body with the tears of his eyes.

**102.** Though burnt by the fire of separation, still he rejoiced,
and holding Narottama in his arms could not let him go.

**103.** Narottama, falling at Gadadhara's feet,
washed both his feet with the tears of his eyes.

**104.** What he asked when he had calmed Narottama,
Narottama told him point by point.

**105.** What went on in his heart as he heard it —
who has the power to describe it with one mouth?

**106.** Mercifully he said to Narottama again and again:
"All your desires will be fulfilled.

**107.** Prabhu will surely dance in your kirtana
and shower prema — you will see it with your eyes.

**108.** Go quickly to the village of Kheturi
and distribute the treasure of Sri Gaurachandra's prema."

**109.** Saying many such things, he was lost in great affection
and could not check the tears of prema from his eyes.

**110.** Sri Yadunandana and others, informing him with care,
took Narottama to Bharati's place.

**111.** They said to Narottama in sweet words:
"Here Sri Keshava Bharati lived.

**112.** In this place Prabhu had His head shaved;
here, from Bharati, He took sannyasa."

**113.** As soon as they said this, everyone's throat choked,
tears flowed from their eyes like the current of a river.

**114.** Narottama floated in the tears of both his eyes
and, almost senseless, fell and rolled on the ground.

**115.** His body grey with dust — seeing that state,
who could restrain his heart?

**116.** After a while all regained outer consciousness
and saw the wondrous Gaurachandra in the temple.[^05-4]

**117.** Prabhu, unable to bear the grief of His dear ones,
calmed them all by the grace of His form.

**118.** Narottama stayed there that day;
what befell him cannot be told.

**119.** How he took his leave in the morning —
who could keep composure enough to describe it?

**120.** Constantly remembering Nityananda-Balarama,
he went to Rarh, to the village of Ekachakra.

**121.** As he was entering the village, merciful Nityananda,
in the guise of an old brahmana, asked Narottama:

**122.** "Tell me your name, where have you come from,
on what business, where are you going, where do you live?"

**123.** Narottama said: "My name is Narottama.
I have come from the Kshetra; I have business in this village.

**124.** It is known that Nityananda descended here,
whose mother is Padma and father Hadai Pandita.

**125.** Where He was born and the places of His pastimes —
all that I have set my heart on seeing.

**126.** Across the Padmavati is a village named Kheturi;
I live there and shall go there from here."

**127.** Hearing Narottama's sweet, gentle speech,
He laughed within Himself but did not show it.

**128.** He said to Narottama: "I know everything;
I shall show you — come with me.

**129.** Look, here Nityananda with His friends,
dressed as a cowherd boy, playfully tended the cows.

**130.** Here Nityananda, taking the plough and pestle,
went about giving everyone the boon of fearlessness.

**131.** Here Nityananda performed the rasa-lila,
here He built the bridge and entered Lanka,

**132.** slew Ravana and rescued Sita —
look, here are the endless pastimes of Ayodhya.[^05-5]

**133.** As Balarama sports in Shvetadvipa,
so Nityananda sports in this place.

**134.** Look, here is Hado Pandita's house:
here Nityananda Raya was born.

**135.** Crawling about the courtyard,
here He played, seizing a snake by its hood.

**136.** Look, here was His first tonsure,
here the Enchanter of the worlds put on the sacred thread.

**137.** Here He worshipped Vishnu with care —
see, this is the temple of Vishnu.

**138.** Here the sannyasi ate in supreme bliss
and begged Nityananda from Hado Ojha.

**139.** Taking Nityananda, the sannyasi went along this road,
and the villagers ran to see Nitai.

**140.** Here all wept aloud
as the sannyasi quickly led Nityananda away.

**141.** Here the mother of Nityananda-chandra
rolled on the ground, crying, 'O my son! O my son!'

**142.** Here Hado Pandita, whose life was in his son,
overwhelmed with weeping, rolled on the ground.

**143.** Here Padmavati Devi lay unconscious,
and Hadai Pandita, steadying himself, consoled her.

**144.** O Narottama, the places I have shown you
are hard even for the gods to attain — who else knows them?

**145.** In this village of Ekachakra Nityananda Raya
sports even now — the fortunate see Him."

**146.** Saying this, the brahmana vanished right there;
not seeing him, Narottama pondered in distress.

**147.** Narottama said: "I have been struck by a thunderbolt!
He was here — where has he suddenly gone?

**148.** If I do not see the brahmana again,
I shall kindle a fire and give up my life in it.

**149.** Ah, brahmana, where have you gone, leaving me?" —
and Narottama wept, raising his arms.

**150.** The ocean of mercy, Nityananda, the Holder of the plough,
in that same guise of a brahmana came before his eyes.

**151.** The brahmana became Rama of boundless sweetness:
horn and staff in hand, a crest on His head, lovely attire.

**152.** At that moment He became Balarama-Nityananda —
His beauty has no comparison in the three worlds.

**153.** Smiling, He said softly to Narottama:
"You are dear to Me; I cannot deceive you.

**154.** All your desires will soon be fulfilled.
Do not reveal that you saw Me here."

**155.** Saying this, Prabhu vanished right there,
and Narottama stood like a painted doll.

**156.** What he became in the ecstasy of that vision —
all that cannot come from my mouth.

**157.** He stayed that day in the village of Ekachakra
and in the morning went on, having seen many marvels.

**158.** "Glory to the Lord of Ekachakra, the son of Rohini!
Glory to Nityananda, the life of the poor and suffering!" —

**159.** taking the Lord's names thus, he went along the road,
his face and chest washed by the streams of both his eyes.

**160.** On the way to Kheturi he crossed the Padmavati;
the joy of the people cannot be told.

**161.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the fifth vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^05-1]: Vyasa Chakravarti and Krishnavallabha were disciples of Srinivasa Acharya at Yajigrama.
[^05-2]: Kantaka-nagara is Katwa, where the Lord took sannyasa from Keshava Bharati; there Dasa Gadadhara served the Deity of Gauranga.
[^05-3]: So the text: after Vishnupriya's departure Dasa Gadadhara left Navadvipa and settled in Katwa.
[^05-4]: That is, the Deity of Gauranga in the temple at Katwa.
[^05-5]: Nityananda is Balarama; in His childhood games at Ekachakra He enacted the pastimes of Krishna and Rama (CB Adi-khanda).

---

# Vilasa 6. Gauranga in Kheturi and the Gathering of the Vaishnavas for the Festival

*Narottama in Kheturi: in a dream the Lord reveals His Deity in a granary; the beginning of the kirtana; news of Srinivasa (his marriage, Ramachandra, his second journey to Vrindavana); the meeting at Budhari; the invitations and the coming of the Vaishnavas, led by Jahnava*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of mercy!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** Having crossed the river Padmavati, the Mahashaya
entered the village of Sri Kheturi at an auspicious moment.

**4.** People came from all sides and gazed their fill;
Kheturi was immersed in an ocean of bliss.

**5.** Sri Santosha and others carefully took
Sri Thakura Mahashaya to a very secluded dwelling.

**6.** Still there was no end to the coming and going of people;
the crowd stayed day and night, into the late watches.

**7.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, at night, in seclusion,
thought: "How shall I manifest the service?"

**8.** At the end of the night sleep drew him in,
and in a dream the son of Shachi said:

**9.** "O Narottama, watching the road for you,
I have long been present in a metal Deity.

**10.** In your kingdom there is a prominent householder;
everyone knows him as a very wealthy man.

**11.** In his house are many granaries for grain,
but no one can go there for fear of a huge snake.

**12.** In the biggest of them am I:
open the door and bring Me quickly."

**13.** Having spoken also of the making of other Deities,
He embraced Narottama and vanished.

**14.** In separation from the dream Sri Thakura Mahashaya
awoke in agitation and saw that six dandas of night remained.

**15.** Passing the rest of the night in chanting the holy name,
he quickly cleaned his teeth and bathed.

**16.** With great joy he asked everyone:
"Where is there a householder with a large family,

**17.** who has many granaries for grain,
where no one can go for fear of a snake?"

**18.** All said, "We know him."
The Thakura said, "Then let us go.

**19.** I have a very secret purpose there."
Saying this, the Mahashaya set out.

**20.** He went quickly to that householder's house,
and he with his whole family deemed himself blessed.

**21.** As Sri Thakura Mahashaya went toward the granary,
the householder anxiously fell at his feet,

**22.** and with joined palms said, weeping:
"Everyone knows there is danger there from a huge snake.

**23.** Many snake-charmers came to drive the snake away,
but at the snake's hissing none could stand firm.

**24.** We abandoned it long ago;
there are many valuable goods in it, and we grieve.

**25.** Come what may, we will not let you go there;
whatever your business, we will do it here."

**26.** Smiling, Sri Thakura Mahashaya said:
"Do not worry: the fear of the snake will go far away.

**27.** In your granary is something greatly needed;
you will see it with your own eyes, and your eyes will be fulfilled."

**28.** Saying this, the Thakura Mahashaya went on,
while all the people trembled with fear.

**29.** At the sight of Sri Thakura Mahashaya's approach,
the huge snakes vanished.

**30.** As Narottama, in prema, opened the door,
he saw the Moon of Navadvipa with His Beloved.[^06-1]

**31.** His body shone, adorned with ornaments —
nowhere could anything be found to compare.

**32.** As he spread his arms to take Him up,
He flashed like lightning and entered his embrace.

**33.** Seeing this, all the people were astonished,
crying "Jaya! Jaya!" with tears in their eyes.

**34.** They said to one another, "We have seen a marvel:
such a deed is never possible for a man."

**35.** Some said: "Others cannot recognize him;
by his mercy this land will be blessed."

**36.** Some said: "If we are fortunate,
we shall surely take shelter of these feet."

**37.** Crying "Glory, glory to Prabhu Narottama!"
all danced with arms upraised.

**38.** As the Mahashaya carried Prabhu to his dwelling,
people ran from all sides; there was a great crowd on the road.

**39.** Reaching his dwelling, he carefully seated
Gaurachandra with His Beloved on a wondrous seat.

**40.** Gazing on that beauty with unblinking eyes,
he was overwhelmed and could not check his tears.

**41.** Suddenly there arose in his heart
all that the science of music speaks of — dance, song and instruments.

**42.** At once the Mahashaya, clapping his hands,
sang of Gaurachandra's virtues with his people.

**43.** What wondrous singing the Mahashaya created!
At the sight of that dancing the pride of the gandharvas was destroyed.

**44.** Thus in the *Stavamrita-lahari*:
*To him who with his own dancing, destroying the pride of the gandharvas,*
*astonished all the people of the age of Kali,*
*who is renowned for the songs he himself created —*
*to Sri Narottama, obeisances, obeisances!*[^06-2]

**45.** On whomever he casts a merciful glance but once,
he becomes a singer and in his singing showers prema.

**46.** Even the lowest yavana, barbarian or evil-doer
sings, maddened, of Gauranga's pastimes.

**47.** The sound of the kirtana rose and filled the world;
the gods from heaven showered flowers.

**48.** Who could keep composure hearing that lofty singing?
What to speak of others — wood and stone split apart.

**49.** The gandharvas and kinnaras said: "What a marvel!
Who has suddenly made such singing known?

**50.** Such a mode of song is rare even in the world of the gods —
have dance, song and music themselves taken form?"

**51.** Some said: "What is impossible for a devotee of Chaitanya?
Chaitanya's devotees are worshipped by all the gods."

**52.** Saying this, they came in human guise
and fell at Narottama's feet, rolling on the ground.

**53.** What happened — who could describe it?
After a while all were calmed with effort.

**54.** That same day Balarama and many others
received the holy mantra from the Thakura.

**55.** From that day the kirtana had its auspicious beginning;
the other festivities I cannot describe.

**56.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, with joy in his heart,
beheld Gaurachandra with Lakshmi and Vishnupriya.[^06-3]

**57.** Having appointed the brahmana Balarama and other disciples
to the service of the Deity of Gaura,

**58.** and manifesting five more services by command in a dream,[^06-4]
he was anxious, having no news of the Acharya.

**59.** The Mahashaya pondered within himself:
"Without his permission how can I send a man?

**60.** What shall I do now? Many days have passed,
and no one has come here from Yajigrama."

**61.** As he pondered thus, growing anxious,
at that moment a man came from Yajigrama.

**62.** Seeing him, Sri Thakura Mahashaya rejoiced,
seated him and asked after his welfare.

**63.** He said: "All is well; I shall tell it in order.
In Yajigrama everyone is always anxious about you.

**64.** He[^06-5] mostly stays in Shrikhanda and Kantaka-nagara,
and at times goes about the region of Navadvipa.

**65.** One day Acharya Thakura went to Khanda,
and Sri Sarakara Thakura admonished him at length.

**66.** Again taking his hand, he commanded again and again:
'My child, you must marry.'

**67.** A command given again and again cannot be transgressed —
he married; hearing this, everyone rejoiced.

**68.** Having married, he lives in Sri Yajigrama,
and many learned men come there to become disciples.

**69.** A resident of Khanda, the son of Chiranjiva Sena,
named Ramachandra, expert in all the shastras —

**70.** that he has made him his disciple" — on hearing this,
Narottama felt prema spontaneously arise in his heart.

**71.** He went on: "Thus he has made many disciples
and spread the Gosvamis' books everywhere.

**72.** He sent news to Vrindavana,
and a man came from there with a letter.

**73.** The books that Sri Jiva Gosvami sent again
he quickly spread everywhere.

**74.** A letter came from Navadvipa with news:
many devotees in Nadia have passed from sight.

**75.** In Shantipura and other places the Lord's associates
have almost all passed away, burned by the fire of separation.

**76.** At the passing of Sri Sarakara Thakura and Sri Dasa Gadadhara
the Acharya's heart was burned.

**77.** The state the Acharya fell into cannot be told;
the life in the Acharya's body hung in doubt.

**78.** Hearing his weeping, beasts and birds wept;
the Acharya could not restrain himself even for a moment.

**79.** Without saying anything to anyone he left in the morning
and in a very short time entered Vrindavana.

**80.** Seeing the Acharya, all the Gosvamis rejoiced,
sat with him in seclusion and asked after his welfare.

**81.** How he took the books and how he spread them —
the Acharya told it all from beginning to end.

**82.** When he told of the passing of the Lord's associates,
all, distraught, wept.

**83.** Growing calm and understanding the Acharya's heart,
all spoke many words of consolation to the Acharya.

**84.** As four or five days passed thus,
Ramachandra Sena came and joined them there.

**85.** All learned about Ramachandra —
whose grandson and whose son he is.[^06-6]

**86.** He showed himself in Vraja as a great logician and poet,
and all the Gosvamis gave him the title 'Kaviraja.'

**87.** How the Acharya had married
all heard from the Kaviraja.

**88.** Sri Jiva Gosvami and the others, with great care,
sent him off, entrusting him with some books,

**89.** and gave him four men of Vraja as companions.
The Acharya set out, weeping much.

**90.** Sri Gopala Bhatta, Lokanatha and the others
were distraught, gazing at the road the Acharya had taken.

**91.** The Thakura came back to Gauda very quickly
and, going to Vishnupura, set the king at ease.

**92.** Hearing that he would come to Yajigrama,
I came alone with all the news."

**93.** Even as he said this, another man came
and gave him a letter in Sri Acharya's own hand.

**94.** Reading the letter, the Thakura Mahashaya
lost his composure, but still told the letter's meaning:

**95.** "Sri Acharya, leaving his house with his people,
came to Kanchanagadiya and made two disciples:

**96.** the brahmana Haridasa, foremost among the Lord's associates —
Shridasa and Gokulananda, his two sons —

**97.** the two brothers became disciples at their father's bidding;
they are great panditas, intoxicated with the rasa of sankirtana.

**98.** From there both have come with joy at heart:
Acharya Thakura came to Budhari yesterday."

**99.** "Today is a blessed morning for me!" — saying this,
he went with the two to the temple of Sri Gaura

**100.** and briefly told everything
to the pujari Balarama and all who were there.

**101.** The pujari Balarama, with supreme joy at heart,
fed the two with Sri maha-prasada.

**102.** Meanwhile the Mahashaya went to see
the storerooms prepared for the great festival.

**103.** Seeing all ready, with gladness in his heart
he set each one to his task.

**104.** Taking with him Devidasa, Gokula and Gauranga,[^06-7]
he set out at dawn for the village of Budhari.

**105.** As he entered the village, people, seeing him, rejoiced
and went quickly to tell Sri Acharya Thakura.

**106.** Acharya Thakura, with great joy in his heart,
saw at the gate of the house that the Mahashaya had come.

**107.** As the Mahashaya fell to the ground in obeisance,
the Acharya embraced him and could not be calm.

**108.** The ocean of prema surged up immeasurably;
all who saw it were amazed.

**109.** The Acharya himself introduced Sri Thakura Mahashaya
to Ramachandra and everyone else.

**110.** What a meeting it was, full of the bliss of prema —
I have told something of it at length in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**111.** Acharya Thakura told Sri Thakura Mahashaya
all that had happened, in a secluded room:

**112.** how he made Ramachandra and others his disciples,
how, after marrying, he went to Vraja,

**113.** how Ramachandra and others went to Vrindavana,
how he received the title "Kaviraja,"

**114.** how he came back to Gauda, to Vishnupura,
how he came from Yajigrama to Budhari,

**115.** how the title "Kaviraja" was given to Govinda —
all this he told with joy in his heart.

**116.** He asked Sri Thakura Mahashaya how things stood,
and the Mahashaya told everything in order:

**117.** how Sri Santosha Raya and others became disciples,
how Bhakti-devi showed mercy to all,

**118.** what festivity there was at the finding of Sri Gaura's Deity,
how the five other Deities were made,

**119.** how the great festival was prepared,
how the holy temple and the throne were built.

**120.** Having said this, he added: "As soon as I received the letter,
I set my mind on the festival at Phalguna-purnima."

**121.** The Acharya said, "That day is settled,"
and, saying this, had letters of invitation written.

**122.** Wherever devotees lived in Sri Gauda-mandala,
there letters of invitation were sent.

**123.** He quickly sent a man to Utkala
and wrote all this to Shyamananda.

**124.** Having joyfully sent letters everywhere,
he told the Mahashaya something in private — I do not know what.

**125.** Overwhelmed by the relish of talks about Krishna,
he entrusted Narottama to Ramachandra.

**126.** These two have one body, life and mind;
only to the eye are they two separate embodiments of prema.

**127.** The ways of Sri Acharya, Narottama and Ramachandra
became known within a day or two.

**128.** Some said: "These three are not human at all;
the three have appeared to deliver living beings."

**129.** Some said: "O brother, looking at the three,
it seems to me that they are one substance in three."

**130.** Some said: "What arises in my heart
I cannot reveal or tell to anyone."

**131.** People said many such things to one another;
for fear of the book's length I cannot expand on them.

**132.** The Acharya, keeping the Mahashaya four days,
sent him off to go ahead to Kheturi.

**133.** He gave him as companions Ramachandra and other dear ones,
and arriving in Kheturi, all saw Gauranga.

**134.** Shridasa and Gokulananda, treasuries of virtues,
Vyasa Acharya and others — all greatly learned —

**135.** all rejoiced at heart
to see the great abundance for Prabhu's service.

**136.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya together with Ramachandra
gave everyone lodgings, choosing secluded ones.

**137.** Narottama, Ramachandra and all the others
watched the road for the Acharya.

**138.** Meanwhile Sri Acharya, having made some more disciples,
came quickly to Kheturi with Govinda and others.

**139.** What wondrous splendour there was as he entered the village!
All the Vaishnavas came out to receive him.

**140.** The bliss of prema surged in everyone's heart;
they brought the Acharya to a wonderful lodging.

**141.** From the lodging Acharya Thakura with his companions
went quickly to have darshan of Sri Gauranga.

**142.** Seeing Gaura Raya with Lakshmi and Vishnupriya,
he was overwhelmed and bathed in tears.

**143.** Seeing the five other Deities as well,
he entered such prema as cannot be described.

**144.** After a while, growing calm, with his dear ones
he looked over all the provisions prepared in the house.

**145.** Coming back to the lodging with his people, he kept saying:
"Why is Shyamananda so late in coming?"

**146.** At that moment someone suddenly came and said:
"Shyamananda has come from Utkala."

**147.** Hearing this, the Acharya rejoiced at heart,
and the Mahashaya went forward with his people.

**148.** At that moment Shyamananda with his people
came and quickly entered the Acharya's house.

**149.** Shyamananda, seeing the Acharya,
could not hold himself up; tears poured from his eyes.

**150.** Acharya Thakura, out of affection, could not be calm
and embraced Shyamananda as he was bowing.

**151.** He drenched Shyamananda with the tears of his eyes,
and all, seeing it, lost their composure in prema.

**152.** The Acharya, gazing at Shyamananda's face,
asked after his welfare and after a while grew calm.

**153.** What Narottama and Shyamananda were like in prema
cannot be told.

**154.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya introduced
all the Vaishnavas to Sri Shyamananda:

**155.** Shridasa, Gokulananda, Vyasa Chakravarti,
Ramachandra, Govinda and others bearing the title "Kaviraja."

**156.** The joy of meeting Chattaraja, Ramakrishna, Kumuda and others[^06-8] —
who could describe it?

**157.** Sri Shyamananda's disciples, Rasikananda and others,
Narottama, the treasury of virtues, brought together with all.

**158.** The ways of love and devotion in their meeting —
whoever saw it deems himself virtuous.

**159.** Narottama Mahashaya with Ramachandra
took Shyamananda to a wonderful lodging.

**160.** Giving him lodging there, with great gladness of heart
he said affectionately to Rasikananda:

**161.** "My child, you will see to everything,
so that no one is in any way slighted."

**162.** Hearing this, Rasikananda, with joined palms,
deeming himself blessed, stood silent.

**163.** Seeing Rasikananda's conduct, the Mahashaya
was more pleased than can be told.

**164.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya together with Ramachandra
went to where Sri Acharya Thakura was.

**165.** He sent Sri Govinda Kaviraja,
and he came gladly to Shyamananda.

**166.** The mahanta Shyamananda, with supreme joy at heart,
went quickly to have darshan of Sri Gauranga.

**167.** He saw the sweet form, streams flowed from his eyes,
and again and again he bowed, falling to the ground.

**168.** All over his body a most lovely thrill;
his body was helpless in the ecstasy of prema.

**169.** After a while, growing calm, he said to Sri Govinda:
"Let me have darshan of the five other Deities too."

**170.** When Sri Govinda Kaviraja showed them to him,
what Shyamananda became I cannot describe.

**171.** Where the provisions for the festival were kept,
he showed him that too, and seeing it he was greatly pleased.

**172.** Meanwhile Sri Rasikananda, Sri Purushottama,
Sri Kishora and others, excellent in every way,

**173.** had all the provisions they had brought from their land
placed in Gauranga's storeroom.

**174.** The many people with them, by their care,
were given what was fitting and assigned lodgings.

**175.** In this way Shyamananda's disciples, the life of the Vaishnavas,
arranged everything everywhere.

**176.** Meanwhile Shyamananda went to where the Acharya was
and became absorbed in talks of Gaura and Krishna.

**177.** Having passed that day in supreme bliss,
in the morning all performed their morning duties.

**178.** Having bathed and so on, all thought within themselves:
"Why is Sri Jahnava Devi so late?"

**179.** At that moment a brahmana carefully reported:
"Jahnava Ishvari has crossed the Padmavati."

**180.** As soon as they heard it, all were filled with the bliss of prema
and went forward along the road to the bank of the Padmavati.

**181.** People everywhere ran about, jostling;
all said, "Jahnava, full of prema, has come!"

**182.** One of Sri Jahnava Ishvari's companions
came to announce the Ishvari's coming.

**183.** Seeing the Acharya approach, with great joy at heart
he bowed to the ground and reported the Ishvari's coming.

**184.** Acharya Mahashaya, bowing to him,
asked for particulars, and he related them in detail:

**185.** "Receiving the news from here by letter,
all became eager to come here.

**186.** There were the most generous Krishnadasa,
whose elder brother is Suryadasa Sarakhela,

**187.** Shrila Raghupati Upadhyaya, Mahidhara,
Murari Chaitanya, the charming Jnanadasa,

**188.** Kamalakara Piplai, Sri Jiva Pandita,
Madhava Acharya, whose deeds are well known,

**189.** Nrisimha Chaitanya Dasa, Kanai, Shankara,
Sri Gauranga Dasa, the wise Vrindavana,

**190.** Sri Minaketana Ramadasa Mahashaya,
Nakadi, Sri Balarama and others full of prema.

**191.** All submitted at the feet of the two Ishvaris[^06-9]
that they wished to go to Kheturi.

**192.** Hearing this, Jahnava Ishvari said with delight:
'Why delay? Let us go there quickly.'

**193.** At the Ishvari's command Sri Parameshvara Dasa
joyfully made everything ready for the journey.

**194.** On the day the Ishvari set out from Khardaha,
Vaishnavas came from afar to see her.

**195.** The Ishvari told them about the journey here,
and hearing it, all became eager.

**196.** All went quickly to their homes and came back;
that is why there was delay, and they set out again.

**197.** At the time of departure a voice spoke from the sky —
it was most wonderful; hear it, Mahashaya.

**198.** In a deep and solemn voice it said again and again:
'Srinivasa and Narottama are dear to Me;

**199.** with their people they are skilled in giving bhakti;
I am ever subdued by the prema of the two.

**200.** In the village of Kheturi, in sankirtana with My associates,
I shall dance, and everyone will see it.

**201.** By the power of My prema all people will be maddened;
no one will be left with any sorrow or grief.

**202.** By your going there everything will be accomplished;
all are watching the road for you.

**203.** From Kheturi you will go to Vrindavana,
and coming back from there you will distribute the treasure of bhakti.'

**204.** Hearing this, the Ishvari was astonished at heart;
she could not be calm, tears flowed from her eyes.

**205.** All the wise who were in the village of Khardaha,
and those who had come from other places —

**206.** all, hearing it, were maddened with gladness of heart;
unable to check their tears, they were bathed in them.

**207.** Sri Jahnava, remembering Gaura and Nityananda,
at once set out with everyone.

**208.** Having told Sri Vasudha Devi something in private,
having carefully settled Ganga and Virachandra,

**209.** having arranged everything in every way,
they travelled some distance by boat.

**210.** As soon as they set out, the cry spread through the land:
"The Ishvari is going to Vraja by way of Kheturi."

**211.** Some way along they left the boat
and went to the house of a fortunate, dear merchant.

**212.** Day and night they are intoxicated with Nityananda's virtues;
at the sight of the Ishvari the bliss of prema surged up.

**213.** Sri Ishvari, showing mercy to all,
stayed there that day with her people.

**214.** Raghunatha, the son of lame Bhagavan,
the dearest disciple of Jagadisha Pandita,

**215.** came and met the Ishvari there.
Rising very early, all came to Ambika.

**216.** Sri Hridaya Chaitanya went out a long way
and brought the Ishvari and everyone to his house.

**217.** What befell them at the sight of Nitai and Chaitanya-chand
cannot be described.

**218.** After a while, regaining composure,
they ate and did their other duties there.

**219.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari told Hridaya Chaitanya
everything, quietly.

**220.** Hearing it, Sri Hridayananda rejoiced
and resolved to go to the village of Kheturi.

**221.** Sri Vamshivadana's son Sri Chaitanya Dasa
at that moment came with his people to Prabhu.[^06-10]

**222.** Seeing the feet of Sri Jahnava Ishvari,
he deemed himself blessed; streams flowed from both his eyes.

**223.** Again and again he bowed, falling to the ground,
and at the Ishvari's bidding grew calm and sat down.

**224.** With gladness of heart they told him everything,
and hearing it he was overwhelmed with joy.

**225.** Sri Chaitanya Dasa and others resolved:
'We shall go to Kheturi to see the holy festival.'

**226.** With gladness of heart all made ready
and informed Sri Hridaya Chaitanya Thakura.

**227.** From Shantipura came a man
who related the news from there:

**228.** 'Sri Achyutananda Prabhu, Advaita's son,
is so worn by separation that his life hangs in doubt.

**229.** To carry out the command of Mother Sri Sita,
he will set out at dawn to go to Kheturi.'

**230.** Hearing this, the Ishvari's joy grew greatly,
and she quickly sent all the news back by him.

**231.** Sri Jahnava with everyone at the Pandita's house
passed the night with great gladness of heart.

**232.** At dawn she saw the mangala-arati
and offered herself at the feet of Nitai and Chaitanya.

**233.** Having bidden all engaged in the holy service to be careful,
the Ishvari set out with everyone for Navadvipa.

**234.** Gazing from afar toward Sri Navadvipa,
she shed streams from both eyes that washed her breast.

**235.** Remembering all those pastimes of Navadvipa,
she heaved sighs like tongues of flame.

**236.** Her body grew helpless, her heart distraught;
after a while by Prabhu's will she grew calm.

**237.** All those dear to Prabhu who were in Navadvipa
heard of Sri Jahnava Ishvari's coming.

**238.** With gladness of heart all came forward to meet her;
seeing them from afar, the Ishvari got down from her palanquin.

**239.** Having seen the Ishvari, all
extolled their own good fortune:

**240.** 'Today the Creator has given us a blessed morning!' —
saying this, they came near and bowed again and again.

**241.** What Sri Jahnava Devi did, what was in her mind —
who understands the wondrous ways of prema?

**242.** With the dear ones who had come with Sri Ishvari
all met, each as was fitting.

**243.** At the meeting everyone's composure was gone:
some took the dust of another's feet again and again,

**244.** some in prema, clasping one another by the neck,
remembering the Lord's pastimes, wept aloud.

**245.** How wondrous is the glory of prema — who knows it?
After a while those dear to Prabhu grew calm.

**246.** Srivasa Pandita's brother Pandita Shripati
said carefully to Sri Madhava Acharya and others:

**247.** 'It would be good to bathe in the Ganga here.'
Hearing these words, all were pleased.

**248.** All bathed in the Ganga right there,
and Shripati hurried into Navadvipa,

**249.** and quickly brought various provisions;
meanwhile all finished their bathing and daily rites.

**250.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari, with supreme joy at heart,
fed everyone, having eaten a little herself.

**251.** Quickly entering Navadvipa,
the Ishvari came to the house of Srivasa Pandita.

**252.** There came the son of Prabhu Advaita,
named Sri Achyutananda, purifier of the worlds,

**253.** Achyuta's brother Sri Gopala, full of prema,
Sri Kanu Pandita, Vishnudasa Mahashaya,

**254.** Vanamali Dasa and other very wise ones —
their meeting was most wonderful.

**255.** The ocean of prema surged immeasurably;
it cannot all be told with one mouth.

**256.** Shrimati Ishvari, joyfully, in complete seclusion,
told everything to Sri Achyutananda.

**257.** Hearing it, Prabhu Advaita's son, in prema,
lost all composure and roared incessantly.

**258.** When Shripati, Shrinidhi and others were told,
all wished to see the festival.

**259.** The news of the journey to Kheturi spread everywhere,
and all gathered at Srivasa's house.

**260.** The bliss there was that day as all dined there
cannot be described.

**261.** The people of Navadvipa ran from all sides;
there was a great crowd at Srivasa's house.

**262.** Receiving the blessed sight of the Lord's associates,
they soothed hearts burned by the cruel fire of sorrow.

**263.** After part of the night all the people went home,
and the Lord's associates all lay down to sleep.

**264.** Rising at dawn, all set out quickly
and came to Akaihat, to Krishnadasa's house.

**265.** The excellent singer Krishnadasa, in prema,
bringing them to his home, deemed himself blessed.

**266.** Preparing food very quickly,
he made ready to go to Kheturi.

**267.** Rising at dawn, all with joyful hearts
came very quickly to Kantaka-nagara.

**268.** First Krishnadasa Thakura went
and told Sri Yadunandana everything in detail.

**269.** As soon as he heard it, with great gladness of heart
he went forward and quickly brought them to his house.

**270.** There came Sri Raghunandana with his people;
with Shivananda came the brahmana Vaninatha,

**271.** Vallabha, Chaitanya Dasa, Bhagavata Acharya,
the dancer Gopala, the best of brahmanas Jita Mishra,

**272.** Raghu Mishra, Kashinatha, Pandita Uddhava,
Sri Nayanananda Mishra, Mangala Vaishnava —

**273.** so came many dear to Prabhu,
and their meeting was most wonderful.

**274.** Seeing the beauty of Dasa Gadadhara's Gauranga,
all were enraptured, and their eyes were soothed.

**275.** To where Gaurachandra took sannyasa
all went, weeping and weeping.

**276.** The state they all fell into at the mere sight of the place —
all that cannot come from my mouth.

**277.** After a while all grew calm
and quickly bathed in the Ganga.

**278.** Meanwhile Yadunandana and others, with great care,
arranged various sweets on plates,

**279.** offered them row on row to Sri Gaurachandra
and placed them separately in the lodgings.

**280.** All meanwhile finished their bathing and other rites
and partook of Sri maha-prasada with great care.

**281.** That day Jahnava Ishvari herself,
with joy in her heart, went quickly to the kitchen

**282.** and quickly cooked many kinds of dishes;
hearing of it, all were astonished.

**283.** She offered the food to Sri Gaurangachandra,
and Prabhu ate in supreme bliss.

**284.** After a while she carefully removed the offering
and with the greatest care fed everyone.

**285.** Everything was like nectar — what comparison can be given?
Whoever ate forgot himself in bliss.

**286.** Sri Ishvari partook of the prasada,
and the minds of all the mahantas rejoiced.

**287.** Sri Yadunandana Chakravarti and all the rest
ate afterwards, with all care.

**288.** What they felt as they tasted the Sri maha-prasada
cannot be told: streams flowed from both their eyes.

**289.** Remembering his own beloved Dasa Gadadhara,
he grew calm after a while, sitting in seclusion.

**290.** Greatly eager to go to Kheturi,
he made preparations for going there.

**291.** The servants who serve Sri Gaurachandra
he instructed to be careful in every way.

**292.** When everything was done, evening came,
and all the mahantas came to Gauranga's courtyard.

**293.** Having seen the arati of Sri Gaurachandra,
they sang the holy name for a while.

**294.** All passed the night in the relish of talks about Krishna
and slept a little, with gladness of heart.

**295.** At dawn, bowing to Gaurachandra,
they all came along the road this way, addressing everyone.

**296.** Today they quickly crossed the Padmavati
and sent me to bring you the news at once."

**297.** Hearing all this, Acharya Thakura
became such as words cannot express.

**298.** In the hearts of Sri Thakura Mahashaya, Shyamananda and the others
joy reached its limit.

**299.** As they went, they saw before their eyes
everyone coming, radiant as the sun.

**300.** As they gazed, their eyes forgot to blink;
their bodies grew helpless; they could not walk.

**301.** Seeing their state, Jahnava Ishvari,
remembering Prabhu, got down from her palanquin.

**302.** Sri Achyuta and others who were in carriages
gladly and quickly got down to the ground.

**303.** Srinivasa Acharya and others, awash in tears of prema,
fell rolling at the Ishvari's feet.

**304.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari could not be calm;
the mercy she showed — who could tell it?

**305.** To Sri Achyutananda and the others dear to Prabhu
they bowed at the feet one by one.

**306.** Gazing at Srinivasa Acharya and the others,
Sri Achyutananda and the rest could not restrain their hearts.

**307.** One, embracing Srinivasa, wept;
one embraced Narottama again and again;

**308.** one, embracing Ramachandra, would not let him go;
one drenched Sri Gokulananda with tears;

**309.** one, spreading his arms, clasped Shridasa;
one showed great affection to Shyamananda;

**310.** one, gazing at Sri Govinda Kaviraja's face,
embraced him, and streams from his eyes washed his breast.

**311.** Seeing such ways of prema, such a wondrous meeting,
even the gods deemed themselves blessed.

**312.** As they entered the village, people ran from all sides;
the village of Kheturi was drowned in a flood of bliss.

**313.** Acharya Thakura, respectfully asking everyone's leave,
took them to separate lodgings.

**314.** Where the Ishvari's lodging with her people was,
there he appointed Ramachandra Kaviraja.

**315.** To the lodging of Raghunatha Acharya and others
he appointed Kaviraja Karnapura.

**316.** Where Hridaya Chaitanya's lodging was,
there he carefully appointed Shyamananda.

**317.** Where Sri Chaitanya Dasa and others put up,
there he appointed Sri Nrisimha Kaviraja.

**318.** To the lodging of Shripati, Shrinidhi Pandita and others
he appointed Sri Vyasa Acharya.

**319.** To the lodging of Krishnadasa of Akaihat and others
Sri Vallavikanta was appointed.

**320.** In the lodging where Sri Raghunandana was with his people,
Sri Govinda Kaviraja was appointed.

**321.** To the house of the brahmana Vaninatha, Jita Mishra and others
he appointed Rama, Krishna, Kumuda and others.

**322.** To Sri Yadunandana Chakravarti's lodging
he carefully appointed Kaviraja Bhagavan.

**323.** Where the lodgings of other Vaishnavas were,
