**16.** "Sri Gopala Bhatta, Sri Bhugarbha, Lokanatha,
Sri Jiva, Sri Krishna Pandita and others together —

**17.** all these have come out to meet you."
Saying this, he pointed them all out from afar.

**18.** Seeing them all, Sri Jahnava Ishvari
became such as I cannot tell.

**19.** All the Gosvamis, at the sight of the Ishvari,
lost their composure and could not check their tears.

**20.** Falling to the ground and bowing at the Ishvari's feet,
they could not say anything of all that rose in their hearts.

**21.** With Krishnadasa Sarakhela, Madhava Acharya and all the others
the meeting took place as was fitting.

**22.** Sri Parameshvara Dasa, taking Govinda and others,
brought them together and introduced everyone.

**23.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja and all the others
bowed to the ground at the Gosvamis' feet.

**24.** All, with great mercy toward them,
embraced them with gladness of heart.

**25.** How that meeting was —
for fear of the book's length it is not told in detail.

**26.** Sri Jiva Gosvami, saying many things carefully,
seating the Ishvari in a palanquin carried by men,

**27.** quickly led everyone to a secluded lodging;
people ran from all sides to see the Ishvari.

**28.** From Sri Govinda, Gopinatha, Madanamohana
their servants came from there.

**29.** Who was doing what, no one remembered;
what wondrous bliss there was in Vrindavana!

**30.** When the Ishvari with everyone had settled in the lodging,
all the food was brought there.

**31.** Quickly feeding everyone with various prasada delicacies,
the Ishvari ate a little herself.

**32.** Sri Gopala Bhatta and others, with gladness of heart,
took leave and went to their own dwellings.

**33.** For the darshan of Govinda's raja-bhoga arati
all went together with Sri Jiva Gosvami.

**34.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari, entering the temple,
lost her composure on seeing Radha-Govinda.

**35.** Sri Madhava Acharya and others, at the sight of Govinda,
were overwhelmed; tears streamed from their eyes.

**36.** They saw the arati of Sri Govinda
and with great joy honoured the maha-prasada.

**37.** Coming back from there, all rested,
and Sri Jiva Gosvami went joyfully to his dwelling.

**38.** In the afternoon Sri Jahnava Ishvari
quickly bathed with everyone.

**39.** Going to the temples of Madanamohana and Gopinatha,
she had darshan, overwhelmed with prema.

**40.** Sri Radha-vinoda, Sri Radharamana,
Radha-Damodara — she saw them all.

**41.** The transformations of bhava at these darshans —
how can I, a wretch, describe them with one mouth?

**42.** The various cloths and ornaments she had brought
she offered everywhere.

**43.** What shall I say of the bliss in eating the prasada
of Sri Govinda, Gopinatha and Madanamohana?

**44.** Before Lokanatha and the others they told everything:
how the great festival in Kheturi had been,

**45.** how they had come along the road — they told it all,
and hearing it, all the Gosvamis rejoiced.

**46.** All the Gosvamis, gathering their composure,
told what had happened to them.

**47.** Hearing it, the Ishvari's heart was distraught,
and Madhava Acharya and others could not contain themselves.

**48.** After a while, growing calm, all said:
"Please hear some of Govinda's poetry."

**49.** Hearing Govinda's verses, they praised them greatly,
and the title "Kaviraja" was approved by all.[^09-2]

**50.** Sri Ishvari, with their permission,
went to Sri Kunda by way of Bahulavana.

**51.** Those who had come from Sri Kunda
all went along with the Ishvari.

**52.** Having seen Radha-kunda and Shyama-kunda,
they saw Sri Manasa-Ganga and Govardhana.

**53.** By way of Vrishabhanupura they went to Nandishvara
and saw the lovely village of Sri Javat.

**54.** Seeing the place where Balarama performed the rasa-lila,
they came back to Vrindavana.

**55.** For Sri Govinda, Gopinatha, Madanamohana,
Sri Radha-vinoda and Sri Radharamana,

**56.** and Sri Radha-Damodara — for all of them the Ishvari carefully
cooked and offered food in turn.

**57.** What bliss arose in the hearts of all the Gosvamis
as they ate that prasada — who knows?

**58.** So Sri Jahnava stayed some days,
and Sri Jiva Gosvami read her something from his books.

**59.** Again the Ishvari, taking everyone with her,
went in turn around the twelve forests.

**60.** What bliss there was on which day in which place —
for fear of the book's length it is not described.

**61.** She made ready to travel to Gauda,
and all the Gosvamis gave their consent.

**62.** Sri Govinda, Gopinatha, Madanamohana,
Radha-Damodara and Sri Radharamana,

**63.** Sri Radha-vinoda — from all of them
she took leave, telling what was in her heart.

**64.** What the Ishvari became at the parting
could not be described even with a thousand mouths.

**65.** Madhava Acharya and others calmed themselves with effort,
and that day all stayed in Vrindavana.

**66.** The dearest disciple of Gauridasa Pandita,
named Bara Gangadasa, incomparable in virtue,

**67.** had come to Vrindavana earlier;
he never stayed in one place but always wandered.

**68.** The Ishvari herself, showing him mercy,
commanded: "You will go with me to Gauda."

**69.** Receiving such a command, he made ready.
Meanwhile Govinda and others went to the Gosvamis' dwellings.

**70.** Bowing at the feet of Sri Gopala Bhatta and Lokanatha,
they told what was in their hearts.

**71.** Sri Bhatta and Sri Lokanatha were very pleased
and blessed Srinivasa and Narottama.

**72.** Placing their feet on the heads of all,
what more they said cannot be described.

**73.** From there they went to the dwelling of Bhugarbha Gosvami,
and he showed them all great mercy.

**74.** From there they went to Jiva Gosvami;
Krishnadasa Kaviraja and others were there.

**75.** At once they saw many together,
and falling to the ground they bowed at the feet of all.

**76.** All showed them great mercy;
Sri Jiva Gosvami said affectionately to Govinda:

**77.** "Tell the Acharya the news from here
and send me the nectar of the songs you have composed.

**78.** I shall finish this book very soon
and send the book with a man, together with a letter."

**79.** Saying this, he gave them the *Gopala-virudavali*,[^09-3]
and Krishnadasa Kaviraja and others praised it.

**80.** So, having visited everywhere,
they came quickly back to the lodging and rested.

**81.** When the Ishvari lay down late at night,
Gopinatha appeared to her in a dream.

**82.** He gave Jahnava the garland from His own neck
and, smiling softly, said slowly:

**83.** "What you resolved on seeing My Beloved,
go to Gauda and quickly send it.

**84.** She will be on the left, and this one on the right;
the beauty that will be — you will see it."[^09-4]

**85.** Saying many such things, He went into the temple;
when the sleep broke, she saw what she had seen.

**86.** Keeping Sri Gopinatha's garland hidden,
she went for the darshan of the Sri mangala-arati.

**87.** Having seen the arati and offered many prayers,
she came back to the lodging, greatly rejoicing.

**88.** When the night turned to morning, at a very auspicious moment,
Sri Ishvari set out from the lodging.

**89.** All the Gosvamis came there;
what they said cannot be described.

**90.** Having gone some distance, all, at the Ishvari's command,
took leave, bathed in streams of tears.

**91.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari could not be calm:
tears flowed from her eyes like the current of a river.

**92.** Krishnadasa Pandita, Sri Madhava Acharya,
Murari Chaitanya and others lost their composure.

**93.** They wept, and the people of Vraja wept;
having gone some distance, all grew calm.

**94.** The people of Vraja went to their dwellings,
and Sri Ishvari with everyone came to Mathura.

**95.** That day she stayed in Mathura
and carefully fed the brahmanas of Mathura.

**96.** From there she set out for Gauda
and after some days came to the village of Kheturi.

**97.** Hearing from people of the Ishvari's coming,
Narottama forgot himself for joy.

**98.** Calling Ramachandra, he told him the news;
hearing of her coming, all rejoiced.

**99.** They went out to meet her with their people,
and the people of the village of Kheturi ran from all sides.

**100.** Going some distance, they saw a wondrous procession,
and the meeting took place in great bliss.

**101.** They fell rolling at the Ishvari's feet,
and the Ishvari rejoiced to see them all.

**102.** Casting a merciful glance on the people of Kheturi,
she entered the village of Kheturi with everyone.

**103.** Sri Ishvari stayed in her former lodging,
and many people were appointed to serve.

**104.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja and all the others gladly
stayed in their former lodgings.

**105.** Baru Gangadasa and the other wise ones
stayed there, finding a wonderful secluded place.

**106.** Ramachandra Kaviraja with great care
took various provisions to every place.

**107.** Before the Ishvari, Sri Thakura Mahashaya
begged her again and again to bathe.

**108.** Quickly bathing in warm water and doing her rites,
the Ishvari ate a few prasada sweets.

**109.** Quickly cooking, she offered it to Prabhu,
and when He ate, everyone was glad.

**110.** So all the mahantas bathed and so on,
and Sri Santosha dressed them all in new cloths.

**111.** All ate the prasada sweets,
and meanwhile food was quickly cooked in one place.

**112.** The cooks, having offered the food to Krishna,
joyfully fed all the mahantas.

**113.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja and all the others
ate together with the cooks.

**114.** All chewed prasada betel
and lay down for a little while in their own places.

**115.** Baru Gangadasa and others, going to their own place,
rested for a while, happily.

**116.** Sri Ishvari, having rested a while,
quickly bathed and did her rites again.

**117.** Narottama, with Ramachandra, Santosha and others,
came with joyful hearts to Sri Ishvari.

**118.** At the Ishvari's command all sat on their seats;
Narottama wished to ask something.

**119.** Knowing what was in his heart, Jahnava Ishvari
told in detail of her journey to Vrindavana and so on.

**120.** Reflecting on the doings of all the Gosvamis,
she lost her composure; streams flowed from her eyes.

**121.** After a while, growing calm, she consoled everyone
and told by hints of Sri Gopinatha's command —

**122.** that she must go quickly. When she made this known,
Ramachandra Kaviraja said with joined palms:

**123.** "We had hoped you would stay here some days —
our wish has come to nothing."

**124.** The Ishvari said: "I can say nothing;
consider and tell me what is right, and I shall do it."

**125.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya said slowly:
"In two or four days set out for Khardaha.

**126.** It is better that the image be made in your presence;
in such matters there must be no delay.

**127.** On the road you will be delayed some days;
tomorrow morning I shall send a man to Khardaha."

**128.** Saying this, in Sri Jahnava Ishvari's presence
he had a letter written and gave it into Santosha's hand.

**129.** He wrote a letter to Acharya Thakura too
and quickly sent a messenger with the two letters.

**130.** It was time for the darshan of the evening arati,
and they went with Sri Ishvari to Prabhu's courtyard.

**131.** Sri Madhava Acharya and all the others came quickly
and joyfully saw Prabhu's arati.

**132.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari, entering the temple,
had darshan — what did she say to Him by a hint?

**133.** For a while she listened to the kirtana,
and Sri Ishvari went to her own lodging.

**134.** Madhava Acharya and all the others went to their lodgings,
and Ramachandra and others remained in Prabhu's courtyard.

**135.** Quickly taking Prabhu's prasada delicacies,
they fed everyone with the greatest care.

**136.** Tired from the road, all lay down,
and Sri Santosha and others massaged their feet.

**137.** Ramachandra went quickly to the Ishvari
and gave her a little prasada milk to drink.

**138.** All the brahmanas' wives who were with Sri Ishvari
he carefully fed a little.

**139.** When Sri Ishvari had lain down, the Mahashaya
came with Ramachandra to Prabhu's house.

**140.** Taking Ramachandra, Govinda and all the others,
the Mahashaya joyfully ate prasada.

**141.** Finding the moment, to Thakura Mahashaya
Sri Govinda Kaviraja carefully reported

**142.** what all the Gosvamis had bidden him say,
and having said it, gave him the *Gopala-virudavali*.

**143.** Hearing it, the Mahashaya fell silent
and lost his composure in a way I cannot tell.

**144.** After a while, consoling himself, he grew calm
and gave the *Gopala-virudavali* to Ramachandra.

**145.** Still he lay down distraught,
and in a dream Lokanatha appeared to him.

**146.** Narottama, falling at the Gosvami's feet,
drenched his lotus feet with the tears of his eyes.

**147.** The Gosvami embraced Narottama
and spoke nectarean words of consolation.

**148.** Giving Narottama great joy,
smiling softly, he vanished.

**149.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya was greatly delighted
and passed the rest of the night chanting the holy name.

**150.** All, having done their morning duties, with Narottama
became absorbed in talk of Sri Vrindavana.

**151.** So in great bliss they spent four days;
as before, the Ishvari cooked and so on.

**152.** The bliss she manifested in those four days —
who could describe it? The fortunate saw it.

**153.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya with Ramachandra
together fixed the time of departure.

**154.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja and some others
they sent with supreme joy to Budhari.

**155.** Sri Santosha said: "The departure is tomorrow morning —
quickly make preparations for the journey."

**156.** To all the pujaris he said with great care:
"Be attentive in the service of Prabhu and the Vaishnavas."

**157.** So he instructed everyone in every task
and reported it all to Sri Ishvari.

**158.** Meanwhile Sri Santosha Raya and some others
quickly made preparations for the journey.

**159.** What was fitting to send with Sri Ishvari
he entrusted to Sri Parameshvara Dasa.

**160.** When the night turned to morning, into Prabhu's courtyard
all came to take leave.

**161.** Seeing Prabhu, all, with gladness of heart,
offered many prayers in soft words.

**162.** The pujari gave everyone prasada garlands and cloths,
and all, bowing to the ground, took leave.

**163.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari, losing composure at the darshan,
took leave — what did she say within herself?

**164.** Bowing, placing the garland and cloth on her head,
she went out of the courtyard with everyone.

**165.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya took leave
and bowed, reciting a verse of his own composition.

**166.** Thus:
*O Gauranga, Vallavikanta, Sri Krishna, Vrajamohana,*
*Radharamana, O Radha, Radhakanta — obeisance to You!*

**167.** Taking with him all who were to go,
Ramachandra at the parting was distraught at heart.

**168.** The people of the village of Kheturi, losing their composure,
all went with them to the bank of the Padmavati.

**169.** Sri Ishvari, consoling all the people,
wholly without composure, boarded the boat.

**170.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya said to the helmsman:
"Take the boat quickly across the Padma."

**171.** The helmsman brought the boat across the Padma,
and the people here went back distraught to the village.

**172.** On the bank of the Padmavati the Ishvari with everyone
bathed and quickly came to Budhari.

**173.** The people of the nearby villages there
came running to see her.

**174.** Seeing all the mahantas, they
could not restrain their hearts and were bathed in tears.

**175.** Seeing them so, the wise ones rejoiced
and addressed them in the sweetest words.

**176.** Sri Ishvari with everyone, with gladness of heart,
stayed in a wonderful secluded lodging.

**177.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja asked the cooks
to go and cook.

**178.** They quickly and joyfully cooked,
offered the food to Krishna and removed it.

**179.** Sri Ishvari, cooking very briefly,
offered it to Krishna with milk and so on,

**180.** removed the offering, ate happily,
bathed again and sat on her seat.

**181.** Meanwhile the cooks with great care
fed all the mahantas.

**182.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya and all the others
ate together with the cooks.

**183.** What bliss the Ishvari felt that day:
she arranged the marriage of Baru Gangadasa!

**184.** Baru Gangadasa, the crown of the renounced,
who has no desire even in dreams,

**185.** was very abashed, but
married by the Ishvari's will.[^09-5]

**186.** How Jahnava Ishvari arranged the marriage
I cannot describe for fear of the book's length.

**187.** A lovely Deity named Shyamaraya —
what a wondrous pose, how lovely in every limb! —

**188.** said to the Ishvari in a dream:
"Now entrust Me to Baru Gangadasa."

**189.** At the command in the dream, the Ishvari with great joy
gave the service to Baru Gangadasa.

**190.** At once she established the order of offerings,
and the next day there was a great festival.

**191.** What the Ishvari told Baru Gangadasa in private
I cannot understand.

**192.** Leaving Baru Gangadasa in the village of Budhari,
she came with everyone to Kantaka-nagara.

**193.** Sri Yadunandana and others, with joyful hearts,
came out to meet them and brought them to Prabhu's house.

**194.** As Prabhu, having eaten, was about to rest,
at that moment all entered the courtyard.

**195.** Seeing Gaurachandra with great joy at heart,
she stayed with everyone in the former lodging.

**196.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya and all the others
were given wonderful lodgings in complete seclusion.

**197.** All went to bathe in the Ganga,
and meanwhile all the provisions were made ready.

**198.** A man was sent quickly to Yajigrama,
and Sri Acharya Thakura came with everyone.

**199.** Meanwhile all, having bathed and done their rites,
ate a few prasada sweets.

**200.** At that moment the Acharya arrived,
and seeing him, all rejoiced.

**201.** Srinivasa Acharya bowed to all,
and all, bowing, embraced Srinivasa.

**202.** Affectionately they asked Srinivasa of his welfare;
Srinivasa said: "This darshan is my welfare."

**203.** All who were with Srinivasa
bowed at the feet of all the mahantas.

**204.** All the mahantas did as was fitting;
the affection they showed I cannot describe.

**205.** Meanwhile the cooks cooked,
offered the food to Krishna and removed it.

**206.** Sri Ishvari, quickly and briefly cooking,
fed Prabhu and carefully ate herself.

**207.** Bathing again, she said to everyone:
"The day is ending; sit down to eat."

**208.** Taking Srinivasa Acharya and all the others,
all the mahantas ate joyfully.

**209.** Having rinsed their mouths, all sat on their seats,
and the Acharya went to have darshan of the Ishvari.

**210.** He bowed to the ground at the Ishvari's feet,
and the Ishvari affectionately asked of his welfare.

**211.** Srinivasa said: "By the darshan of these feet
all ill fortune of so many days has gone far away."

**212.** Sri Ishvari again, in the sweetest words,
told Srinivasa everything from beginning to end.

**213.** Srinivasa listened with gladness of heart
and came to the lodging of his dear Narottama.

**214.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja told him
all that the Sri Gosvamis had bidden him say.

**215.** Hearing it, the Acharya thought within himself:
"Shall I ever see Prabhu's lotus feet again?"[^09-6]

**216.** After a while Ramachandra Kaviraja
gave the Acharya the *Gopala-virudavali*.

**217.** The Acharya took it and placed it on his head,
and then went quickly to see the evening arati.

**218.** All the mahantas came together into the courtyard,
and at the darshan of the arati there was supreme bliss.

**219.** For a while they sang the sankirtana of the name;
the bliss there was cannot be described.

**220.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari, in Prabhu's temple,
lost her composure at the darshan of Prabhu.

**221.** Calming herself with effort, she went to her lodging;
after a while Gauranga retired to rest.

**222.** All the mahantas, taking Srinivasa Acharya,
went to the lodging, overwhelmed with bliss.

**223.** For a while they spoke of Sri Vrindavana;
it grew very late, and they lay down.

**224.** Srinivasa Acharya and others went to their lodging,
and the Acharya lay down with a distraught heart.

**225.** He slept a little, and at the end of the night
Sri Gopala Bhatta appeared to him in a dream.

**226.** Srinivasa fell rolling on the ground
and washed his lotus feet with the tears of his eyes.

**227.** Sri Bhatta Gosvami embraced him firmly
and spoke sweet words to Srinivasa:

**228.** "I am always near you;
birth after birth you are my chief servant."

**229.** Saying many such things, placing his feet on his head,
he vanished — and he came to his senses.

**230.** Meditating on the lotus feet of Sri Gopala Bhatta,
he rose and sat up, remembering Krishna Chaitanya.

**231.** Morning came; all, having done their morning duties,
joyfully bathed in the Suradhuni.

**232.** Having seen Sri Gauranga, they saw Bharati's place too;
in taking leave their hearts were distraught.

**233.** Saying many things to Sri Yadunandana and calming him,
Sri Ishvari came with everyone to Yajigrama.

**234.** Acharya Thakura sent a man to Khanda;
hearing the news, the people of Khanda rejoiced.

**235.** Sri Raghunandana came to Yajigrama
and saw Sri Jahnava Ishvari.

**236.** The gladness there was at meeting everyone
I cannot describe here at length.

**237.** He stayed a while in Yajigrama
and, hearing the accounts of Vraja, lost his composure.

**238.** Again, taking Acharya Srinivasa with him,
he said to the Ishvari in soft words:

**239.** "I have heard everything: there must be no delay;
you must go quickly to Khardaha.

**240.** Tomorrow morning please come to Khanda;
I must go there now."

**241.** Saying this and bowing, he set out for Shrikhanda,
having first asked leave of each of the mahantas.

**242.** Addressing Srinivasa Acharya and all the others,
Sri Raghunandana came joyfully to Khanda.

**243.** He had all the provisions prepared
and the lodgings cleaned at once.

**244.** He saw that everything everywhere was ready;
the people of Khanda were eager for darshan.

**245.** Meanwhile in Yajigrama the Ishvari with everyone
quickly finished the meal and so on.

**246.** The Acharya read from a book for a while,
and after that there was a wondrous sankirtana.

**247.** That day in Yajigrama there was no end to happiness;
what shall I describe? The fortunate saw it.

**248.** At dawn, having done her morning duties,
the Ishvari came with everyone to Shrikhanda.

**249.** The people of Khanda were overwhelmed with bliss,
seeing the two feet of Sri Jahnava.

**250.** The bliss there was at the darshan of all the mahantas —
what shall I describe? Whoever saw it knows.

**251.** Going quickly with everyone to Prabhu's courtyard,
their hearts rejoiced at the darshan of Prabhu.

**252.** Where Prabhu Nityananda danced,
where in prema he drank honey,[^09-7]

**253.** where Nitai watched Narahari's dancing,
where he became grey with dust —

**254.** seeing all those places with gladness of heart,
all stayed in a most wonderful lodging.

**255.** That day the cooking was finished quickly;
offering it quickly to Prabhu, all ate.

**256.** Knowing the Ishvari's wish, Sri Raghunandana
began a sankirtana that blesses the world.

**257.** In the sankirtana a wondrous flood of prema arose;
all swam, and no one's mind was steady.

**258.** All forgot themselves;
someone clasped another's feet and wept;

**259.** they rolled on the ground, overwhelmed at heart,
and everyone's body grew grey with dust.

**260.** As the song and music were, so was the dancing:
at it a heart like stone melts.

**261.** Someone, standing to one side, said to another:
"There is no comparison for the song, dance and music."

**262.** Someone said: "O brother, I feel I shall die,
taking on myself the misfortunes of this dance, song and music!"

**263.** Someone said: "In the ocean of song, dance and music
only he drowns to whom they give their mercy."

**264.** Saying this, drenched with streams of tears,
they went about like maddened elephants.

**265.** In the wondrous ecstasy of such a sweet kirtana
they remembered nothing — the night came to an end.

**266.** By Prabhu's will, growing calm after a while,
all came to the lodgings and rested.

**267.** They slept a little, and the night turned to morning;
all quickly finished their morning duties and so on.

**268.** The Ishvari quickly bathed and did her rites
and fed Prabhu with wonderful cooking.

**269.** At the Ishvari's command all — Madhava Acharya and others
together with Sri Raghunandana — sat down to eat.

**270.** The Ishvari herself served;
I do not know with what bliss all ate.

**271.** Sri Jahnava Ishvari, having fed everyone,
ate last of all, with love.

**272.** At the Ishvari's affection Sri Raghunandana
lost his composure and could not check his tears.

**273.** The people of the village of Shrikhanda, by the Ishvari's virtues,
were overwhelmed; their happiness grew moment by moment.

**274.** Sri Ishvari, having joyfully bathed again,
sat with Srinivasa Acharya and others

**275.** and said in the sweetest words, with great affection:
"From here all of you go quickly to Kheturi.

**276.** I shall set out for Khardaha tomorrow morning;
from there I shall send news quickly."

**277.** Saying many such things, they came to Prabhu's courtyard,
and there was bliss at the darshan of the evening arati.

**278.** After listening for a while to the singing of the name,
she took leave and went to her lodging.

**279.** Sri Raghunandana and others, before the Ishvari,
said something in sweet words:

**280.** "We have heard you will leave tomorrow morning;
we cannot keep you now by insisting.

**281.** You are independent — we are afraid to ask,
but it would be good if you came from time to time.

**282.** There is no one as shameless as I:
burning in such a fire of separation, I still live."

**283.** Hearing such words of Raghunandana,
the Ishvari lost her composure; streams flowed from both her eyes.

**284.** After a while Sri Raghunandana, growing calm,
came, having said many humble words,

**285.** and gave everyone Gauranga's prasada provisions;
though not hungry, all ate.

**286.** What he gave at once to go with Sri Ishvari
he entrusted to Sri Madhava Acharya.

**287.** It grew very late, and they lay down;
when the night turned to morning, all took leave.

**288.** How distraught everyone's heart was at that time,
what tears flowed — who has the power to describe it?

**289.** By the road by which Shrimati Ishvari had come before,
by that road, visiting everyone, she went to Khardaha.

**290.** How the Ishvari went, how people came and went —
what power have I to describe all that?

**291.** Meanwhile in Khanda Sri Thakura Raghunandana,
with the Acharya and others, was overwhelmed with prema.

**292.** That day the Acharya and others stayed there,
and in the morning, taking leave, they came to Yajigrama.

**293.** Staying two or four days in Yajigrama,
he went quickly to Nadia with two companions.

**294.** How they went about Navadvipa
is told at length in the book *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**295.** From there Sri Acharya, coming back to Yajigrama,
spent that night in sankirtana.

**296.** The next day he set out in the morning
and in four or five days came to the village of Budhari.

**297.** Leaving Sri Govinda Kaviraja and some others there,
the next day he came to Kheturi.

**298.** Hearing of his coming, people ran from all sides
and saw him with great gladness of heart.

**299.** Acharya Thakura and Sri Thakura Mahashaya
gladdened them all with sweet words.

**300.** Going quickly with everyone to Gauranga's courtyard,
they had darshan, wholly losing their composure.

**301.** At that moment a letter came from Khardaha;
reading the letter, they learned that all was well.

**302.** At once writing a letter of supreme good news,
they sent it with great joy to Khardaha.

**303.** After staying there a while, they came to the lodging
and day and night were intoxicated with talk of Krishna.

**304.** Every day was like a great festival —
I cannot describe it for fear of length.

**305.** The Acharya, Sri Mahashaya and Ramachandra, the three —
what they spoke of in private I do not know.

**306.** Sri Acharya, staying fifteen days,
went by way of Budhari to Kanchanagadiya.

**307.** There he stayed five days in supreme bliss
and with many disciples came to Yajigrama.

**308.** Continually he taught everyone the shastras of bhakti;
no one had the power to dispute with him.

**309.** Among all he roared like a maddened lion,
and hearing him, the logicians and others fled far away.

**310.** People came from various lands to study
and went home as teachers of the books of bhakti.

**311.** The great treasure of prema-bhakti, rare even for the gods,
he distributes by Sri Chaitanya's will.

**312.** Sinners and pashandis, by the Acharya's mercy,
sing Sri Krishna Chaitanya's virtues continually.

**313.** Not different in body from such a Sri Acharya,
Sri Thakura Narottama, an ocean of virtues,

**314.** with Ramachandra, dearer than life,
enjoys the festivity of prema in the village of Sri Kheturi.

**315.** The *Srimad-Bhagavata* and the Gosvamis' books
he continually has his disciples study.

**316.** Hearing his explanations of the books of bhakti, karmis and jnanis
become Vaishnavas and condemn karma and jnana.

**317.** Brahmanas and Vaishnavas from other lands, coming together,
study the Gosvamis' books and teach them everywhere.

**318.** So he distributes the jewels of the books of bhakti;
fortunate people hear of this.

**319.** One day Narottama with Ramachandra
sat speaking of Krishna.

**320.** At that moment a distinguished brahmana came
and said to the Mahashaya, weeping:

**321.** "Before my students, in my pride,
I said so much that it cannot be repeated.

**322.** From the day I regarded you as a shudra,
I was struck with leprosy.

**323.** To cure the disease I took many medicines,
performed rites for welfare to Shiva and much else.

**324.** Instead of a cure my torment grew,
and I resolved to enter the Ganga.[^09-8]

**325.** In a dream Bhagavati, turning away from me,
said in anger: 'A special misfortune awaits you.

**326.** In your pride you regarded Narottama as a shudra;
your study and learning have turned your mind to ashes.

**327.** Whoever regards Narottama as an ordinary man —
that sinner has no deliverance ever.

**328.** But if he, by your good fortune, is kind to you,
then alone you will be saved — know this for certain.'

**329.** Saying this, she vanished;
morning came, and I came here.

**330.** I was afraid to come before you,
but on the way a wise man said that you are merciful.

**331.** Seeing you from afar,
my eyes were soothed — as if I had found life.

**332.** Forgive my offense this once:
I take shelter at these feet of yours."

**333.** Saying this, he was bathed in the tears of both his eyes,
and, distraught, the brahmana fell to the ground.

**334.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya said again and again:
"You have committed no offense against me."

**335.** The brahmana said: "Place your feet on my head;
only then will this sinner's heart be at peace."

**336.** Narottama, remembering Sri Krishna Chaitanya,
embraced the brahmana in prema.

**337.** The brahmana, embracing him and taking the dust of his feet,
began to dance with both arms raised.

**338.** After a while the brahmana grew calm:
the disease had gone, his body had become clean.

**339.** Prema-bhakti arose in the brahmana's heart,
and when the disease was cured, he thought:

**340.** "It would have been better had the disease stayed in my body:
I fear lest pride should arise again."

**341.** So the brahmana thought, and by the power of bhakti
he became a Vaishnava together with his whole family.

**342.** All this became known everywhere,
and the brahmanas' fear grew immensely.

**343.** Someone said to another: "Be careful:
do not regard Sri Narottama as a shudra."

**344.** Someone said: "Brahmanas, intoxicated with pride,
cannot recognize such a jewel as Narottama."

**345.** Someone said: "Narottama is an abode of mercy:
by his own goodness he mercifully destroys the fear of worldly existence."

**346.** Someone said: "I saw with my own eyes:
by singing Sri Narottama's virtues the low became exalted."

**347.** Someone said: "Narottama is an ocean of virtues:
so many virtues are never possible in a human being."

**348.** Someone said: "He only has a human form:
he is a descent of a part of Ishvara to deliver living beings."

**349.** Saying many such things, the old and worthy brahmanas
each warned their own families.

**350.** They sing Sri Narottama's virtues continually;
what shall I say of all his deeds?

**351.** How the Mahashaya from time to time went to Yajigrama
and spent happy times with the Acharya,

**352.** what his meeting with Vira Hambira was like —
is described in the book *Bhakti-ratnakara*.

**353.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the ninth vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^09-1]: Madhupuri is Mathura.
[^09-2]: Govinda Kaviraja (Govindadasa), the famous poet of padas, brother of Ramachandra Kaviraja and disciple of Srinivasa.
[^09-3]: The *Gopala-virudavali* is a work of Jiva Gosvami; "this book," which he promises to finish, is apparently the *Gopala-champu*.
[^09-4]: According to tradition, on returning to Gauda Jahnava had a Deity of Radha made and sent it to Gopinatha in Vrindavana; "this one" is the earlier Radha beside Gopinatha. Cf. the *Prema-vilasa* (ev-jahnava-gopinatha).
[^09-5]: Baru (Bara) Gangadasa was a disciple of Gauridasa Pandita; the marriage of a renunciant at Jahnava's will underlines her authority as guru.
[^09-6]: That is, of Gopala Bhatta, Srinivasa's guru.
[^09-7]: On Nityananda's dancing in Shrikhanda and the cup of honey that became nectar — a tradition of Shrikhanda (cf. BR).
[^09-8]: That is, to end his life in the Ganga.

---

# Vilasa 10. The Deliverance of the Fallen

*Jahnava's Radha beside Gopinatha; the conversion of Harirama and Ramakrishna and the defeat of their father Shivai and the scholars; Ganganarayana Chakravarti; Narottama's disciples as a potter and a betel-seller humble the proud scholars; the conversion of King Narasimha, Rupanarayana, the bandit Chand Raya and the robbers of Vanga*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of compassion!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** The Acharya's disciples Rama and Sri Raghunandana —
the two came from Vrindavana.

**4.** Having told the Mahashaya of the well-being of Vraja,
they spoke again with great gladness:

**5.** "The Thakurani sent by Sri Jahnava Ishvari[^10-1] —
what wondrous beauty she has, we cannot say —

**6.** all the Gosvamis, at Gopinatha's command,
placed at the left side of Sri Gopinatha.

**7.** There was a great festival — we saw it with our eyes;
the people of Vraja and the Vaishnavas rejoiced with great love."

**8.** Hearing this account, all rejoiced,
and Ramachandra quickly sent the two to bathe.

**9.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya together with Ramachandra
went in prema, the two of them, to bathe in the Padmavati.

**10.** Along that road came two young brahmanas,
with goats, sheep and buffalo calves.[^10-2]

**11.** Seeing them, the Mahashaya said to Ramachandra:
"These two brahmanas are fit for the worship of Krishna."

**12.** Ramachandra, with those two brahmanas in view,
walked along, discoursing on various shastras.

**13.** The two brahmanas, who were close by,
hearing the proofs of the shastras, gained clear knowledge.

**14.** Seeing the two, they said to each other with gladness of heart:
"These are the Kaviraja and Sri Thakura Mahashaya.

**15.** From afar we heard from people of their greatness,
and today is a blessed morning — we have seen them face to face."

**16.** Saying this, they had the goats and the rest kept at a distance
and, very timidly, came near.

**17.** The two said to them in sweet words:
"What are your names, whose sons are you? Tell us who you are."

**18.** Hearing this, the brahmana said: "My name is Harirama,
and this is my younger brother, named Ramakrishna.

**19.** Our father, Shivai Acharya, is known to all;
he spends much wealth on the worship of Bhavani.

**20.** Balarama Kaviraja is a fine physician.[^10-3]
At our father's bidding we came to fetch the goats and the rest.

**21.** He has no fear of killing living beings:
he believes that by such acts one enjoys heaven."

**22.** Saying this, he called out to his men:
"Let the goats and the rest go, and all of you go across the Padma."

**23.** At the word of Harirama Acharya
the goats and the rest were let go right there.

**24.** All the men went across the Padmavati,
and the two spoke humbly before the two:

**25.** "We came here to buy goats at an auspicious moment:
by the sight of these feet the darkness of ignorance is gone.

**26.** Now accept these low brahmanas —
let your fame resound in the world."

**27.** Saying this, they fell prostrate on the ground,
greatly drenched with the tears of their eyes.

**28.** Seeing them distraught, the two grew compassionate;
each embraced one and calmed him.

**29.** Bathing in the Padmavati, the two, taking the two,
went joyfully to Prabhu's house.

**30.** That day, according to the shastras, was wholly auspicious;
worldly attachment was strong, yet love grew in their hearts.

**31.** Harirama Acharya, from Sri Kaviraja,
received mantra initiation with great care.

**32.** To Ramakrishna Acharya Thakura Mahashaya
gave mantra initiation with gladness of heart.

**33.** Harirama and Ramakrishna are most fortunate:
for them Ramachandra and Narottama are one.

**34.** The two fell rolling at the feet of the two,
and the two infused great power into the two.

**35.** Entrusting them to the feet of Radha-Krishna and Chaitanya,
they joyfully revealed to them the siddhanta of bhakti.

**36.** Harirama and Ramakrishna, two brothers,
were continually intoxicated with the rasa of prema-bhakti.

**37.** On the ekadashi after Vijaya-dashami
they came to Prabhu's courtyard and took leave,

**38.** and placing on their heads the dust of their gurus' feet,
they came from Kheturi to the village of Goas.

**39.** They met Balarama Kaviraja
and spent that night at his house.

**40.** Telling him all that had happened to them,
they heard all the news from him.

**41.** In the morning they met their father;
Shivai, seeing his sons, blazed up like fire.

**42.** So that all the people gathered there could hear,
he said to his son in great anger:

**43.** "You fool, tell me, in what shastra is it said
that a Vaishnava is greater than a brahmana?

**44.** Bhagavati has punished you at last:
your life is wasted without Bhagavati.

**45.** A brahmana, you became a disciple to serve some Vaishnava!
I shall have you defeated in the assembly of panditas.

**46.** By Durga's grace I shall punish you fittingly,
so that no one ever does such a thing here again."

**47.** Hearing this, Harirama in anger said again and again:
"Bring the panditas — let us see who has what power.

**48.** If you defeat me first, then I will grant it;
otherwise these words are like the croaking of frogs."

**49.** Hearing his son's words, the father lost all composure in anger
and quickly called his son into the assembly of panditas.

**50.** Harirama, like a lion, with great boldness
refuted every doctrine and set bhakti above all.

**51.** With proofs from the Vedas and others — that the Vaishnava is worshipful to all;
hearing this, all were defeated.

**52.** All the people looked at Harirama;
some said, "Where did he learn so much?"

**53.** Some said: "By the mercy of the Vaishnavas
learning comes without effort; there is no need to study.

**54.** What Narottama and Ramachandra are —
you must have heard of the virtues of the two.

**55.** These two brothers are vessels of their mercy;
they cannot be defeated anywhere."

**56.** So many spoke, seeing the assembly of panditas;
all were defeated and deeply ashamed.

**57.** Saying, "Great is the power of the Vaishnavas,"
all went humbly to their homes.

**58.** In great anger Shivai brought Murari,
a conqueror of all quarters, living in the city of Mithila.

**59.** With many people, a very learned brahmana,
intoxicated with pride, he regarded others as straw.

**60.** Balarama Kaviraja, going to him,
defeated him easily with his own words.

**61.** Defeated, the digvijayi said to all:
"It is beyond me to describe the greatness of the Vaishnavas."

**62.** Saying this, he gave away all his possessions
and out of shame never went back to his own land.

**63.** At once he took up the life of a mendicant,
and all say, "Murari is the third path."[^10-4]

**64.** Shivai, disgraced, became as if dead:
by hating the Vaishnavas he met great sorrow.

**65.** Bhagavati punished him fittingly,
and people became attentive to Vaishnava dharma.

**66.** All these accounts spread through every land,
and the Vaishnavas rejoiced to hear them.

**67.** Harirama and Ramakrishna Acharya, the two,
always sang the sankirtana of the name in great bliss.

**68.** Devoted to the supremely rare path of bhakti,
living amid the world, they were wholly detached.

**69.** Intoxicated day and night with Sri Krishna Chaitanya's virtues,
they always stayed with Balarama Kaviraja.

**70.** One day the two, on their own business,
came to the bank of the Suradhuni, to the village of Gambhila.

**71.** There were many learned men there, and foremost among them
was the virtuous Ganganarayana Chakravarti:

**72.** of a sattvic nature, devoted to good works,
wholly self-controlled, wise, generous in teaching.

**73.** Standing unnoticed in his own house,
he watched Harirama and Ramakrishna Acharya.

**74.** Seeing their divine radiance, he reflected:
"I have seen them before, but now I see a marvel.

**75.** The Kaviraja and Sri Thakura Mahashaya,
the two, have mercifully favoured them.

**76.** They have become Vaishnavas and captivate hearts with their beauty;
by the mercy of those two all the shastras have been revealed to them.

**77.** They defeated many panditas;
the digvijayi became a mendicant out of shame.

**78.** Their power comes from the strength of that mercy;
both are greatly fortunate, their birth is fruitful.

**79.** He who on their account reviled the Mahashaya —
Bhagavati in anger punished that pashandi.

**80.** I, a low brahmana, proud of my petty learning,
surely have also slighted that Mahashaya.

**81.** If the Mahashaya shows me mercy,
then I shall be saved from hell.

**82.** He will surely accept a sinner like me:
I have heard there is no one so merciful."

**83.** So reflecting, Ganganarayana,
deeming himself wretched, wept.

**84.** As he wept, bhakti arose in him,
and, lamenting greatly, he cried out again:

**85.** "There is no dharma higher than the Vaishnavas' dharma,
and my mind did not turn to such a dharma!

**86.** Shame, shame! What fruit is there in this wretched life?
I have passed my life in vain, without devotion to Krishna.

**87.** O Narottama, Prabhu, give me the treasure of bhakti:
I take shelter at your lotus feet."

**88.** So, in much lamentation, he passed day and night,
and toward the end of the night sleep drew him in a little.

**89.** In a dream Thakura Mahashaya appeared to him,
a form made of compassion, greatly radiant.

**90.** Smiling softly, he said to Ganganarayana:
"You are my servant — why do you lament?

**91.** All your desires will be fulfilled:
tomorrow, as you bathe in the Ganga, you will see me.

**92.** I have come here from Kheturi;
at the bathing I shall tell you everything."

**93.** Saying this, the Mahashaya vanished,
and the Chakravarti, waking from the dream, was distraught.

**94.** Morning came; quickly finishing his morning duties,
he went to the bank of the Ganga and sat in meditation.

**95.** Harirama and Ramakrishna Acharya came there,
and the Chakravarti received the two with great honour.

**96.** Most humbly he said softly:
"Stay here with me for a while.

**97.** If by my good fortune Prabhu gives me darshan,
you two make me known to him."

**98.** As they were speaking thus to one another,
the Mahashaya with everyone came to the bank of the Ganga.

**99.** Harirama Acharya said: "Look, here he is:
Prabhu has unexpectedly come to bathe in the Ganga."

**100.** Ganganarayana was overjoyed:
as he had seen in the dream, so he saw before his eyes.

**101.** The Chakravarti said to Harirama Acharya:
"What is each one's name? Tell me who they all are."

**102.** Harirama, naming them all from a distance,
went ahead and told about the Chakravarti.

**103.** The Mahashaya, smiling, said softly:
"Bring Ganganarayana to me quickly."

**104.** Harirama brought Ganganarayana,
and Ganganarayana bowed to the ground.

**105.** The Mahashaya in prema embraced him
and spoke sweet words to the Chakravarti:

**106.** "My child, seeing such conduct of yours,
what will the brahmanas here think?"

**107.** The Chakravarti said: "Prabhu, whom you favour —
does he fear brahmanas devoid of bhakti?"

**108.** Saying this, he bowed at Ramachandra's feet,
and he met with everyone as was fitting.

**109.** Seeing Ganganarayana's conduct, someone
said to another very quietly:

**110.** "I have seen him many times here in Gambhila,
but never seen such a disposition in him."

**111.** Someone said: "He who was intoxicated with learning —
how has he become so humble?"

**112.** Someone said: "It was never to be expected of him —
how did such bhakti arise in him?"

**113.** Someone said: "O brother, I have considered:
at the sight of the Mahashaya everything is possible."

**114.** Someone said: "Whomever the Mahashaya favours
attains all perfection without effort."

**115.** "Blessed, blessed is Ganganarayana in the brahmana line —
he has become a Vaishnava!" So saying, they praised him.

**116.** The Chakravarti wished to request something;
understanding this, Thakura Mahashaya said to him:

**117.** "Do not think of any of that now:
having bathed, I shall go at once to Budhari.

**118.** Tomorrow morning I shall go to Kheturi:
there is a special task at Gauranga's house.

**119.** Together with Harirama and Ramakrishna
come tomorrow and stay in the village of Budhari.

**120.** Gathering there with Karnapura and others,
rise early and come quickly to Kheturi."

**121.** Saying this, quickly bathing and so on,
the Mahashaya came with everyone to Budhari.

**122.** Ganganarayana quickly bathed in the Ganga
and brought Harirama and Ramakrishna to his home.

**123.** That day the three stayed in Gambhila,
and very early in the morning the three set out.

**124.** Going quickly to Budhari, with gladness of heart,
they stayed at the house of Sri Govinda Kaviraja.

**125.** Govinda's son is Divyasimha Kaviraja;
seeing his way of bhakti, they were amazed.

**126.** Karnapura Kaviraja and others were there;
in the morning all came quickly to Kheturi.

**127.** All went and saw Gauranga,
and everyone's mind was greatly delighted.

**128.** Ganganarayana Chakravarti, before Prabhu,
begged only for the fulfilment of his desire.

**129.** That day they spent in the bliss of sankirtana;
in the morning all performed their morning duties.

**130.** Considering it a most auspicious day,
the Mahashaya made Ganganarayana his disciple.

**131.** Giving the mantra initiation, the Mahashaya rejoiced
and entrusted him to the lotus feet of Sri Krishna Chaitanya.

**132.** Narottama Mahashaya, the incarnation of bhakti,
infused his own power into Ganganarayana.

**133.** Thus in the *Stavamrita-lahari*:
*Narottama, bhakti incarnate,*
*into whom he joyfully placed his own power —*
*may Sri Chakravarti Ganganarayana,*
*an ocean of the rasa of prema, be merciful to me!*[^10-5]

**134.** Ganganarayana was overwhelmed with bliss,
unable to check the tears of both his eyes.

**135.** He fell rolling at the lotus feet,
and the ocean of mercy, Narottama, embraced him.

**136.** He entrusted him to Ramachandra Kaviraja,
and he bowed at Ramachandra's feet.

**137.** Sri Govinda Kaviraja and all the others —
as he bowed, they bowed in return and embraced him.

**138.** There was bliss in the hearts of all the Vaishnavas;
the news of the mercy to Ganganarayana spread everywhere.

**139.** Ganganarayana, expert in all the shastras,
studied the books of the Gosvamis.

**140.** Ceaselessly in the ocean of the happiness of sankirtana
Ganganarayana swims in great bliss.

**141.** The Chakravarti became rich with the treasure of prema-bhakti,
his form even more radiant than before.

**142.** Ganganarayana became exclusively devoted to Krishna.
So the Mahashaya blesses brahmanas and others.

**143.** A foremost brahmana named Jagannatha Acharya
was greatly devoted to the worship of Bhagavati.

**144.** The Goddess, being pleased with him, commanded:
"Go and take shelter at Narottama's lotus feet.

**145.** Only then will your bondage to the world be cut;
you will obtain the treasure of bhakti, rare even for us.

**146.** Be exclusively devoted to that Prabhu's feet:
without worship of Krishna, life is in vain."[^10-6]

**147.** Receiving such a command, the brahmana at dawn
came in distress to the village of Kheturi.

**148.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya was seated;
coming before him, he bowed, falling to the ground.

**149.** With tears, distraught at heart, the brahmana,
with joined palms, said slowly:

**150.** "Bhagavati commanded me, and I have come to you;
my good and ill, Prabhu, now rest with you.

**151.** Give me mantra initiation and deliver me:
this sinner's all is these feet of yours.

**152.** My understanding is small; I do not know how to speak."
Hearing the brahmana's words, compassion arose in his heart.

**153.** Thakura Narottama made the brahmana his disciple,
and by the power of bhakti he became most excellent.

**154.** So the Mahashaya makes many his disciples;
some hear of it with joy, others are pained to hear it.

**155.** A king named Narasimha lived in a distant land;
many brahmana panditas stayed with him.

**156.** The brahmanas in anger said to the king again and again:
"Dharma is perishing, and no one looks into it.

**157.** Narottama Dasa, son of Krishnananda Datta,
taking up the Vaishnava doctrine, has ruined everything.

**158.** We do not know what sorcery he knows,
that brahmanas easily become his disciples.

**159.** If you say he is learned in the shastras —
that is only false pride before fools.

**160.** Would any words come to him before us?
Go quickly and take us with you.

**161.** You will see the fun: out of fear of me alone
he will run away from there with his bhava-kali.[^10-7]

**162.** Your fame will be great in every land;
through you the brahmana caste will endure.

**163.** The king is the punisher, for he is a portion of Ishvara;
otherwise many of the brahmana caste will be destroyed."

**164.** Hearing this, King Narasimha set out,
and Rupanarayana went with the king.

**165.** The teachers, taking many books,
went in great pride, rejoicing.

**166.** To Kumarapura, a village near Kheturi,
the king came with many people.

**167.** Hearing that the king had come there, the Mahashaya
said very softly to Ramachandra:

**168.** "We shall have to hold a debate with the teachers;
I thought it would interfere with bhajana."

**169.** Hearing such words of Sri Mahashaya,
Ramachandra Kaviraja said with a smile:

**170.** "Their pride will be crushed without effort,
and afterwards they will come and fall at your feet."

**171.** Saying this, Ramachandra and Ganganarayana,
the two, went to the village of Kumarapura.

**172.** On the way they became a potter and a betel-seller:
one carried betel on his head, the other pots.

**173.** Entering Kumarapura, at the market place,
the two set up a stall and sat down.

**174.** Here a student came to buy betel;
he asked the price, and this one answered in Sanskrit.

**175.** The student, proudly, spoke in Sanskrit —
and after two or four sentences he was defeated.

**176.** The betel-seller said: "Fool, what do you know?
If you are ashamed, bring your teacher."

**177.** The student went and said to his teacher:
"I have been defeated by a betel-seller and a potter.

**178.** In the village of Kheturi, where Narottama lives,
the betel-seller and the potter supply betel and pots.

**179.** What shall I say? Their learning is immense —
it seems these two may defeat even you.

**180.** If you can defeat the betel-seller and the potter,
then go to Kheturi; otherwise let us go home."

**181.** Hearing this, he became like fire and said again and again:
"Show me where this betel-seller and potter are!"

**182.** Saying this, the teacher went quickly
and debated various shastras with the betel-seller.

**183.** One by one the teachers came there,
King Narasimha and Rupanarayana.

**184.** On all sides a great crowd gathered;
what a wondrous battle of shastras took place between them!

**185.** The betel-seller and the potter, with great gladness of heart,
refuted and explained in the sweetest words.

**186.** The teachers were filled with great anger,
but could not establish their explanations against the unearthly ones.

**187.** All this cannot be told briefly:
the teachers were quickly defeated.

**188.** The king with the teachers went to their lodging;
there someone said to another, laughing:

**189.** "The teachers came like lions,
and, defeated, became like dogs.

**190.** They cannot know Sri Mahashaya even by name —
he whose disciples brahmanas are told by Parvati to become!"

**191.** So all spoke of the Mahashaya's greatness;
hearing it from people, the king became afraid.

**192.** He said softly to Rupanarayana:
"What is to be done now, brother? Tell me."

**193.** Rupanarayana said: "The essence of all
is the dharma of the Vaishnavas; there is no higher dharma.

**194.** If one always listens to blasphemy of Vaishnavas,
one surely goes to hell.

**195.** Let us go and take shelter at his feet;
only then shall we be saved — I tell you for certain."

**196.** Narasimha said: "That is what I have resolved:
there is no use in delay — let us go at once."

**197.** Rupanarayana said: "Stay here today;
tomorrow morning go with your people."

**198.** At once the news spread everywhere:
"Tomorrow the king will go with his people to Kheturi."

**199.** The teachers were in great distress:
for shame they could not appear before the king.

**200.** As if dead, they sat in their places
and said to one another, "What will happen tomorrow morning?"

**201.** Meanwhile, having defeated the teachers,
the betel-seller and the potter went to Kheturi.

**202.** Ramachandra, calling the poor, gave away the betel,
and Ganganarayana gave away the pots.

**203.** In great merriment the two came to Kheturi
and told everything to Sri Thakura Mahashaya.

**204.** Meanwhile King Narasimha thought within himself:
"Will he be merciful to such a wicked man?"

**205.** Rupanarayana, lamenting much, said:
"Without his mercy life is in vain."

**206.** Suddenly someone said from a distance:
"He will be merciful out of his own goodness."

**207.** Hearing this, they became most eager,
thinking, "How long until this night is over?"

**208.** It grew very late, and they lay down.
Meanwhile, among the teachers,

**209.** the foremost of all, extremely proud —
toward the end of the night he slept a little.

**210.** In a dream he saw the Goddess, sword in hand,
standing before him and speaking in great anger:

**211.** "In vain you studied, evil-minded one!
For reviling a Vaishnava you will fall low.

**212.** Only if I cut your head to pieces
will the grief of my heart be appeased.

**213.** Wicked asura, what lesson shall I give you?
If Narottama is merciful to you, you will be saved."

**214.** Saying many such things, glaring with blood-red eyes,
the Goddess stayed a moment and vanished.

**215.** The teacher awoke, trembling with fear,
and with a terrible cry woke everyone.

**216.** Weeping, the brahmana said to all:
"By great good fortune I have just been saved.

**217.** Because I regarded Narottama as low,
the Goddess came sword in hand to slay me.

**218.** If that Mahashaya shows mercy,
then I shall be saved from a terrible hell."

**219.** As he was saying this, the night turned to morning,
and he went and told all this before the king.

**220.** The king said: "I forbade you before — you did not listen;
you regarded the Mahashaya as an ordinary man.

**221.** What he does — is it within a man's power?
Sri Thakura Mahashaya is supremely worshipful."

**222.** Saying many such things, he calmed the teacher,
and in the morning, having bathed and so on, they made ready.

**223.** Without carriages the king with the teachers and others
went quickly to Kheturi, to Gauranga's courtyard.

**224.** At the sight of Gauranga, most humbly,
they bowed, rolling on the ground.

**225.** The very wise Ramachandra, Govinda and others who were there
received them with honour, and all were pleased.

**226.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya was in seclusion;
all were eager to see him.

**227.** At that time, having finished his regular tasks, the Mahashaya
came from a distance, and all gazed at his beauty.

**228.** King Narasimha and Rupanarayana
went forward from the courtyard to meet him.

**229.** Ramachandra said to the Mahashaya:
"This is King Narasimha, and this is Rupanarayana."

**230.** The two said: "Prabhu, how shall we introduce ourselves?
We are worldly men, low and great offenders.

**231.** We take shelter, yet we are afraid to ask:
give us mantra initiation and fulfil our desire."

**232.** Saying many such things, the two, falling to the ground,
bowed again and again, bathed in tears.

**233.** Seeing the two greatly distraught, the Mahashaya
consoled them much and embraced them both.

**234.** Narasimha and Rupanarayana, falling to the ground,
placed the Mahashaya's feet on their heads.

**235.** Sorrow went far away, bliss came into their hearts,
and bringing the teacher, they said to the Mahashaya:

**236.** "Of all the teachers he is the foremost;
his pride has gone — now deliver him."

**237.** The teacher, standing before the Mahashaya,
told in distress of the Goddess,

**238.** and said: "Forgive my offense:
I, a great evil-doer, take shelter."

**239.** Saying this, the brahmana wept, rolling on the ground,
and however he tried, could not compose himself.

**240.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, the embodiment of compassion,
embraced the brahmana, showing him mercy.

**241.** Receiving his touch, the brahmana, with joyful heart,
taking the dust of his feet, rolled in the dust.

**242.** Ramachandra calmed the teacher,
and the teacher deemed himself blessed.

**243.** All became vessels of devotion to Krishna Chaitanya;
all this became known everywhere.

**244.** The Mahashaya, happily satisfying everyone,
came with all to Prabhu's courtyard.

**245.** Having seen the raja-bhoga arati,
everyone's heart rejoiced greatly.

**246.** Sri Santosha Raya, honouring them all,
took them to a most wonderful lodging.

**247.** He quickly had various provisions brought there
and with great care offered them for cooking.

**248.** King Narasimha, the teachers and all the others
said, "We shall partake of the Sri prasada."

**249.** Hearing this, Santosha insistently and carefully
gave foodstuffs to the people who were with them.

**250.** King Narasimha and Rupanarayana,
the teachers and some other distinguished men

**251.** all went joyfully together there,
where Sri Thakura Mahashaya was with his people.

**252.** The bliss of the meal there —
I cannot describe it for fear of the book's length.

**253.** Rupanarayana and the others ate the prasada
and passed the day and night in supreme bliss.

**254.** The next day, at a most wonderful hour,
they became disciples with great bliss at heart.

**255.** Sri Thakura Narottama showed them great mercy:
giving them mantra initiation, he entrusted them to Prabhu's feet.

**256.** For some days all stayed there
and studied the books of the Gosvamis.

**257.** The bliss day by day cannot be told:
all became eligible for prema-bhakti.

**258.** Without sankirtana no one's mind was at rest:
all became intoxicated with the bliss of sankirtana.

**259.** The holy songs composed by Sri Govinda Kaviraja
they always relished with great love.

**260.** From the holy lips of Ganganarayana Chakravarti
all heard the *Srimad-Bhagavata* with great happiness.

**261.** Day and night no one had any leisure:
all were intent on performing the limbs of bhakti.

**262.** Whoever came once to the village of Kheturi
could not leave such bliss and go away.

**263.** King Narasimha and Rupanarayana
went to their own land, and the two came back quickly.

**264.** King Narasimha's wife was Rupamala,
most devoted to her husband, modest and virtuous.

**265.** Seeing her way of bhakti, with joy at heart,
the Mahashaya gave her the holy mantra.

**266.** Bliss grew greatly in Rupamala's heart:
she took the vow of chanting a hundred thousand names.[^10-8]

**267.** For the feet of Radha, Krishna and Chaitanya with their associates
a deep attachment arose in her, growing day by day.

**268.** So Sri Thakura Mahashaya, out of his own goodness,
shows mercy, and all sing his virtues.

**269.** A bandit named Harishchandra Raya,
hearing of his virtues, took shelter of the Mahashaya.

**270.** Giving him mantra initiation, he delivered him,
and later he came to be called Haridasa.

**271.** He became eligible for rare bhakti
and gave up his zamindari in Jalapantha.

**272.** Seeing the mercy to the bandit, amazed,
sitting in private, people said to one another:

**273.** "Sri Thakura Mahashaya is a treasury of virtues:
he delivered the bandit without effort."

**274.** Someone said: "The chief of the bandits is Chand Raya:
people always tremble for fear of him.

**275.** If the Mahashaya is merciful to this low one,
then this land will be safe in every way."

**276.** Someone said: "O brother, do not worry:
Chand Raya will surely receive mercy.

**277.** By that mercy all this evil-mindedness will go,
and Chand Raya with his kin will become a Vaishnava."

**278.** Someone said: "This evil-doer is the last of all;
I feel he will soon be delivered."

**279.** At that moment a brahmana came joyfully and said:
"The Mahashaya has shown mercy to Chand Raya!

**280.** Making Sri Narottama's lotus feet his all,
he has been delivered from the peril of worldly life.

**281.** Before, at the sight of him there was great fear;
now, at a mere glance, bliss arises.

**282.** What shall I say? All his former evil-mindedness is gone;
he has become peaceful — what a wondrous Vaishnava!

**283.** I saw it and came: in Prabhu's courtyard
he dances in the sankirtana, grey with dust."

**284.** Hearing all this, greatly delighted,
people ran to see Chand Raya.

**285.** From afar they saw Chand Raya in prema,
fallen on the ground, bathed in tears.

**286.** Thrills all over his body, trembling again and again —
seeing this, all the people were amazed.

**287.** Someone said: "At last the fear of bandits is gone;
the Mahashaya has protected us in every way."

**288.** Saying many such things, with great bliss at heart,
all extolled Sri Chand Raya's good fortune.

**289.** At that moment some men came there,
all bearing various weapons, from distant lands.

**290.** Without making themselves known, they asked these people:
"What sort of Vaishnava has Chand Raya become, and how?"

**291.** Hearing this, they told them briefly:
"Chand Raya with his kin was a worshipper of the Devi.

**292.** Chand Raya is a mighty zamindar,
the chief of bandits, most wicked in conduct.

**293.** The Devi, greatly angered at seeing his misdeeds,
punished him fittingly through a brahma-daitya.[^10-9]

**294.** Then the same Devi, seeing his life in danger,
commanded: 'Take shelter at Narottama's feet.

**295.** Narottama Mahashaya is most merciful:
he will save you from hell.'

**296.** At this command in a dream Chand Raya at once
took shelter at the Mahashaya's feet.

**297.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, seeing his great suffering,
out of his own goodness gave him the holy mantra.

**298.** His evil-mindedness was gone, he deemed himself lowly;
the yavana king seized him by force and punished him.[^10-10]

**299.** Chand Raya counts all those sufferings as nothing:
his mind is fixed solely on Prabhu's feet.

**300.** The yavana brought an elephant to kill Chand —
the elephant fled in fear of Chand Raya.

**301.** The king, greatly alarmed, said to everyone:
'Keep him in prison with the greatest care.'

**302.** Chand, reflecting, rejoiced:
'I did evil — this punishment is just.'

**303.** Someone said, 'Remove the trouble with the Devi's mantra.'
Chand Raya said, 'I will not touch any other mantra.'

**304.** Seeing such steadfastness, Prabhu became merciful,
and suddenly great fear came upon the yavana.

**305.** Begging pardon, he released Raya,
and he came here a few days ago."

**306.** Hearing all this, they asked everyone again:
"Where is Sri Thakura Mahashaya?"

**307.** Someone said: "Look, there under the tree
he is sitting with his dear associates."

**308.** As soon as they saw the Mahashaya from afar,
Bhakti-devi showed them mercy at once.

**309.** Throwing away their swords and other weapons,
they fell before the Mahashaya, rolling on the ground.

**310.** Seeing them all greatly distraught, the Mahashaya
said in sweet words: "Tell me who you are.

**311.** Where have you come from, and for what purpose?"
Hearing this, all said with tears:

**312.** "We are bandits of Vanga, evil brahmanas;
Chand Raya is, as it were, our chief.

**313.** We were going by boat to rob
and came to Raya to take his counsel.

**314.** From people we heard about Raya —
and as soon as we heard, our hearts turned.

**315.** When from afar we saw your lotus feet,
we did not understand what happened in our hearts.

**316.** There is no one as low as we are:
we take shelter — now deliver us."

**317.** Saying this, all wept, distraught,
and the Mahashaya consoled and calmed them all.

**318.** At that moment Chand Raya came there,
and all were overjoyed to see him.

**319.** Chand Raya, seeing them so humble,
was greatly delighted and praised them all.

**320.** Sri Thakura Mahashaya, some days later,
mercifully made them all his disciples.

**321.** All became eligible for great bhakti;
their wondrous doings I cannot relate at length.

**322.** Whoever lets all these accounts enter his ears
loses his evil-mindedness and obtains holy bhakti.

**323.** Listen to all this continually and with care:
Narahari tells the *Narottama-vilasa*.

*Thus ends the tenth vilasa of the Narottama-vilasa.*

[^10-1]: That is, the Deity of Radha sent by Jahnava to Gopinatha in Vrindavana (Vilasa 9, vv. 81–85).
[^10-2]: The animals were being taken for sacrifice to Bhavani (Durga).
[^10-3]: Balarama Kaviraja was a physician (vaidya) of Goas, a Vaishnava; see below, vv. 39, 60.
[^10-4]: A play on words: "Murari is the third path" (মুরারেস্তৃতীয়ঃ পন্থাঃ) is a well-known saying about Murari Mishra, the Mimamsa philosopher who founded a "third" school; here it refers to the defeated digvijayi named Murari, who chose a "third path" — mendicancy.
[^10-5]: A verse from Vishvanatha Chakravarti's *Stavamrita-lahari* (praise of Ganganarayana, his parama-guru in Narottama's line).
[^10-6]: Cf. *Prema-vilasa* 19 (ev-narottama-delivers-brahmanas): there too Jagannatha Acharya is converted at the Goddess's word.
[^10-7]: "Bhava-kali" — here a mocking name for Narottama's singing and ecstasies.
[^10-8]: That is, to chant a hundred thousand holy names daily (laksha-nama).
[^10-9]: A brahma-daitya is the ghost of a dead brahmana (a kind of evil spirit).
[^10-10]: That is, the Muslim ruler (nawab) seized Chand Raya. Cf. *Prema-vilasa* 18–19 (ev-chand-raya-deliverance, ev-chand-raya-elephant).

---

# Vilasa 11. Virachandra in Kheturi; the Departure of Srinivasa, Ramachandra and Narottama

*Virachandra visits Srinivasa at Yajigrama and goes with him to Kheturi; kirtana with Virachandra's dancing; Srinivasa and Ramachandra go to Vrindavana and pass from sight; Narottama's lament; his last days and his departure in the Ganga at Gambhila*

**1.** Glory to Gaura, Nityananda and Advaita with their associates!
Be merciful, Prabhu, to this wretched beggar.

**2.** Glory, glory to the listeners, oceans of compassion!
Hear what I shall now relate.

**3.** Kaviraja Thakura and Thakura Mahashaya
wrote two letters with all the news

**4.** and, together with a list of the songs composed by Sri Govinda,
sent them with great care to Vrindavana.

**5.** Hearing of the welfare there, they rejoiced
and sent all this news to Yajigrama.

**6.** In Yajigrama the Acharya with his people
rejoiced unceasingly in discussing the scriptures of bhakti.

**7.** How Sri Narottama gives bhakti to the poor and wretched,
how in his kindness he delivers robbers and pashandis —

**8.** hearing all this, what bliss swelled
in the Acharya's heart — who could tell?

**9.** As soon as he resolved to go quickly to Kheturi,
various auspicious omens appeared at once.

**10.** Someone came and said, "Virabhadra has come here."
The Acharya rejoiced to hear of his coming.

**11.** Going out, they saw that he was already near the village;
seeing him, all were overjoyed.

**12.** Prabhu Virabhadra, seeing Acharya Thakura,
quickly stepped down from his palanquin.

**13.** The Acharya with his people bowed to the ground,
and Prabhu Virabhadra embraced him with great affection.

**14.** Asking after his welfare with great joy in his heart,
he took the Acharya by the hand and walked slowly on.

**15.** The Acharya asked with great respect,
"From where have you suddenly come?"

**16.** Prabhu said, "In Khardaha I decided
to go to the village of Kheturi by way of Yajigrama.

**17.** With my people I went round Nadia and other places
and came here quickly by way of Srikhanda."

**18.** So saying, he entered the house, and at his place
Prabhu Virabhadra sat on his seat of honour.

**19.** There was great bliss at Prabhu's coming —
as if the master of the house had come home.

**20.** Draupadi Ishvari and Sri Gauranga-priya,
both wives of the Acharya, came and bowed down.

**21.** Bringing cool water, with jubilant hearts
they washed the feet of Prabhu Virabhadra.

**22.** The Acharya's eldest son, very wise,
to whom Sri Jiva Gosvami had given the name Vrindavana,

**23.** Radha-Krishna and Sri Gati-Govinda — these three
fell at the feet of Prabhu Virachandra.[^11-1]

**24.** Prabhu blessed the three boys
and placed his holy feet on their heads.

**25.** The Acharya's three daughters, devoted to bhakti and prema —
Hemalata, Krishnapriya and Sri Kanchanalata —

**26.** all three bowed at the feet of Prabhu Virachandra,
and Prabhu blessed them with fatherly affection.

**27.** The men and women of the village came for darshana
and all bowed at the feet of Virachandra.

**28.** Prabhu asked each one after his welfare,
and all, with soft words, surrendered themselves to him.

**29.** Having stayed there a while, Prabhu
went with his people in the highest bliss to bathe.

**30.** Meanwhile the Acharya's wife, bathing quickly,
began to cook — how, I cannot say.

**31.** She prepared greens and other dishes, boiled and fried,
sweet rice, shikharini and much else,

**32.** carefully cooked fragrant rice
and, pouring fresh ghee over it, set it on a plate.

**33.** A very wise disciple of the Acharya
offered the meal to Shalagrama-chandra.

**34.** The Govardhana-shila given by Prabhu Nityananda,
whom Prabhu Virabhadra serves, was also with him:

**35.** to Him too the meal was joyfully offered,
and the Two partook together with great love.

**36.** The two Thakuranis set out the offering —
what wondrous beauty it was, I cannot say.

**37.** The Govardhana-shila and Sri Vamshivadana[^11-2]
partook, and the pujari gave Them water to rinse Their mouths,

**38.** carefully offered betel
and laid Them on a wonderful bed.

**39.** Meanwhile all, having bathed and so on, went with Prabhu
and sat down to eat in a lovely courtyard.

**40.** Prabhu Virabhadra said to Sri Acharya,
"Sit down to eat and take everyone with you."

**41.** Acharya Thakura said, "That frightens me:
it is fitting that I eat last."

**42.** Hearing this, Prabhu took the Acharya's hand and laughed,
saying, "This is what is fitting: sit beside me."

**43.** Acharya Thakura could not transgress the order
and sat with everyone at Prabhu's command.

**44.** The very wise companions of Prabhu Virabhadra —
all their minds rejoiced.

**45.** How wonderfully the circle of Vaishnavas shone!
Who, seeing Prabhu Virabhadra, could remain composed?

**46.** All took lovely banana leaves,
and Prabhu ordered the food to be served.

**47.** The Acharya's two wives, embodiments of bhakti, devoted to their husband,
served with great bliss.

**48.** Sridasa, Gokulananda and Vyasa — these three
set out the various dishes on lovely plates.

**49.** Placing in rows sweet water, fried delicacies and the rest,
they sat down to honour Sri prasada.

**50.** Virabhadra, first tasting a little of this,
said with a smile, "Today it is just like Vraja!"

**51.** Then he partook of the sweetest boiled and fried dishes,
tasting the greens and the rest, in great bliss.

**52.** With great merriment all ate,
rinsed their mouths and chewed betel.

**53.** After resting a little, in rapturous bliss
they passed the day and night relishing talks of Krishna.

**54.** In the morning Sri Ramachandra and the Acharya
set out with great jubilation in their hearts.

**55.** All the dear companions of Prabhu Virabhadra
set out with joy in their hearts.

**56.** The Acharya's disciples, with blissful hearts,
went some alongside, some ahead.

**57.** By way of Kantaka-nagara they came to Budhari;
Govinda and others, hearing beforehand, came out to meet them.

**58.** On the road there was a wonderful meeting with all,
and Govinda joyfully brought them to his house.

**59.** Prabhu Virachandra, with great joy in his heart,
stayed with his people in a lovely lodging.

**60.** Acharya Thakura with his people was there too;
there was no limit to everyone's delight in one another.

**61.** What festivity of feasting and so on there was —
I cannot describe it for fear of prolixity.

**62.** For two days they stayed in the village of Budhari,
and a great many Vaishnavas came there to meet them.

**63.** Crossing the Padma with everyone and bathing,
Prabhu went on to Kheturi with a joyful heart.

**64.** Hearing beforehand of his coming, Mahashaya
had various provisions prepared:

**65.** curd, milk, chhana, mangoes and other fruits,
mango and other pickles — all was made ready.

**66.** Having the lodgings cleaned, Mahashaya
went out with his people and watched the road from afar.

**67.** As if moons had risen to dispel heat and darkness —
so, seeing them from afar, he refreshed his eyes.

**68.** Approaching with great joy in his heart,
he bowed at the feet of Prabhu Virabhadra.

**69.** Prabhu Virachandra, embracing Narottama,
lost his composure and could not restrain his heart.

**70.** Narottama, bathed in the tears from his eyes,
fell again and again at his feet, rolling on the ground.

**71.** What the meeting of all with one another was like —
not even a small part could be told with one mouth.

**72.** Acharya Thakura and Sri Thakura Mahashaya
brought Prabhu into the house of Gauranga.

**73.** Gauranga, Vallavikanta, Sri Vrajamohana,
Radha-Krishna, Radhakanta, Sri Radharamana —

**74.** Virachandra, seeing them all,
lost his composure, and tears flowed from his eyes.

**75.** He bowed again and again, falling to the ground;
what was rising in his heart — who knows?

**76.** After a while Prabhu collected himself,
and the pujari carefully gave him the prasada garland.

**77.** Acharya Thakura and Mahashaya with care
led him to the lodging where Ishvari had stayed before.

**78.** Meanwhile all the Vaishnavas, overwhelmed by the darshana,
checked their tears and grew calm.

**79.** The pujari gave everyone prasada garlands,
and with jubilant hearts they went to Prabhu.

**80.** People from Sri Kheturi and other villages
came running from all sides for darshana.

**81.** Having seen him, all went home
and said to one another in sweet words:

**82.** "The world-enchanting Nityananda-Balarama —
his son is Prabhu Virabhadra, abode of virtues."

**83.** Someone said, "Blessed is our life:
without effort we have obtained a rare darshana."

**84.** Someone said, "Thanks to Sri Thakura Mahashaya
our desires have been fulfilled in the village of Kheturi."

**85.** So the people said much in rapturous bliss;
the news of Virachandra's coming spread in all directions.

**86.** Meanwhile Prabhu Virachandra in his lovely lodging
sat down with everyone with a blissful heart.

**87.** Acharya Thakura, smiling gently,
spoke sweet words to Prabhu Virachandra:

**88.** "Today you are to eat fried delicacies here —
all was prepared beforehand as soon as your coming was heard."

**89.** Prabhu Virachandra took from his casket
the Govardhana-shila to offer Him food.

**90.** Various dishes were carefully brought to Him;
removing the offering, the shila was put back in the casket.

**91.** From the holy temple various prasada was brought;
all was ready, and he was respectfully informed.

**92.** Hearing the Acharya's words, the Gosani said,
"I am hungry; there is no need to delay."

**93.** So saying, he sat down with everyone in the courtyard;
the fortunate saw a wonderful beauty.

**94.** Harirama, Ramakrishna, Ganganarayana,
Sri Govinda Chakravarti and a few others,

**95.** carefully taking various fried delicacies,
served with the highest bliss in their hearts.

**96.** Mangoes, jackfruit, pomegranates and various fruits,
curd, milk, chhana, sugar, drinks — all

**97.** they served in order and merrily watched the beauty;
Prabhu with the Acharya and all the others ate joyfully.

**98.** Puris, pupas, laddus and other most charming things —
relishing taste after taste, the meal was abundant.

**99.** Rinsing his mouth, Prabhu sat on his seat
and joyfully chewed prasada betel.

**100.** Afterwards countless people ate;
I cannot describe all this in detail.

**101.** The Acharya and Thakura Mahashaya with their companions
were immersed in bliss beside Prabhu Virachandra.

**102.** Drinking the nectar of the deeds of Radha-Krishna and Chaitanya,
in what happiness they passed the day and night without sleep!

**103.** In the morning all performed their morning duties and bathed,
and Sri Santosha came to Prabhu Virachandra.

**104.** He dressed him in new garments of the finest cloth —
the sight of Prabhu's beauty refreshed the eyes.

**105.** Humbly entreating the Vaishnavas who were with him,
he dressed them too in new garments, his heart rejoicing.

**106.** Before Prabhu he arranged a wonderful seat;
sitting on it, Prabhu performed the service of the Govardhana-shila.

**107.** Adorning Him with flowers, tulasi and sandal paste,
he at once offered Him various foods.

**108.** Removing the offering, he bowed down many times
and distributed the prasada to everyone.

**109.** Prabhu Virachandra's cooks
very quickly prepared a wonderful meal.

**110.** It was offered to the Govardhana-shila;
removing the offering, the Shila was placed in a golden casket.

**111.** Having seen the arati of Sri Gaurachandra,
Prabhu joyfully took prasada with everyone.

**112.** Chewing betel, he rested,
and after a while sat down with everyone.

**113.** Prabhu Virachandra said to the Acharya,
"I wish to hear sankirtana."

**114.** The Acharya replied, "You fulfil all desires;
I know that now our desire too will be fulfilled."
